AFTER, dpr 2, back-lane shot: whole p50 17.3 / p95 23.5 / max 31.7 in the dive, hold p50 17.6 / p95 24.2, no frame over 33.4 ms in the shot; dpr 1: whole p50 16.7 / p95 18.6 / max 27.5, hold max 19.3. Sector 1's own opening frame: dpr 1 100.7 -> 42 ms (then, with first-use warming and the dot scan) dpr 2 148 -> 42 ms, no long task in the shot; the dive's pass over the base links nothing. td-tab unchanged at 13,505 lines. Not changed: the shot's poses, timing, the sinkhole's opening, the bloom chain.

Alternatives: Rendering the dust at half resolution, fewer octaves, fewer puffs, or dropping it from the bloom pass: not done; the diff showed the dust never reached the screen, so hiding it while no depth is bound is pixel-identical, and a real soft-particle path (a depth prepass into frame.uSceneDepth) is the owner's call, with the fill cost in hand: ~45 ms a frame at dpr 2 for the settling dust from 5 cells up.; Freezing the landmark tiers through a shot: not needed once the near tier's programs are warmed before it can be swapped in; the swap distance and hysteresis are untouched.; Warming the sinkhole's programs at breach creation instead of boot: rejected; create() and the shot's first frame are the same frame, so the stall would only move onto the cut.; Staging rebuildAfterBreach and recomputePortalDist across frames: not done; the two BFS passes are ~9 ms of the opening frame on the 71k-cell planet and the frame is now under the 50 ms target without them.; compileAsync alone: tried first; the links moved off the opening frame but the frame still stalled 49 ms in getProgramInfoLog on first use, hence the warmer pulling uniforms and attributes itself.

Evidence:

- artifacts/qa/dive-probe.mjs [label] [--dpr=2] [--profile-sector] [--no-skips] [--diff-only] through scripts/browser-lock.sh; outputs under artifacts/qa/dive-<label>-dpr<n>/ (per-shot json with every frame, cpuprofile, screenshots); artifacts/qa/prof-window.mjs and slow-frames.mjs read them back.
- BEFORE dpr 2 back-lane shot: whole p50 19.4 p95 79.9 max 173.8 over 473 frames; hold p50 57.3 p95 82.3 (165 of 209 over 33 ms); skip schedule in the hold: nothing 79.9 / smoke skipped 17.0 / motes 76.9 / grit 79.9 / post-lum 68.4 / decal 59.2 / fissures 51.4 / stone 52.9 / crater 51.5 / nothing 38 (the dust thinning over the hold).
- BEFORE dpr 1 sector shot: opening frame 100.7 ms (5 links, getProgramInfoLog 49 ms, texSubImage2D 39 ms, bfsDist 13 ms); hold p50 29 p95 41.4 max 81.7. BEFORE dpr 2 sector shot: opening 148 ms, hold p50 60.7 p95 84.3 max 155.7.
- Visibility diff at 1440x987, hold, dpr 1: smoke skipped vs drawn mean 0.150 / motion floor 0.173 (max 218 both); motes 0.063 / 0.105; grit 0.064 / 0.106; fissures skipped 14.6 (visible, as expected).
- AFTER dpr 2 back-lane shot (126a6908): whole p50 17.3 p95 23.5; dive max 31.7; hold p50 17.6 p95 24.2; the one 71.8 ms frame in the hold was the probe's own getImageData. AFTER dpr 1 (126a6908): whole p50 16.7 p95 18.6 max 27.5; hold max 19.3; sector opening 42.1 ms with 2 links left (the wall debris material).
- AFTER dpr 2 sector shot (7ede3ae2, a desktop Chrome sharing the GPU during the run): opening frame 41.7 ms, no links, no long task; back-lane shot whole p50 18.3 p95 24.3 max 33.2, no frame over 33.4 ms.
- npm test: 120 test programs passed (test/game-breaches.mjs extended: the template warms once, uploads its maps one per update once loaded, is freed on dispose). npm run check: architecture, line budgets, pinned sinkhole imports hold; src/td-tab.js at 13,505.
- Browser suites after 7ede3ae2 through the lock: --sinkhole exit 0 (10 PASS); --breach-game exit 0 (8 PASS); --backdoor exit 0 (4 PASS; collapse window: no long task, max frame 24 ms, call 7.7 ms, 6 cells; baseline max frame 22.8); --story-world exit 0 (31 PASS).

## 2026-09-18 — The back gate is built: plan.gate becomes a list, the swarm's surprise mouth gets a door once the player has held it, and sentries can be ordered behind the bays

change · accepted · 2026-09-18-back-gate-built

Owner, 2026-09-18: "Back gate is a reminder that focusing on defense on one side leaves one open; surprise it opens! Tank to the rescue + orbital + gunship. Then Isao installs a gate and we can put sentries." The last and largest of the 2026-09-16 known gaps (`2026-09-18-robustness-pass` left it deliberately unstarted): the back door stayed open once sector 2 cracked it, because the base could only ever have one gate. The single gate reached six places — the story base's gate mixer and open/close rule, base-print's gate plot, the pathfinder's gate rule in src/td-tab.js under its 13,505-line budget, the sector loop's gate integrity and pressure, Isao's repair orders and the HUD's GATE line.

src/domain/base-plan.js returns `gates`, entry 0 the front gate; `plan.gate` IS that same object, so every single-gate consumer reads what it read before and a plan built without a back mouth is otherwise byte-identical (test/base-plan.mjs deep-compares the two plans). A caller that passes `backMouth` (src/domain/back-door.js findBackMouth, which src/platform/story-world.js already computed) gets a second gate, always `pending`: it is never pre-built at any stage. Its cells are the WHOLE collapse, the mouth plus the flank rock that came down with it, and `plan.backSockets` are mounts on rock beside the back lane, found by walking the lane outward rather than taking the rock beside the mouth (that rock IS the flank and is gone once the mouth is down). src/fx/story-base.js keeps one record per gate — model, hydraulics clip, open radius, built flag — with base.gate()/growGate(k) unchanged for the front door and base.gateList()/gateSealed(id)/growGate(k, id) for the rest. story.sealed(ci) is a map lookup over the plan's gate cells instead of a comparison against one cell. src/fx/sector-run.js keeps one src/domain/gate-integrity.js per door, filling the map in as doors stand, presses each with the bodies nearest its own cells, and prints them side by side: GATE 100% · BACK 100%. src/domain/repair-orders.js takes a `gates` list and sends Isao to the worse door first (broken before chewed, lower share before higher, ties keeping the front door); a lone `gate` still means exactly what it meant. src/content/base-programme.js gains a tenth step, `backgate`: gate: 'back', no walls, its own plot, when { sector: 2, idle: true, back: 'held' }; src/domain/build-programme.js due() takes `back` and the host reads it off the sector loop's new backOpenBreaches(). Because the door is the one step a jumped-to stage cannot already have standing, story-world's standing() resolves a step's door by id (so it stays pending everywhere) and Isao's build loop lets that one step through on a base that otherwise prints nothing — which is what makes it reachable from ?skip=defence. Two lines from Isao (src/isaobriefs.js build_back_gate). src/td-tab.js unchanged at 13,505 lines: every edit was inside existing one-liners.

Alternatives: Keeping plan.gate a single object and adding a parallel plan.backGate. Rejected: the sector loop, the repair rule and the HUD would each have grown a second code path for the same thing, and the owner's ask is that the second gate behaves exactly like the first.; Putting the back sockets straight into plan.sockets. Rejected: they would have been mountable from the first frame of every page, before the mouth is even cracked. They ride on plan.backSockets and join socketToward when the door is printed.; A door across the back mouth's two cells only. MEASURED AND REJECTED: the browser step caught thirteen bodies inside in forty seconds with the door standing — the collapse opens six cells and the swarm walked around the door through the four flank cells. The gate's cells are the whole collapse now.; Sockets on the rock immediately beside the mouth. Rejected for the same reason from the other side: that rock is the mouth's flank and comes down with it, so a mount there would stand on nothing. The lane walk finds rock further out (45954, 45953 on the shipped planet).; Testing the back gate under the sector's own waves. Rejected: sector 2 opens its own back-side breach, so backInside() counts bodies that never touched this door. The step keeps the sector quiet and puts one real back breach on the board.

Evidence:

- npm test (120 test programs) and npm run check green on every one of the eight commits; the architecture guard holds src/td-tab.js at 13,505 lines throughout (net zero in all four commits that touch it).
- test/base-plan.mjs: with no back mouth, gates.length 1, gates[0] === plan.gate, backSockets []; with one, the rest of the plan deep-equals the one-gate plan, the back gate is pending, on +Z against the front gate's -Z, carries the mouth plus the flank, and its sockets stand on rock aimed at the back lane and are in neither plan.sockets nor the gate's own cells.
- test/repair-orders.mjs: two whole doors ask for no trip; the chewed back door is picked; the worse of two is picked and the front door carries no id; a broken door outranks a chewed one; doors come before walls; a tie keeps the front door first; repairsPending lists worst door first. One gate still reads exactly as it did.
- test/build-programme.mjs: two gate steps, the front one with walls and the back one a wall-less door with a plot and when.back 'held', last on the programme; due() holds a when.back step while the back is untouched and while it is merely open, and releases it at 'held'.
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --back-gate, all steps PASS: on ?skip=defence the programme's only unprinted step is backgate with the front door standing and the back one not; the cracked mouth with no door on it is a way in; once the sector is 2 and the back is held Isao queues, flies and prints it; printed ['backgate'], perk on, gates [{gate,built},{back,built}], sector gates [{gate,60/60},{back,60/60}]; sealedAt() true on all six collapse cells; the back mounts join the sockets and a sentry is ordered on 45954; ninety seconds of a live 16-body back breach and zero of them inside on the back half, with the front door's hp untouched; breakGate('back') and they come through.
- Screenshots looked at: artifacts/browser/back-gate-standing.png (the kit door standing on the back mouth, HUD SECTOR 2 · THE BACK DOOR · BREACHES 2 · WAVE 0/4 · GATE 100% · BACK 100%), back-gate-under-pressure.png (the swarm massed outside the closed door while the sentry on its mount fires on them), back-gate-printing.png, back-gate-socket.png, back-gate-down.png.
- Regressions, all green after the change: --sectors (sector 2 secure, breaches A/gate/held 4of4 B/back/held 4of4), --backdoor (stage6 wallCount 43,015; collapse 6 cells, call 8.1 ms, no long tasks, max frame 22.9 ms against an 18.5 ms baseline; swarm inside), --grow (every step in order, Isao's gate repair 21.9 -> 60 hp), --skip-tutorial (secure, debrief, next sector), --defense (all steps).
- NOT VERIFIED: the door opening for the tank is covered only by the shared code path — the same record, the same radius test in storyBase.tick — and by breakGate('back') proving that an open back door is a way through. No run drove a hull up to it. And the back door's wear under a real pile was not measured in the browser: sector-run gates pressure behind `quiet`, and the step keeps the sector quiet so the only bodies on the board are its own. The pressure rule itself is the front door's, unchanged, and is covered by test/gate-integrity.mjs and --sectors.
- Reproduce through the lock only: /Users/minikai/Dev/stalheart/scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --back-gate.

Supersedes: 2026-09-18-back-gate-after-the-surprise

## 2026-09-18 — The back door is a surprise you answer with the tank, SOL-82 and the gunship; then Isao installs a gate and sentries can stand there

decision · accepted · 2026-09-18-back-gate-after-the-surprise

The robustness pass (2026-09-18-robustness-pass) left the second gate undone because six systems assume one gate. Owner, 2026-09-18: 'Back gate is a reminder that focusing on defense on one side leaves one open; surprise it opens! Tank to the rescue + orbital + gunship. Then Isao installs a gate and we can put sentries.' Also: the foundry beat is trimmed from 8 s to 4 s because everything must feel faster.

Accepted: the back gate is NOT pre-built. Sector 2 opens the back door as today (the surprise), the player holds it with the tank, SOL-82 and the gunship, and once the back breach is closed or spent Isao's build programme queues a back gate on the back mouth's cells plus sockets on the rock beside the back lane, so sentries can be ordered there from then on. The second gate behaves like the first: closed = wall to the swarm, opens for the tank, wears down under pressure, repaired by Isao. plan.gate becomes a list; story-base, base-print, the pathfinder, gate integrity, repair orders and the HUD all take the list.

Alternatives: Print the back gate before the surprise; rejected: it removes the lesson the owner wants ('one side open').

Evidence:

- Owner note 2026-09-18.
- Single-gate assumptions listed in docs/log/entries/2026-09-18-robustness-pass.json.

## 2026-09-16 — The Quiver's rocket stays in frame from launch to ignite, the story sheds campaign furniture, and the tank's keys are taught

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-quiver-round-in-frame-and-a-readable-first-run

Owner note: when the Quiver shoots its rocket, the rocket should never get out of frame during the initial pop-out, hover, drop and ignite. QA measured 3 of 192 samples in frame: the heavy flight's pop-out scaled with range (25 m for a 235 m shot) and the optic snapped to an aim-only pose through the 3x lens. QA also found the story crowded with campaign buttons, a stale 'B build' hint, the dev frame readout on by default, an ORBITAL ASSET ARMED toast with a colour glyph and no strike controls, a live C cheat, the TALON feed popping up while driving, and no word about the tank's keys.

src/domain/missile-flight.js caps the opening's rise at OPENING_METRES = 8 (frame option `metre`; the game passes cellSide/10, flat ranges default to 1 so every lab range keeps the authored scale) and eases back to the range scale by u = 0.58. src/core/round-framing.js: while the piloted mount's round is at fraction <= 0.42 the lens eases toward 1x and the view follows the round in a dead zone (top edge 0.62, in the upper third), then slerps back to the aim by 0.62; the aim and lock never move, and the reticle and cross fade while framing. td-tab passes host.round and host.lens to the pilot. Story clutter: #tab-td.story-world hides the campaign chips except sound, and the key hint; the frame readout is off unless turned on (backtick, DEV) or acceptance=1; the orbital toast is campaign-only and glyph-free; C needs DEV or acceptance; the seeker feed shows only the piloted mount's round. src/fx/controls-card.js: a monochrome card of the keys the handler honours, shown once past the handover (td.controls-card-seen), H or ? again, pad labels on touch, a one-time empty-rack toast. td-tab net -1 line. Not done here: the GUNSHIP countdown before the handover (base-growth agent) and the seat hitch.

Alternatives: A fixed pop-out height for every shot; rejected for a cap, because short lab and campaign darts would pop far higher than their range and lab parity would change.; Widening the lens alone; rejected, QA showed the round 70-80 degrees above the aim line.; Framing the whole flight; rejected, the climb goes far above the lane and the pilot needs the aim back for the next lock; the seeker feed carries the climb.

Evidence:

- node test/missile-flight.mjs: a 235 m shot pops at most 8 m for every profile, a 20 m shot keeps the authored lift exactly, no jump through the hand-back, the metre option scales the cap. node test/round-framing.mjs: every sample of a heavy opening in frame at 70/235/400 m and 20/60 degree lenses.
- npm test (112 programs) and npm run check green.
- browser-lock --quiver-frame (new): PoV 147 of 147 samples in frame from launch to ignite (fov 23 -> 60), third person 152 of 152; during the climb hand-back 29/72 and 36/72. Strips artifacts/browser/quiver-frame-{pov,third}-{0..5}-*.png.
- browser-lock --defense (with the controls-card and H assertions), --gunship and --story-world pass. The default suite fails twice at the Units lab step '#units-next leaves no missiles' (scripts/browser-test.mjs:974); see the report for the baseline check.

## 2026-09-16 — Isao stops printing rock where the kit walls belong: a grown base read its unprinted rim as twelve breaches and repaired them before the gate

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-isao-prints-walls-not-rock

Owner, playtesting the published V1 on the bare index.html: 'When Isao starts to build at the beginning of story mode, he builds ROCKS instead of WALLS, in the phase before building the gate.' The base now grows in play (2026-09-16-isao-grows-the-base): the programme's first step is the gate and its twelve walls, and src/fx/story-base.js holds the pending kit WALL instances at zero scale until Isao prints them.

The cause was the repair rule's caller, not the print and not the lattice. story.wallCells (src/platform/story-world.js:118) is the PENDING wall cells, which are open FLOOR until the gate step stands; storyApi.build() in src/td-tab.js fed them straight to nextRepair as 'cells that should be rock and are not', so at the landing a grown base reported eleven holes and Isao flew out to 'repair' a rim that had never been built. Each repair ran storyApi.repaired(): it tagged the cell BLOCKED in dungeon.tags and tdFullTags and called rebuildAfterBreach(), so board-surface.refreshCells() drew a ROCK top there, and storyBase.restoreWall() snapped a single kit WALL segment to full scale. That is the rock the owner watched rise before the gate. Fix: in storyApi.build() the repair scan (gate and walls alike) runs only once the gate step stands - programmeHas(pg, 'gate'), the perk the gate-and-walls step carries, which a static stage-4 base already holds from its first frame through makeBuildProgramme's standing() - and state().programme.broke reports 0 until then. Isao now prints the gate and the kit walls as their models and storyApi.printed() alone turns the rim to rock, exactly as the static base has it at load. src/td-tab.js stays at 13506 lines (net 0; both edits are inside existing one-liners), so the budget in docs/architecture-budget.json holds.

Alternatives: Stop the lattice drawing ROCK at printed wall cells (the reported hypothesis). REJECTED by evidence: the static stage-4 base does exactly the same tagging - src/platform/story-world.js:83 blocks every non-pending wall cell at load - and both plans produce the identical wall cells, so stage 4 stands with those six lattice cells as rock under its kit walls and the owner has never complained about that look. Suppressing the rock would have made the grown base diverge from the static one and would have left the tank and the swarm walking through the rim.; Widen story.wallCells to every wall cell rather than only the pending ones, so a static stage-4 rim can also be repaired. Left alone: it is a separate gap in the repair feature, not this bug, and it would change stage-4 behaviour during a playtest.; Gate the repair scan on a wave/phase test instead of the programme perk. Rejected: the perk is the same signal the static path uses for a step that already stands, so one rule covers both bases.

Evidence:

- Before, src/td-tab.js at 0b1aa589, grown page index.html?sw=0&acceptance=1&cine=0&world=story&threat=0.35&heart=none&stage=1&grow=1#td: at the landing state().programme reported broke=11 and repairing={kind:'wall',ci:30555} with printed=[] - repairs on a rim that had never been printed.
- Before: state().wallCount rose 43009 -> 43015 BEFORE the gate step became active at 70.4 s, and the gate print (k 0.046 -> 1) then added nothing. 43015 is exactly the static stage-4 wall count, so the phantom repairs had already laid the whole rim as rock. artifacts/qa/isao-walls/timeline.json.
- The static path tags the same cells: node scripts/architecture.mjs aside, test/world-recipe.mjs asserts 'Story world composition blocks wall cells and keeps the gate open', and a Node comparison of planBase(planet, layout, 4) against planBase(planet, layout, 1, {reach: 8}) showed identical wall cells (12 segments on 6 distinct lattice cells), all open floor inside the clearing before either path blocks them.
- After, same script and page: at the landing state().programme reported broke=0 and repairing=null; state().wallCount held 43009 through the whole gate print (k 0.082 -> 0.982) and rose to 43015 only as printed('gate') fired at k=1 - the rim turns to rock when the walls stand, not before. The gate step also began at 39.2 s instead of 70.4 s, the six phantom repair trips gone, and the run logged no console errors. artifacts/qa/isao-walls-after/timeline.json.
- Screenshots from the same camera (state().focusHeart) at the moment the gate-and-walls step finishes: artifacts/qa/isao-walls/B5-after-gate-focus.jpg (before) and artifacts/qa/isao-walls-after/B5-after-gate-focus.jpg (after).
- npm test: 120 test programs passed. npm run check: syntax, source module identity, pinned kernel, logs and assets checked. npm run architecture: game/lab controller boundaries, line budgets and top-level placement hold (src/td-tab.js 13506 lines, unchanged).
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --grow after the fix: exit 0, every step PASS (grow-bare, grow-landing, grow-gate-printing, grow-tremor, grow-fodder, grow-settled, grow-study, grow-solar-rising, grow-bays, grow-hugin, grow-finished). The block after 'gate stands' asserts walls0 < wallCount <= walls0 + 12, the rise the phantom repairs used to defeat by laying the whole rim as rock before the step began.
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --story-world after the fix: exit 0, 31 steps PASS, story-world-rotor-kill included.

## 2026-09-16 — SOL-82 in the arsenal: the pass clock after the handover, the seat on the views strip, the beam through the game's own kill, seal and breach paths

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-sol82-in-the-arsenal

Owner: 'we officially add the Orbital Laser to our arsenal of tools to defend the base.' V1 session design section 3 (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-15-v1-session-design.md): online from sector 2, passes on the lab's clock, the strip counts down and lights overhead, the seat is the satellite scope with the ground view (the lab's layout), bodies die on contact, a breach seals after its burn time, and it burns our own walls, towers and the Stalheart too.

src/fx/laser-arsenal.js runs the domain clock and beam in the game on the unit sphere (R = 10 / cellSide metres): bodies (soft/hard by hp), breaches (killPortal(sp, 'laser'), which now accepts the laser reason), kit walls (storyBase.walls()/dropWall() plus breachWallCell), rock cells, towers (destroyTower), the Stalheart (heartHit) and the tank (playerHit) per LASER_BURN, with per-kind reach metres in LASER_GAME.reach; kills go through damageEnemy with source 'laser'. src/fx/laser-seat.js keeps the owner-approved lab layout: ground view large (groundBack 120 m, groundUp 60 m, 52 degree lens), framed so the contact sits in the open part of the view, and the round scope bottom left, kept clear of the views strip. Mouse hover/drag in the scope aims, mouse button, Space or HOLD burns, WASD glides while off and drags with the beam's inertia while burning, Esc or TANK leaves and restores fov 68. src/fx/laser-station.js composes both for td-tab and catches the strip's SOL-82 button (data-view=laser; first seat behind SOL-82's briefing, paused). The strip shows SOL-82 mm:ss, SOL-82 OVERHEAD lit, amber under a quarter of energy (domain laserStrip). Online with ?laser=online or LASER_GAME.online; storyApi.setLaserOnline(bool) for the sectors. Isao's laser_pass brief on arrival. The game scope calls range at LASER_GAME.range 640 m instead of the lab's 320 m because the game's breaches open about 520 m out. The lab's scope and inset HUD moved to src/fx (laser-scope.js, laser-inset-hud.js) and the lab uses them. td-tab net -10 lines: the briefing cards moved to src/fx/briefing-cards.js.

Alternatives: Invert the layout (scope full screen, ground as inset); rejected: the owner approved ground-large in the lab, and the column, smoke and scorch read best large.; Limit v1 to kit walls and bodies for fear of the rock-breach hitch; not needed: the game's board surface patches cells in place and a rock break measured 0.5-0.7 ms.; Keep the lab's 320 m range call in the game; rejected: every game breach would read OUT OF RANGE while the beam seals it.; A data-mount strip button like the gunship; rejected: --defense counts data-mount buttons as tower mounts.

Evidence:

- npm test (107 programs, test/orbital-laser.mjs pins LASER_GAME, inFootprint and laserStrip) and npm run check green on e6c46fb3.
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --laser-game: PASS laser-game-load, -seat, -burn, -sealed, -tank, -friendly; 19 bodies, 1 breach sealed, 1 rock (slowest break 0.7 ms), 2 of our wall segments with OURS UNDER THE BEAM shown, TANK and Esc restore fov 68, no browser errors.
- --defense passes on the branch (strip change does not disturb its mount count).
- --laser (the lab) fails at 'the beam cut the wall (0)' after burning only 1 queue body; the lab's feel commits (inertia, 500 fleeing bodies) were never browser-verified, so this is probably pre-existing, but it was not confirmed against the base commit.
- Not verified: SITREP hide (CSS only), portrait layout, touch HOLD, tower and Stalheart friendly fire at runtime.

## 2026-09-16 — The solar array recharges the tank's shield from a finite reserve, and every ram floats its premium over the hull

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-shield-array-station-and-ram-readout

Owner, V1 brief: make sure the tank's shield works and unlock the fun ramming bonuses; the shield should be rechargeable at the array station but not infinite (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-15-v1-session-design.md, section 3). A browser probe on the story route (stage 6, phase expedition) found T and the pad button deploy, the dome shows and drains in 10 s, the seam and rack pips read on the panel, and 34 rams in a row built RAM xN with biomass rising. What was missing: the rack refilled only on the retired campaign debrief, so in the story two charges were gone for good and an empty rack said 'buy a case on the debrief'; the ram premium had no readout of its own.

arrayDraw in src/domain/shield.js: parked within radiusMetres of the solar island and slower than maxSpeed, the hull takes shield at `rate` s/s, first into a bubble that is up (to the cap), then into the rack a charge at a time (rackFill, never past rackCap), out of a finite station reserve that only refillArray restores; one event per step (start, charge, dry, stop) cues sound and Isao. SHIELD_ARRAY in src/content/shield-array.js: reserve 30 s, rate 2 s/s, radius 12 m, maxSpeed 3 m/s. SHIELD_TUNE unchanged. The story world exposes story.arrayPad {cell, pos, standing} (standing from stage 3; a build programme can stand it later). td-tab: refillArrays() (called at the handover for now; the sector start should call it), a two-ring dotted pad on the island that breathes idle, pulses while feeding and dims when dry (src/fx/shield-array.js, which also now builds the campaign's heart ring and the panel's shield line), ARRAY 30s / ARRAY ▲ 24s · NEXT 40% / ARRAY DRY beside the rack, tank_spool_up / tank_pickup / tank_spool_down cues, Isao's array_charging (once) and array_dry lines, and the empty-rack refusal points at the array. Rams: a bounded pool of six '+N kg ×M' labels rises over the hull (src/fx/ram-readout.js); the x10 tier callouts were already wired and are now asserted. td-tab shrank by 39 lines.

Alternatives: Refill only the rack and never the active bubble; not chosen because the brief asked for the active meter first, and a bubble topped up on the pad costs reserve like anything else.; Refill the reserve per wave like the old heart pad's stationBudget; rejected because the brief makes the sector the unit, so the pad is a resource to plan across two breaches.; Float the ram premium as a 3D sprite; rejected for a DOM pool in the shout type, which matches the combo counter and costs no draw calls.

Evidence:

- node test/shield.mjs: finite reserve runs to zero once and says dry once, refill only through refillArray, nothing while moving fast or off the pad, active bubble first, rack cap stops the draw, the seam still refuses a banked charge.
- npm test (107 programs) and npm run check green.
- Commits 0024896 (rule, content, tests), f343da8 (wiring, readouts, browser step), 0d2d3a3 (step fix) and a legibility commit placing the ram readout beside the hull.
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --shield-story passes end to end on the story route (stage 6, phase expedition): T raises the dome from a rack of two, it drains in 10 s, the seam refuses, the pad button spends the second charge, an empty rack refuses and points at the array; parked on the pad it charges with the ring glowing, the reserve runs 30 -> 0 for exactly three charges and stops, stays dry, the ring dims; refillArrays restores 30 and the pad tops the rack to 4 and stops at the cap; a shielded hull shoves a barbed core without losing a hull; rams reach a combo of ten, the RAM ×10 callout lands, biomass rises and every ram floats '+N kg ×M'. Screenshots artifacts/browser/shield-array-{dome,empty,charging,dry,ram,end}.png.
- Observed and not fixed: with the hull parked on the pad, a live bubble never drops while the reserve lasts (the active meter is fed first, as the brief asked); the reserve is the limit. The combo is not asserted across a shove because the wave's own fodder is rammed on the way.
- Legibility iteration: the first ram capture showed '+N kg ×M' rising through the centred RAM ×N counter and the streak callouts; the labels now float to the hull's right, step out along a chain and are clamped on screen. The re-run of --shield-story passed with the new placement (artifacts/browser/shield-array-ram.png); the final on-screen clamp was checked by npm test and npm run check only.

## 2026-09-16 — The laser lab's browser step aims through the heading-up lens and stands on a wall cell until it burns

change · resolved · 2026-09-16-laser-lab-step-retimed

node scripts/browser-test.mjs --laser failed after laser-lab-burn with 'the beam cut the wall (0)', also on a tree without the 2026-09-16 work. The suspected cause was the 2026-09-15 beam inertia (slew 10, accel 5) outpacing the step's timing.

b7677c2f. The inertia was not the cause: the step pins slew=40&accel=0. The aim loop was. It mapped inset up to +z and left to +x, which only holds for a north-up lens, but frameSat has set sat.up = viewForward (heading-up) since the 2026-09-15 view change, so each aim drove the contact away from its target. With the old mapping the contact ended 'at the gate' 217 m from the nearest wall cell, and walking toward that cell took it to 413 m and spent the pass's energy. The lab hook gains inset(point), which projects a pole-origin point through the satellite camera, and state().wallPoints, the standing wall cells. The step aims with the projection, walks onto the nearest wall cell and polls state() until the cut is counted, within LASER_BURN.wall + 2.5 s. The sinkhole walk uses the same aim. No gameplay numbers changed.

Alternatives: Re-time the sideways nudges for the new view; rejected because the nudges were open-loop in axes that no longer match the world.; Flip the fixed axis mapping; rejected because a heading-up lens rotates with the view, so no fixed mapping holds.

Evidence:

- Run 1 (closed-loop wall step, old aim mapping): nearest wall 217.2 m from the contact at the gate, 413.4 m after the walk, 0 s energy, 0 walls.
- Run 2 (projected aim): at the gate with 20 burned and 8.09 s of energy; wall cell 9.5 m off, reached to 2.4 m, 1 cut after 0.4 s held; PASS laser-lab-load, laser-lab-overhead, laser-lab-burn, laser-lab-sealed.
- npm test: 107 test programs passed; npm run check passed.

Supersedes: 2026-09-16-laser-lab-step-fails-before-sol82

## 2026-09-16 — The gunship creeps toward the busiest breach and circles it; the seat holds its world aim while the hull turns

decision · accepted · 2026-09-16-gunship-creeps-toward-the-breaches

Owner note, 2026-09-15: the gunship feels too static, it should move slowly towards the breaches. V1 session design section 3 (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-15-v1-session-design.md). Before this the platform drifted along a straight track latched once per pass toward gunshipLane(), from passProgress (2026-09-15-gunship-track-latched-and-seat-lens-reset).

src/domain/gunship-track.js (pure) keeps a ground point on the unit sphere and a heading. Each frame on station td-tab builds the target list from the live spawn points with the enemies within GUNSHIP_TRACK.nearCells (6) of each; pickTarget takes the most enemies (ties: nearest to the ship; none: over the base) and keeps its pick until another breach leads by switchMargin (3). stepTrack moves the point with inertia: velocity changes at most accelCells 0.2 cells/s² toward the velocity it wants, top speed speedCells 1.2 cells/s (12 m/s), braking on the stepped curve a*dt*(sqrt(1/4 + 2e/(a*dt^2)) - 1/2) onto a loiter circle of loiterCells 8 round the target, never through it, then circling it anticlockwise from above at loiterSpeedCells 0.6 (the left pylon turn). The heading follows the travel at yawRateDeg 8. Off station the track parks at rest where it is and the next pass eases out from there. ride() in src/fx/gunship-optic.js places the platform altitudeCells above the track's point with +Z along its heading; passProgress remains only for the host's progress/HUD. GUNSHIP_PLATFORM.trackShare and driftCells are retired. The seat (src/sentry-pilot.js holdAim) subtracts the platform's heading turn about its up (headingTurn) from its relative yaw every frame, so the view stays where the gunner left it. Crossing time from over the base to a breach 45 cells out: about 35 s (test/gunship-track.mjs asserts 30..60 s).

Alternatives: Ground-stabilised optic (keep the reticle on the same ground point as the ship moves, like a real sensor ball): not chosen; the seat opens looking almost straight down (pitch -1.45) and a stabilised aim that passes under the eye flips yaw by 180 degrees, rolling the image, which is the cut this work removes. With world-referenced yaw the ground slides slowly under a still reticle instead.; Point-mass aircraft that only flies along its heading: rejected because a capped yaw rate lets it overshoot the loiter circle; the velocity-with-inertia model guarantees no overshoot and lets the hull heading lag the travel.; Keep the latched straight track and only lengthen the drift: does not go to the breaches, and does not follow a second breach.

Evidence:

- test/gunship-track.mjs: toward the target, never through the loiter circle at dt 1/120..0.1, speed and acceleration caps, braking onto the circle, holds the radius and circles anticlockwise at the loiter speed, yaw rate cap, a mid-flight target switch with velocity change within accel*dt and no position or heading jump, home with no breach, pick ties and margin, loads, headingTurn.
- npm test (108 programs) and npm run check green; src/td-tab.js stays at 13858 lines (net 0).
- node scripts/browser-test.mjs --gunship (through scripts/browser-lock.sh) passes with a new step after gunship-skip-enemies: over a 9 s window with the gunner holding still the ground point closed from 41.99 to 30.07 cells from the live breach at 1.20 cells/s while the hull turned 9.07 degrees; the seat camera's largest frame-to-frame rotation was 0.017 degrees (limit 0.25) over 541 frames, 8.2 degrees in all, which is the planet's curvature under 11.9 cells of travel. Screenshots artifacts/browser/gunship-track-before.png and gunship-track-after.png: the ground slides under a still reticle, the impact readout moves from N 79.26 to N 70.20, no cut.
- node scripts/browser-test.mjs --defense (through the lock) passes: the called gunship pass, towers firing under the seat, the expedition part carried and dropped.

## 2026-09-16 — The gunship takes its track once per pass, and leaving a seat restores the tank's lens

decision · accepted · 2026-09-15-gunship-track-latched-and-seat-lens-reset

Owner playtest, 2026-09-15: in the gunship the camera still sometimes jumps to a totally new spot when launching weapon 3, the heavy; getting into the tank after the gunship puts it in third person at the wrong angle.

The platform's track pointed at gunshipLane(), which is the live breach while it stands. A 105 that killed the breach switched the lane to the ring or the lane end, and ride() rebuilt the platform's position and heading in one frame; the seat's yaw is relative to that frame, so the view cut. ride() now latches the lane end on the first tick of a pass and keeps it until the pass ends. Leaving a seat never restored the camera: the seat's zoom sets fov 60/zoom, so the tank's third-person view came back through the gunship's narrow lens. leavePilot now resets fov to the camera's 68 before re-seating third person, which also covers leaving a sentry.

Alternatives: Ease the track toward the new lane: rejected, a slow swing of the whole seat mid-aim is still the camera moving on its own.

Evidence:

- src/fx/gunship-optic.js ride(); src/td-tab.js leavePilot().
- npm test (107 programs) and npm run check pass. No browser playtest: the memory guardrail was at WARN.

## 2026-09-16 — The second front: the swarm cracks the sealed mouth behind the bays, and the story is no longer sector-gated

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-second-front-back-door

Owner: "at some point we must protect the OTHER SIDE OF THE BASE." V1 session design, sector 2 THE BACK DOOR. The story planet has 18 clearing mouths and only the gate's (frame [0,-155]) is open; buildStoryPlanet recomputes the sealed ones on every load, but td-tab keeps only the planet's mesh and dungeon. td-tab is at its line budget. The investigation notes flagged a risk that applySector's round 1 also runs in the story and seals the outer world.

src/domain/back-door.js (pure): findBackMouth picks the sealed mouth nearest frame +Z that touches the clearing, leads out (reach share 0.5) and is at most 2 cells wide: cells [1086, 34817] at bearing 11.6 deg, frame [32, 154], open lane beyond [1257, 34816], plus a flank of four rock cells [1084, 34814, 45955, 34815] that come down with it. backBreachCells keeps open lane cells 25-35 hops out from the mouth whose route with the collapse open is at least 20 hops shorter than through the gate alone; the collapse (mouth plus flank) is closed for the gate field and opened for the back field, so the answer is identical before and after the collapse: 272 cells, 40-51 hops from the heart (gate route 75+). Tunables in STORY_BACK_DOOR (src/content/story-defaults.js). story-world.js carries story.backMouth, story.backDoor and story.backPlanet { graph, clearing }. src/fx/dive-shot.js takes the sinkhole's establishing dive out of td-tab (startDiveShot(hooks, direction, { preRoll, hold, tail, dive })); the breach uses it unchanged and the collapse uses it with its own pose (3 cells up, 6 cells out over the rock, the mouth centred with the bays and the Stalheart beyond). storyApi.openBackDoor(): breachWallCell on the six cells, one rebuildAfterBreach, one recomputePortalDist, a tank.shell explosion per cell, sinkhole_quake, Isao's back_door brief ("They cracked the back wall!" / "Build towers behind the bays."), a BACK DOOR diamond on the radar (story-hud back(dir)) and the dive; idempotent via story.backOpen (reset by a new world). storyApi.backDoorOpen() and storyApi.backBreachCandidates() -> [{ cell, side: 'back', hops, route, pos }] (hops: the route once the back is open; route: the live field now). SECTOR GATING WAS ACTIVE: at stage 6 the story had 68,497 rock cells and 'sector 1: 2817 open cells'; rocket-a, rocket-d and wreck-b had no route to the heart. Fix, two same-line changes in td-tab: tdSectorId.fill(storyMode ? 1 : 6) and applySector(!storyMode), the second so the story keeps its own lane-end spawn (plan.cells.fodder, 19498) instead of the planet's farthest cell 276 hops out; spawnFodder's breach then lands on cell 55727 (69 hops, 13.5 cells from the lane end) instead of 56569 (72 hops), both routes joining 35 hops out, outside the gate. Acceptance hooks: openBackDoor, backDoorOpen, backCandidates, backMouth, backBreach(n, k) (a real breach on candidate k with n fodder queued on its own spawn point, leaving story.source alone), backInside (alive bodies inside the clearing on the mouth's half), viewBack(height, back). td-tab 13,858 -> 13,856 lines.

Alternatives: Gate only the mouth cells in the back-breach test; rejected: once the flank rock is down it is a second way through the same wall and the gate-only field found no back cells at all after the collapse.; Only fill the sector map with 1 in the story; rejected: applySector(true) then re-picks the spawn as the planet's farthest open cell (276 hops, far side), which moves spawnFodder's breach off the gate lane.; Put openBackDoor inside the one-line storyApi; rejected to keep the diff mergeable with the other agents editing that line: it is an Object.assign on its own lines right after it.; Frame the collapse with the sinkhole's pose (5 cells up, 4 toward the pole); kept for the sinkhole, but for the mouth it looks outward over empty rock, so the collapse takes a pose from outside looking in.

Evidence:

- node test/back-door.mjs: the pinned mouth, beyond and flank; ~110 hops through the gate to <= 17 through the back for the lane beyond; 13,408 routes shortened; 272 candidates at 40-51 hops, identical before and after the collapse; every candidate's descent on the collapsed route field crosses the fallen rock; the lane end is not a back cell. test/world-recipe.mjs: buildGameWorld carries story.backMouth [1086, 34817].
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --backdoor (run 1, before the framing change): stage 6 wallCount 43,015 (was 68,497) with 28,299 open cells, exactly the Node prediction for no gating; collapse of 6 cells, call 6.9 ms, max rAF delta 22.5 ms over 2.5 s against a 19.3 ms baseline, no longtask entries in either window; the wall count dropped by 6; a second call returned 0; nearest back candidate route < 60 hops and equal to its planned hops; 16 fodder on a back breach reached the clearing on the back half. Screenshots artifacts/browser/backdoor-*.png.
- Runs 1 and 2 measured the collapse with no shot on screen: at the stage-8 load a hull is still rolling out of its bay, and updateCameraGoal gives the deploy framing priority over any shot, so the dive ran unseen with the board frozen (both dive stills show the deploy camera). openBackDoor now skips the dive while a hull deploys; the step waits for the roll-out. Run 3, with the dive rendering: call 6.8 ms, one long task of 70 ms, max rAF delta 78.4 ms (122 frames in 2.5 s against 150 and 22.1 ms at baseline): under the 100 ms target, but the frame cost comes from the shot, not the rebuild.
- Run 4 (dive on screen, cut to orbit): the call took 11.3 ms, then a 119 ms long task and a 130.9 ms frame 131 ms after the call. That is the pre-roll cutting to a whole-planet frame from 3.3 radii, not the rebuild. Fix: startDiveShot gained fromCamera, and the collapse glides from the current camera. Run 5 on the final commit 8c5f70e: stage 6 wall count 43,015; stage 8 collapse of 6 cells (wall count down by exactly 6); call 9.6 ms; no long tasks; max frame 55 ms at +55 ms against a 20.7 ms baseline; every step PASS (stage6, dive, mouth, swarm-inside).
- On the final commit 8c5f70e, --defense passed all six steps and --story-world passed every step.
- npm test (110 programs) and npm run check green at each commit.

## 2026-09-16 — The Quiver's launch report plays only when a rocket leaves, and quieter

decision · accepted · 2026-09-15-quiver-sound-only-on-launch

Owner bug note, 2026-09-15: the Quiver sound pops non-stop, even when not firing a rocket, and it is too loud compared to the rest.

Both Quiver fire paths played sentry_quiver whether or not launchTowerSeeker actually launched. The board walker's emit played it on every refused launch (the story's one round in flight, the pool not ready, the mount loading), and the piloted path played it on every cooldown through the generic per-shot line. Now the sound follows a launch that returned true: the walker checks the result, the generic per-shot report skips missile towers, and the seeker branch plays the report on a real launch. The shipped sentry_quiver gain drops from 0.5 to 0.28, closer to the Rotor's 0.22.

Alternatives: Keep the gain and only fix the trigger: rejected, the owner found the level itself too loud.

Evidence:

- src/td-tab.js stepWalker emit and the seeker branch; src/content/shipped.js sentry_quiver.
- npm test (107 programs) and npm run check pass. No browser listen test: the memory guardrail was at WARN.

## 2026-09-16 — After the Quiver: normal waves, free tower building, and the occasional gunship and orbital laser run

decision · proposed · 2026-09-15-after-the-quiver-open-build-waves-and-runs

Owner, 2026-09-15: after the Quiver, unlock normal waves and total liberty for the player (still beta) to build towers, with normal waves popping like a proper tower defence, and the occasional Gunship and Orbital Laser runs.

Proposed direction for the next story phase, not yet designed. Past the Quiver beat the scripted story hands over to open play: the wave clock runs normal waves, the player places towers freely (beta), and the gunship and the orbital laser arrive as occasional runs. To reconcile with 2026-09-14-handover-gunship-call-expeditions-landed (automatic towers, the wave clock, the earned gunship call-in, guarded expeditions): does free building replace or sit beside printing through Isao, and do the runs come on a timer, on a meter, or both. The orbital laser exists only as labs.html#laser today; bringing it into the game is part of this.

Evidence:

- Owner note, 2026-09-15.

## 2026-09-16 — Isao prints the base in play: a bare story page grows from the landing to the finished base

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-isao-grows-the-base

Owner: 'Isao keeps building stuff, all the elements we have.' A bare index.html was the story at stage 1 and dead-ended: no gate, so no fodder, so no handover (QA 2026-09-16: the optic handed over at 31 s, then nothing until 110 s, NO TARGET · WAVE 0). Design: section A of the 2026-09-16 base-growth investigation notes; QA stage-4 timings (Rotor ready 29.8 s, cleared 70.6 s, settled 93.7 s) set the budget.

planBase(planet, layout, stage, { reach }) marks later islands, structures, the gate and walls pending; the default output is byte-identical (sha256 of the plan JSON at stages 0-8 unchanged on the Node test planet and the baked planet); a pending rocket opens no ground; until still reads the current stage, so a grown base keeps the intact SH02. src/fx/story-base.js: pending pieces stand at zero scale or hidden and rise with growIsland/growWall/grow/growGate (remembered for a model still loading); gate().built, no hydraulics before; a hidden landmark never fetches its near tier. src/content/base-programme.js + src/domain/build-programme.js: eight steps in order, one at a time (gate and walls at rotor-ready, landing pad, Stalheart at breach, solar at expedition -> station, bays in sector 1 idle -> hulls, HUGIN in sector 1 idle -> gunship meter x1.5, radar at sector 2 -> uplink, assembly in sector 2 idle -> rebuild one lost hull per new sector); steps already standing carry their perks, so a static stage-8 base has them all. src/fx/base-print.js maps a step onto the planet (the cell Isao flies to, the bed his beam rasters, the pieces rising; the gate is built only when its walls stand). story-world: grow for grow=1 and for a story page naming no stage (stage=N stays static); the beats hold rotor-ready until the gate is built, then keep the tremor delay; berths stay the invisible camp until the bays are printed; the solar array's pad (story.arrayPad.standing) is false while the solar complex is pending and set by the station perk. td-tab: structure orders placed by storyApi.build() only when no order waits unclaimed, Isao's line as each starts (never over a manned seat or another line), seconds and bed on the order, storyApi.printed turns the walls to rock in dungeon.tags and tdFullTags and adopts the bays, storyApi.perks()/hasPerk(name) for the laser, shield and gunship; the site ring moved to src/fx/site-ring.js and adoptBays extracted (td-tab net -26 lines). The nav Story entry and the ROTOR jump grow too.

Alternatives: Start the V1 entry at stage 4 or later so the tutorial has a gate; rejected: the owner asked for the base to grow in play.; Print the landing slab before the gate (the notes' order); changed to the gate first, so the wait between the Rotor and the tremor is one 12 s print with no trip (QA budget: tremor by 45-60 s).; Start the Stalheart at 'cleared' (the notes); moved to 'breach' because the handover at 'settled' comes about 23 s after 'cleared' at stage 4, shorter than the print plus the trip, while Isao sits idle for about 25 s during the approach.; Block the walls as they rise one by one; rejected: the walls and the gate turn to rock together when the step stands, so no half-built rim leaks the swarm.

Evidence:

- node test/base-plan.mjs (reach = stage byte-identical at 0-8, the non-pending subset of a grown plan is the static plan); the plan hash script before/after identical at stages 0-8 on both planets.
- test/build-programme.mjs (content coverage of every pending piece, order, gates, standing perks, one rebuild per sector); test/story-beats.mjs (the tremor waits for the gate, then its delay).
- npm test and npm run check green at each commit (112 test programs after the v1-loop merge).
- Browser --grow on c0fe9b3 (headless, lock slot 1): PASS grow-bare, grow-landing, grow-gate-printing, grow-tremor, grow-stalheart-rising, grow-fodder, grow-settled, grow-study, grow-solar-rising, grow-bays, grow-hugin. Timings from stage=1&grow=1: Rotor printed 25.9 s, gate print begins 25.9 s, gate stands 38.6 s, tremor 40.1 s, landing pad 48.2 s, override 72.8 s, first wave cleared 80.4 s, Quiver optic 84.9 s, settled (handover) 99.9 s, study 112.5 s, expedition 112.7 s, solar 134.7 s, bays 160.6 s, HUGIN 190.4 s, radar and assembly 233.3 s. The last assertion compared perks in earned order instead of as a set; fixed in 8fb8f88 and re-run: all 12 steps PASS including grow-finished (every step printed in order, all seven perks, a lost hull rebuilt at the next sector start), handover at 100.0 s, radar and assembly at 233.4 s.
- Browser --story-world and --defense on c0fe9b3: every step PASS (explicit stage=N stays static).

## 2026-09-16 — The end-of-sector debrief is a reusable presentation module with its own Workshop lab

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-sector-debrief-module-and-lab

The V1 session design (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-15-v1-session-design.md, section 4) brings back the old campaign debriefs, more exciting: five pages per sector, a LAST TRANSMISSION variant on loss, THE COLONY HOLDS campaign card, dismissed by hand only. src/td-tab.js is at its line budget, so the card had to live outside the controller and be judged before any hookup.

src/fx/sector-debrief.js: createSectorDebrief(host, { play, beep, onContinue, onNewRun, onKeepHolding, reducedMotion }) returns { show(report, { page, isao }), showCampaign(campaign, { isao }), hide, isOpen, page, next, back, dispose } plus pages/skip/animating/element for labs and tests. Pages: SECURE (or LAST TRANSMISSION) with stamps, THE BREACHES (a column per breach with a CLOSED BY stamp), THE KILLS (attribution bars, belt ladder histogram, tempo sparkline in inline SVG), THE TANK (accuracy gauge, shield used and drawn at the array, hull and crate glyphs), THE COLONY (prints, biomass in/out, arsenal, records with rainbow over 1000, Isao's two lines, CONTINUE). A requestAnimationFrame timeline rolls numbers with a rate-limited tick, grows bars, draws the tempo line and thumps stamps in after the numbers land (tower_upgrade plus a small frame jolt). Click, Space or Enter completes a page then advances; arrows, NEXT and BACK move; Esc only completes; held keys never skip; Space on the last page only points at CONTINUE. Seen pages return complete; reduced motion shows everything complete. The card covers its host and stacks by the host's width through a CSS container query (styles.css, the sector debrief section, prefix sdb-). Pure formatting in src/core/debrief-format.js; samples following the report contract in src/content/debrief-samples.js (DEBRIEF_SAMPLES secure, flawless, lost, campaign; DEBRIEF_SAMPLE_ISAO). The lab is labs.html#debrief (src/labs/debrief-tab.js): sample switch, replay, full/1280/400 px host width, sound and motion toggles, deep links ?sample= ?width= ?sound=0 ?still=1.

Alternatives: Extend the old .dbf-* after-action board inside td-tab.js: rejected, the controller is at its line budget and the board is not reusable.; Viewport media queries for the phone layout: rejected for a container query, so a 400 px host stacks on a wide screen and the lab's width toggle is honest.; Auto-advance or a timer to CONTINUE: rejected by the design; the card is dismissed by hand only.

Evidence:

- node test/sector-debrief-samples.mjs: every sample keeps the contract's keys at every level and adds up (sources, tower kinds, belts, tempo bins, forfeits); formatting helpers, page labels and the tempo path.
- npm test and npm run check green.
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --debrief: each sample at 1280x800 and 400x860, every page skipped and advanced with real key presses, labels and page counts checked, no sideways overflow, no console errors or warnings, Esc and last-page Space do not dismiss, the buttons do; screenshots artifacts/browser/debrief-*.png.

## 2026-09-16 — Expedition pickups are seen and heard: our flag over a cleared site, the crate on the MÖRK's back deck, the drop at home and a trophy flag per part

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-expedition-cargo-seen-and-heard

V1 session design (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-15-v1-session-design.md, section 3, pickups that are seen and heard): a part used to be picked up and delivered with only a HUD mark and Isao's brief, with no sound and no object. src/td-tab.js is at its line budget, so the hookup had to be net zero lines.

Assets: the Void capture flag (skinned nine-joint cloth, Flutter/Gust/Raise/Lower) and the intact cargo crate from SentryTowers_A6 at 4a75269d, pinned in docs/cargo-assets.lock.json, registered in scripts/assets.mjs and credited in ATTRIBUTIONS.md. The flag is the most monochrome of the six (graphite); its cloth, emblem and socket lights are recoloured by material name at runtime to white and cyan (src/content/cargo.js CARGO_TINTS). vendor/ has no SkeletonUtils, so each flag is its own GLTFLoader.parseAsync of one fetched buffer. src/fx/cargo.js (presentation, no controller import) owns createCargo: raiseFlag (Raise then Flutter, gate_hydraulics, the part's crate waiting beside it), lowerFlag, pickUp (a 0.5 s arc onto the deck, tank_pickup then a gate_slam clunk), a damped spring sway on pitch and roll driven by the hull's acceleration with teleports ignored, drop (slide off the back, gravity along the ground normal, two bounces with a gate_slam thud each, linger 4 s, sink and fade, at most three crates on the landing), throwOff (tumble with spin, one landing, fade), trophy flags, and view() stills for the screenshots. The MÖRK has no deck socket (SOCKET_SERVICE is a low towing point), so the deck is read from the hull once: five rays down onto the rear of its local bounds, turret and lift field excluded. The crate rides 2.6 m long on the 10 m hull. src/fx/expedition-glue.js took openSite, the step and the radar marks out of td-tab's storyApi and adds the cargo on top of the unchanged rules in src/domain/expeditions.js: guards down raises the site flag, a reach puts the crate on the deck with PART SECURED · <PART>, a delivery lowers the site flag and drops the crate, and its landing calls <TOWER> UNLOCKED, the NEW TOWER UNLOCKED card, tower_upgrade and a trophy flag in a row on the landing island's edge in the foundry's frame. A lost hull throws the crate off and lowers the site flag; the next step raises it again over the part. A new co-cargo callout class (white, 2.6 s, kept whole when praise callouts are off). td-tab changes are in-line only (13,858 lines, unchanged): the import, storyApi.expeditions() plus thin openSite/expeditionsBegin/expeditionStep delegates, the hull-loss call, glue dispose on world rebuild, the tick right after placeActors so a carried crate never trails the hull by a frame, cargo in __stalheartTest.state() and a cargoView(kind, id) test still.

Alternatives: Parent the crate to the hull object; rejected because code that walks the hull (debris on destruction, the playerSpan bounds in the test state, metal dressing) would pick the crate up. The crate stays in world space and is placed after placeActors instead.; Vendor SkeletonUtils from three r160 to clone one skinned flag; rejected for now: a parse per instance is at most a dozen flags and adds no vendored file.; Leave the glue inline in the storyApi one-liner and append the cargo calls to it; rejected because it would grow the longest line in the controller when the add-module skill asks for extraction.; Keep the site flag flying after delivery; rejected: lowering it tells the player the site is done, and the trophy at home carries the credit.

Evidence:

- test/cargo.mjs: the deck is the hull top, not the turret; flag raise then fly, one per site, hoist cue; the crate swings, seats and rides the moving deck; the drop lands once with a thud per bounce, rests and sinks away; lower removes the flag; one trophy per index; the throw-off calls onGone once; dropped crates are bounded; the glue raises the flag on cleared, calls PART SECURED · FIELD COIL on reach, RELAY UNLOCKED and a trophy on landing, and on hull loss lowers the flag and raises it again.
- npm test green (108 test programs), npm run check green (70 pure-layer modules; td-tab line budget holds at 13,858), npm run assets:check green (2 pinned assets at 4a75269d).
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --defense, run 1: exit 0; PASS defense-flag-raised, defense-part-carried, defense-crate-on-hull, defense-crate-dropped, defense-trophy and defense-part-dropped (throw-off with the site flag lowering). The stills showed the flag in white and cyan reading clearly beside the lander, the PART SECURED · FIELD COIL and RELAY UNLOCKED callouts with the NEW TOWER UNLOCKED card, the crate landed at home and the trophy flag on the landing island.
- Run 1 also showed two test-route problems, not gameplay ones. placeTank on a site's own cell stands the tank inside the lander, and on the home cell inside the foundry, so the tank camera saw only the rocket. After a teleport the chase camera trailed behind. The suite now stands the tank on an open cell within reach (glue.standCell, cargoStand in the test adapter) and snaps the camera when a still ends. The dark blue crate barely read on the night side, so its amber label is now our cyan with a faint glow (CARGO_CRATE_TINTS).
- Run 2 (stand cells, camera snap): exit 0, every step green. The trophy still reads: a white and cyan flag on the landing island beside the salvage. But the crate close-up rendered only starfield, the tank-camera still had nothing at centre, and the dropped crate floated on black with no ground. The assertions pass (attached, callouts, rest, trophy) while the look does not. Suspects: the deck bounds stretched by helpers on the unit (deckOf now reads only HULL_SUSPENSION), or cameras that look down 17 degrees clipping or missing the ground where the level trophy and flag cameras do not. Run 3 writes the view numbers (crate, hull, deck, camera near and far) beside the stills to settle it.
- Run 3 (exit 0) found a real bug the green assertions hid. The dropped crate rested at radius 2.05 on a planet of radius 1: drop() clamped its slide with Math.max(0.5, ...) in raw scene units, 376 m at game scale (metres = 0.00133 units), so the crate left the deck at about 2.9 units/s. The Node test used metres = 0.1, where 0.5 looked plausible. The clamp is now 0.5 * metres, and test/cargo.mjs asserts the crate lands within 12 m of the drop point. The same run measured the crate on the hull where it belongs: 2.16 m from the hull origin, 1.2 m up the deck, 2.6 m long (crateScale 0.00186 at 1.86 m source). Yet the close crate still and the tank-camera still still show no tank. Run 4 adds a line-of-sight raycast (glue.sight via cargoView) to name whatever hides it.
- Run 4 (exit 0). The drop fix holds: the crate rests at radius 1.0001 on the landing island, framed with RELAY UNLOCKED and the unlock card. The raycast named the blank stills. From the tank camera and from the close camera, an unnamed ground mesh stood before the crate (12.1 m vs 12.4 m; 39.2 m vs 39.6 m), so tank and camera sat inside raised rock. The test's stand cell was the nearest cell to the flag, blocked or not. standCell now takes the controller's open-cell predicate (dungeon.tags[ci] !== BLOCKED). This is a fault of the acceptance route, not of play, where the tank can only be on open floor.
- Run 5 (exit 1) failed at 'a stand cell beside rocket-a (-1)'. Apart from the lander's own cell, every cell in pickup reach of rocket-a is rock (BLOCKED), so no open stand exists. Within the budget no run 6 was made. standCell now prefers open floor and falls back to the nearest cell, which is run 4's passing behaviour; test/cargo.mjs covers both branches. The browser suite was not re-run on that final commit.
- Known gap, not settled: the tank-camera and close crate stills never showed the tank with the crate on its back. Measured in run 3, the crate sits 2.2 m from the hull origin on the deck, 2.6 m long, visible and loaded, so the gap is framing on the acceptance route (a tank placed in rock beside a lander), not placement. The site flag, the drop at home (with RELAY UNLOCKED and the unlock card) and the trophy flag do read in the stills. The crate on the hull wants an integrated playthrough, driving to a site.

## 2026-09-16 — The Rotor's first-person bullet sound follows the rounds fired, not the report's cadence

issue · resolved · 2026-09-15-rotor-pov-bullet-track

Owner, 2026-09-15: in first person the Rotor's bullet sound stops while it keeps firing and hitting enemies. The owner wants two tracks: the spin revving up and down with the rotor, and the bullet sound for as long as it fires.

8fc3ebd. Three cues were in play: the spin loop (minigun_ready with rotor_spin.wav, gain and pitch on the barrels' spin), the one-shot report (minigun_fire, gated to the Rotor's base cadence since 2026-09-14 so it does not buzz), and the first-person firing loop rotor_pov_fire. That loop ran only while tNow was within 0.6 s of tw.soundAt, the gated report's timestamp, so it starved between reports while the piloted Rotor kept firing at its multiplied rate. Each shot now stamps tw.firedAt and its gap, and the loop runs while the last round left within max(0.2 s, 1.8 gaps). The spin and report cues are unchanged.

Alternatives: Play the one-shot report on every piloted round; rejected: that is the buzz the 2026-09-14 fix removed.; Widen the 0.6 s window; rejected: any fixed window is either longer than a report gap at some rate or keeps the loop running after the trigger is released.

Evidence:

- src/td-tab.js: the povFiring test read tw.soundAt, which the report gate updates at shotInterval(tw.def.rate), not at the piloted cooldown.
- npm test (107 test programs) and npm run check green on 8fc3ebd; td-tab.js line count unchanged.
- NOT browser-verified (memory guardrail at WARN); the owner checks it in the Rotor's first-person seat.

## 2026-09-16 — V1 playthrough fixes: Isao's base prints booked, the opening's breach caves in at sector 1, the gunship briefing never freezes a live sector, HUD and controls-card tidy, sector 2 proven to complete

change · resolved · 2026-09-16-v1-playthrough-fixes

An end-to-end QA playthrough of the V1 session (v1-loop at b17583b7) found: debrief page 5 read ISAO PRINTED 0 although the bays and HUGIN printed during sector 1; a third live breach during sector 2 (SOL-82's 'breach' aim sealed it while both sector breaches stayed live); the first gunship call paused a running sector behind a six-page briefing headed ORBITAL ASSET BRIEFING; the controls card lingered ~170 s and covered SOL-82's seat; the HUD read SECTOR 1 SECTOR 1 · THE LANE and showed SECTOR 0, the ammo counter and x1.00 before sector 1; sectorReport() still held the previous sector's report after CONTINUE. The --sectors step stopped at sector 2's breaches opening.

(1) Only tower orders recorded { type: 'print' }; storyApi.printed (the build-programme finish) now records { type: 'print', id: step.id } (accumulator already covered by test/sector-stats.mjs). (2) Cause by reading: storyApi.breach keeps the opening's sinkhole as story.source and nothing sealed it when sectors took over; sector-run only retargeted story.source. Rule: when a sector begins, every live breach not owned by it collapses (killPortal 'exhausted') with a callout and a two-line Isao brief old_breach; state().sector.strays counts unowned live breaches; SOL-82's host breaches() lists sector-owned breaches first. (3) A first gunship mount while a sector is fighting takes the seat directly and marks the briefing due; the sector's next brief (a calm moment) opens it paused; outside a fight the first mount still opens it paused as before; G (listed on the controls card) opens it again any time; heading is GUNSHIP BRIEFING. (4) The controls card auto-hides 25 s after its one automatic showing and hides while SOL-82's seat is taken; the story HUD's hud-wave span is empty (the sector line names the sector once); ammo and multiplier are hidden before sector 1; begin() clears lastReport. (5) --sectors continues through sector 2 to SECURE and its debrief. td-tab line delta 0 (13,506).

Alternatives: Adopt the opening's breach as one of sector 1's two breaches; rejected: its cell ignores the sector's placement band and separation rules, and a pre-existing sinkhole would skew the programme's timing.; Keep the gunship briefing mid-fight but unpaused; rejected: it swallows every key while open, so the tank would be undriveable during a live wave.

Evidence:

- npm test: 116 test programs passed; npm run check: architecture and line budgets hold (td-tab 13,506).
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --sectors: exit 0, 11 PASS incl. sectors-sector-2-secure and sectors-sector-2-debrief; 'PASS sector 2 secure: 13s, kills 2, breaches A/gate/held/4of4 B/back/held/4of4, prints []'; strays 0 asserted in sector 1 and sector 2; sectorReport() null after CONTINUE.
- --defense exit 0 (11 PASS), --grow exit 0 (12 PASS), default suite exit 0 (48 PASS).
