## 2026-09-16 — Merging the V1 branches: three regressions each branch's own suites missed, found by the full default suite and fixed

issue · resolved · 2026-09-16-v1-merge-regressions-caught

Eleven feature branches built in parallel worktrees overnight were merged into one tree. Each branch had passed its own browser steps; the merged tree was then run through the full default suite and the targeted steps (--sectors, --defense, --grow, --backdoor, --laser-game, --debrief).

Three regressions, all fixed. (1) The wave simulator never reported: after the sectors branch moved the campaign debrief into src/fx/campaign-debrief.js, simEmit still read the td-tab variable `campaign`, which now lived in the module, so every sim tick threw `ReferenceError: campaign is not defined` (found with a console probe of `index.html?sim=style1...`); the module now returns its log and td-tab destructures it (81c3b158). (2) The Units lab's `#units-next` click left a missile alive: the debrief lab's `#tab-debrief{display:flex}` id rule outranked `.tab-hidden{display:none}`, so the hidden debrief tab stayed a full-page fixed layer over every other Workshop lab and swallowed real clicks (document.elementFromPoint at the button returned DIV#tab-debrief.tab.tab-hidden; a DOM click still worked); the display rule is now scoped to `:not(.tab-hidden)` (260cbac5). (3) --grow and --backdoor assumed no sector loop: the loop now owns story.sectorN, and its two breaches clear rock during the back-door measurement window; the steps follow the loop (0ed33356). Also resolved during the merge: conflicts inside td-tab's multi-thousand-character lines were merged at token level (diff3 plus git merge-file on statement and property tokens), with every resolution asserted and syntax-checked.

Alternatives: Trust each branch's own green suites and skip the full default suite on the merged tree; rejected: two of the three regressions only appear when branches meet.; Resolve the long td-tab one-liner conflicts by hand; rejected as error-prone for 6-7k character lines.

Evidence:

- node scripts/browser-test.mjs (default) on 260cbac5: exit 0, 48 PASS including sim-roster-2, units-dart-quiver, units-dart-heptapod, units-tier-mork.
- --debrief on 260cbac5: exit 0, 35 PASS. --grow on 0ed33356: 12/12 PASS. --sectors 8/8 and --defense 10/10 on the merged tree.
- Before the fixes: the default suite timed out at sim-roster-2 (!!window.__stalheartSimResult) on the sectors merge, and failed `1 !== 0` after units-dart-quiver on every tree carrying the debrief lab.

## 2026-09-16 — STATE.md and the roadmap's V1 section catch up with the playtest rounds; the --story-world Rotor heat peak is sampled per frame inside the page

change · resolved · 2026-09-16-state-refresh-and-heat-probe

docs/STATE.md still read 'Updated 2026-09-16 (the V1 session, overnight build)' and knew nothing of the playtest rounds that followed the same day (input and views, difficulty and rebuild, the grey sheet, walls not rock, the legacy tank, the Quiver's scope, the foundry beat, the MK-9, SKIP TUTORIAL, the merge regressions, the playthrough fixes, rock breaks with live breaches, the laser lab step). Its priority 2 documented the --story-world Rotor heat-peak step as flaky: the harness banked window.__rotorHeatMax only inside its until() poll bodies (one sample per 150 ms plus a CDP round trip) while state().key was 'rotor', so a run where the piloted Rotor cleared the wave quickly sampled the barrels' cooling curve by luck and read 0.037, 0.040 and 0.041 on three branches against a 0.05 floor while passing on main both times (2026-09-16-legacy-tank-removed, 2026-09-16-playtest-grey-sheet, 2026-09-16-quiver-scope-built). The threshold was not to be lowered.

docs/STATE.md rewritten short and current: the V1 session with its two entry points (the bare page and index.html?skip=defence#td), what landed on 2026-09-16 in one line each with its entry id, the owner's two open calls (trimming the foundry beat's +14.5 s; ~48 fps at 400+ concurrent enemies, 406 alive at p50 20.9 ms, being profiled), the known gaps (no second gate, Isao's gate repair unanimated, SOL-82 burning only the Stalheart among buildings, the gunship seat's first-use hitch, the MK-9 ignition fireball oversized close up, one release per pass unverified end to end, the laser scorch surviving NEW RUN, Isao not flying to the dropped crate, touch labels untested, the Y-scale print) and an Evidence section naming every browser step in scripts/browser-test.mjs. The hand-written V1 section of ROADMAP.md says the same in its Committed / Candidate / Question shape; the generated Open in the log block is the renderer's. In scripts/browser-test.mjs the heat measurement moved into the page: once the Rotor seat is taken in the stage-4 world, a requestAnimationFrame hook reads window.__stalheartPilotTest.state() every frame, records the max of .heat while .key is 'rotor' and the frame count, and removes itself when the key changes (the Quiver hand-over), the seat is left (the hook object is deleted) or state() throws. The two poll bodies no longer bank anything; the assertion keeps its meaning and its 0.05 floor, additionally requires that the hook sampled at least one frame, and logs the peak and the frame count. Measured per frame the peak is 0.1015, twice the floor, so the earlier failures were the harness's polling, not the barrels.

Alternatives: Lower the 0.05 threshold to what the polled samples read: rejected, it would have hidden the sampling error rather than fixing it and weakened a real assertion about the barrels.; Fire a fresh burst at the assertion: rejected on 2026-09-14 already, since by then the seat may be the Quiver's.; Poll faster from the harness: still a CDP round trip per sample, so still luck; per-frame in the page is the barrels' real peak.; Also refresh the stale rows of the roadmap's Now table (td-tab at 17,608, Heavy Gunship designed not built): left alone, the task was the V1 section; the rows are the owner's prose to argue with.

Evidence:

- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --story-world on this change: exit 0, 31 PASS from story-world-candidate through story-world-default, story-world-rotor-kill, story-world-harvest and story-world-cleared included; the new log line read 'rotor heat peak 0.1015 over 885 frames in the seat'.
- Before, the same step read 0.037, 0.0367, 0.0402 and 0.041 from harness polls on three branches (2026-09-16-legacy-tank-removed, 2026-09-16-playtest-grey-sheet, 2026-09-16-quiver-scope-built) and passed on main twice.
- npm test: 120 test programs passed. npm run check: syntax, source module identity, pinned kernel, logs and assets checked; architecture 87 pure-layer modules, line budgets hold. npm run log:check: valid.
- The browser steps listed in STATE.md are the flags read by args.includes('--...') in scripts/browser-test.mjs: breach-game, sinkhole, story, gunship, nav, sectors, waves (--last), defense, grow, shield-story, skip-tutorial, backdoor, quiver-frame, story-world, shield-perf, missile-parity, laser-game, laser, debrief, plus --dist.

## 2026-09-16 — A Skip Tutorial button starts the session in the back-door fight with two towers already earned

decision · accepted · 2026-09-16-skip-tutorial-into-the-back-door

Owner, 2026-09-16, playtesting the live build with friends in mind: 'we should have a Skip Tutorial button, which skips to a point where already 2 extra towers are unlocked, and the breach of the backdoor starts. Skip Tutorial = start right into the action of protecting the backdoor.' Today the opening always plays from the landing (about 105 s to the handover on a bare page) and the only ways past it are developer jumps in the PLAYTEST drawer (STORY_JUMPS in src/content/nav.js; the DEFENSE jump is index.html?world=story&stage=6&cine=0&acceptance=1&phase=expedition#td). A friend who wants the fight has no player-facing way in.

Accepted as the session's second entry point, to be built: a SKIP TUTORIAL button offered during the opening beats (and mirrored in the PLAYTEST drawer) that starts a run already past the handover, at the finished base, with the sector loop opening the back door. The state it starts in: towers automatic (beats settled/expedition), the base standing through the bays so the hulls read as lives, two expedition towers already unlocked (the Relay and the Mortar, as though rocket-a and rocket-b came home, with their flags and trophies already up), SOL-82 online, the shield rack full and the array reserve charged, and the sector run beginning at the back-door sector so the collapse behind the bays and its breach are the first thing the player sees. Biomass enough to place a few towers before the first wave lands. Open: whether it is a URL a friend can bookmark (a query the route parses) or only a button; whether the campaign's own sector 1 is skipped outright or replayed later; and how the first Isao line explains the situation in two lines.

Alternatives: Point friends at the existing DEFENSE developer jump; rejected: it carries acceptance hooks, starts with no towers and no threat, and is not a player-facing entry.; Shorten the tutorial for everyone; rejected, the owner wants the opening kept and a way past it.

Evidence:

- Owner note, 2026-09-16, on build 5b254b9e.
- Opening timings on a bare page: Rotor 26 s, gate stands 38.5 s, handover 100 s, expedition 112.7 s (--grow run, 2026-09-16).
- The back door and its sector: src/domain/back-door.js, the backDoor flag in src/content/sectors.js, src/fx/sector-run.js.
- Expedition unlocks: src/domain/expeditions.js, STORY_EXPEDITIONS in src/content/story-defaults.js (rocket-a Relay, rocket-b Mortar).

## 2026-09-16 — SKIP TUTORIAL is built: a button in the opening and ?skip=defence, opening a real run at the back door with the Relay and the Mortar earned

change · resolved · 2026-09-16-skip-tutorial-built

The accepted entry 2026-09-16-skip-tutorial-into-the-back-door left three things open: whether the skip is a bookmarkable URL or only a button, whether the campaign's own sector 1 is skipped outright, and how Isao explains the situation in two lines. The owner asked while playtesting with friends in mind, so the entry point had to be player-facing and shareable, not another developer jump carrying acceptance hooks.

All three are answered and shipped. (1) BOTH: `index.html?skip=defence#td` is the URL a friend can bookmark and `src/fx/skip-tutorial.js` is the button. `skipsTutorial` in src/core/story-route.js makes ?skip= name the story the way ?story= does, so the link needs no acceptance switch and never lands on the campaign board; `skipTutorialUrl` builds the button's href by dropping the opening's keys (stage, story, grow, phase, threat) and keeping the rest, so a click inside a harness run stays a harness run. The button is bottom-right in the story's monochrome (styles.css #skip-tutorial), created only in the story world and only when this run is not itself the skip, and it removes itself the moment the run is automated -- the handover dismisses it if the player does not. It is mirrored as the wired SKIP TUTORIAL entry in STORY_JUMPS (src/content/nav.js), pointing at the same player URL. (2) SECTOR 1 IS SKIPPED OUTRIGHT: createSectorRun takes a `firstSector` hook and the skipped run opens at sector 2, THE BACK DOOR, so the collapse behind the bays, its breach, SOL-82 coming online and the array's refill all arrive through the sector table rather than through a special case. (3) Isao's two lines are the new `skip_defence` brief in src/isaobriefs.js -- 'Base is printed, Relay and Mortar are ours, SOL-82 answers when we call.' / 'The rock behind the bays is about to give. Build, and hold the back door.' -- shown on arrival. The rest of the starting state: STORY_SKIP in src/content/story-defaults.js pins stage 8 (the base standing through the bays, so the three hulls read as lives), phase 'expedition' (towers automatic), 450 kg of biomass and the two parts; readStoryQuery applies them and forces grow off and the full threat of a real run, not a deep link's sparse 0.35; a new pure rule `deliverAtOnce` and a glue method `preDeliver` put the Relay and the Mortar home as though rocket-a and rocket-b had come back, raising each site's flag with no crate beside it (cargo.raiseFlag now takes { crate, standing }) and standing two trophies in the row at the landing; the shield rack is filled and the array refilled. It is a real run: waves come on the run's own clock, the gate can break, the debrief follows and CONTINUE moves to sector 3. No acceptance-only hook is switched on -- ?skip=defence carries no acceptance=1, and the harness adds its own. A bare index.html still plays the tutorial from the landing, now with the offer on it.

Alternatives: A button that mutates the running game into the skipped state instead of navigating; rejected, a browser document owns one game lifetime (docs/STATE.md, Boundaries) and every route change already reloads -- a link is the cheap and honest form.; Leaving the site flags down at the two pre-delivered sites, which is what the real delivery flow does (glue.step lowers the flag when the part comes home); rejected on the owner's wording, and the crate is suppressed instead so the site does not read as still holding its part.; Reusing the DEFENSE developer jump URL for friends; rejected as before -- acceptance hooks, no towers, no threat.; Starting the skipped run at sector 1 and letting the back door arrive a sector later; rejected, the ask is the back-door fight as the first thing seen.

Evidence:

- npm test (120 test programs) and npm run check green on every commit; new cases in test/story-route.mjs (the skip URL is the story, both spellings, the href rewrite), test/expeditions.mjs (deliverAtOnce gives relay and mortar and reveals nothing else) and test/nav-content.mjs (the drawer's jump is the player URL).
- scripts/browser-test.mjs --skip-tutorial: the opening still plays the tutorial and carries the offer; a real pointer click on #skip-tutorial lands on ?skip=defence; the run there is automated at phase expedition with unlocked = rotor, quiver, relay, mortar, two flags up and two trophies home, three hulls, three bays, 620 kg, a full rack, a charged array; the build menu offers the Relay and the Mortar and still reads PART OUT on the lancer and the plasma; sector 2 THE BACK DOOR opens with one breach behind the bays and one on the gate side, SOL-82 online, and a wave arrives without being asked (15 enemies, A/gate 1/4, B/back 1/4).
- The same run fought to the end: SECTOR SECURE at 33 s with both breaches HELD, the debrief card, and CONTINUE into sector 3.
- Screenshots artifacts/browser/skip-tutorial-offer.png, -arrival.png, -back-door-fight.png. The first fight screenshot was a picture of bare lattice because the step's own build-menu probe opens the radial on the first placeable cell on the planet and takes the build camera there; the probe now runs after the fight is photographed.
- src/td-tab.js stays at its 13505-line budget (net 0): the wiring rides on existing lines and one four-line comment was folded to three.

Supersedes: 2026-09-16-skip-tutorial-into-the-back-door

## 2026-09-16 — Sixty fps under the horde: the frame at 400 enemies was going to the post chain's fixed per-pixel work, not to the enemies, and two pixel-identical cuts give it back

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-sixty-fps-under-the-horde

With the wave ramp shipped (2026-09-16-playtest-difficulty-and-rebuild) sector 3's biggest wave runs about 406 bodies at once and the harness had measured it at p50 20.9 ms / p95 46 ms / max 72.7 ms. The owner wants the horde and wants it to play well; lowering sector 3's threat was ruled out. The task was to find where the frame actually goes at 400+ concurrent and fix that without touching a gameplay rule. The 2026-09-13 swarm numbers (505 concurrent at 60 fps, 2000 merged dots under 0.1 ms) were taken in the lab, not on this path.

MEASURED, NOT GUESSED. Reproducing the benchmark on an idle machine gave p50 17.2 / p95 24.5 / max 44.5 at 406 concurrent (the entry's 20.9/46/72.7 were taken while the overnight build had several sessions running: the machine, not the code, was the difference). A CDP CPU profile across the wave put the whole frame() at 4.25 ms, of which the crowd's simulation (updateEnemies) was 0.34 ms and postfx.render 3.23 ms; the enemy scans the brief named as suspects (gunship breachLoads, gate pressure, laser contacts, radar blips, sector polls) are all under 0.1 ms together. A gl hook that skips draw calls by primitive isolated the GPU side: skipping every POINTS draw (all ~360 enemy draws a frame, two passes) saved 0.5 ms; skipping every triangle and line draw saved nothing; skipping EVERY draw still left 5.2 ms of GPU a frame at dpr 1 and 23.7 ms at dpr 2 - the fixed floor of the bloom chain (two full-resolution scene targets, one of them 4x multisampled, half-float clears and resolves, the mip chain). At dpr 2, the display most owners play on, the 404-enemy frame sat at p50 21.6 ms with 4 enemies or 400. THE CHANGES, all in src/postfx.js and all output-identical: (1) applyWeights visited every object in the scene each frame and did nothing for the ungrouped ones, and ran the material-conflict tripwire every frame; with the default weight at 1 it now iterates the weight map, and the tripwire runs once a second (0.43 -> 0.09 ms a frame). (2) Each of the two RenderPasses walked scene.updateMatrixWorld again with nothing moved between them; render() updates once and holds scene.matrixWorldAutoUpdate off only across the two passes. (3) EffectComposer's RenderPass draws into the read buffer (renderTarget2) and never swaps, so the final composer's renderTarget1 only ever receives full-screen shader passes; it no longer carries 4x MSAA or a depth buffer (a fully covered pixel resolves to its one fragment). (4) UnrealBloomPass's closing additive blend back into its buffer is read by nothing (postfx takes the pure bloom from renderTargetsHorizontal[0]); its material is not drawn. RESULT: the fixed GPU floor 5.2 -> 3.8 ms at dpr 1 and 23.7 -> 13.3 ms at dpr 2; the dpr-2 frame at 404 enemies 21.6 -> 16.7 ms p50 (p95 23.2 -> 17.8); CPU frame() 4.25 -> 3.37 ms; at dpr 1 the frame was already vsync-bound and stays at p50 16.7-17.3. No gameplay rule, number or pixel changed; src/td-tab.js untouched at 13,505 lines. WHAT IS STILL HOT: three.js's per-object submission for two passes (~2.1 ms CPU at 400 bodies: renderObjects 0.93, updateMatrixWorld 0.52, projectObject 0.47, setProgram 0.41) - the only remaining crowd-scaling cost, answerable by an instanced swarm with per-instance tint/opacity attributes (src/fx/dot-swarm.js, 2026-09-14-dots-not-draw-calls) but not justified at these numbers; the post chain's remaining floor (13.3 ms at dpr 2: the weighted scene rendered at full device resolution only to be halved by the luminosity pass, the MSAA scene target's clear and resolve) which can only come down by changing the look (bloom scale 0.5 on desktop as the phone tier does, MSAA 2x); the breach dive shot, which renders the sinkhole close up at 5M triangles and ran 25 ms a frame at dpr 1 and 93 ms at dpr 2 in these runs, unrelated to the horde; and the wave's spawn-in, where 400 bodies built over 3.2 s put the p95 of the first twelve seconds at 22-29 ms (the harness's stock window measures that, the steady state after it holds p95 18-19).

Alternatives: Hoisting the per-frame enemy scans into one shared pass or bucketing enemies spatially (gunship breachLoads, tickGate, laser candidates, radar blips): not done - the profile puts all of them under 0.1 ms a frame at 400 bodies; the change would add code for no measurable gain.; Striding low-priority per-enemy work across frames: not done, updateEnemies is 0.34-0.42 ms a frame in total.; Merging the crowd into the instanced dot swarm: not done. The 2026-09-14 lab measurement already showed draw calls are not the wall, and this profile agrees (skipping every enemy draw saves 0.5 ms of GPU); the CPU submission it would remove is ~2 ms, at the price of per-instance tint and opacity attributes and every material call site in td-tab.js.; Lowering the desktop bloom scale to 0.5 or MSAA to 2x: would take most of the remaining dpr-2 floor but changes the picture; left for the owner to decide with the numbers in hand.; Rendering the weighted bloom-source scene at half resolution: the same trade, the bloom of thin bright lines would soften; not done.

Evidence:

- BEFORE, stock: scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --waves --last: 406 concurrent, p50 17.2 p95 24.5 max 44.5 over 657 frames (idle machine; the entry's 20.9/46/72.7 over 474 frames was a loaded one).
- AFTER, stock, same command: 406 concurrent, p50 17.3 p95 22.7 max 52 over 660 frames.
- artifacts/qa/horde-bench.mjs (CDP Profiler across the wave, before/after): frame() 4.25 -> 3.37 ms a frame; postfx.render 3.23 -> 2.21; applyWeights 0.43 -> 0.09; updateEnemies 0.34 / 0.42; the harness's own state() poll costs 1.2-1.5 ms a frame of what the benchmark reports. Top self-time both runs: (idle), the harness's state(), (program), projectObject/updateMatrixWorld/multiplyMatrices/renderBufferDirect.
- artifacts/qa/horde-split2.mjs (gl draw hook, dpr 1): loaded 404 alive p50 16.7 p95 18.1, gpu 9.5-10.3 ms; no points draws 9.8; no triangle/line draws 11.6; NO draws 5.2 -> after 3.8 ms.
- artifacts/qa/horde-split2.mjs --dpr=2: BEFORE loaded 404 alive p50 21.6 p95 23.2, gpu 27.4, no draws 23.7 ms; AFTER loaded p50 16.7-17.2 p95 17.8-18.4, gpu 17.9-18.3, no draws 13.3 ms.
- Quiet window in every split run was the breach dive shot (state().shot === 'breach'): 5M triangles at 25 ms a frame (dpr 1) and 93 ms (dpr 2), 32 alive.
- npm test: 120 test programs passed. npm run check: architecture, line budgets and top-level placement hold; src/td-tab.js unchanged at 13,505.
- Browser suites after the change through the lock: --waves --last PASS waves-pile; --sectors 11 PASS lines through sectors-sector-2-debrief; --defense 11 PASS lines through defense-part-dropped.

## 2026-09-16 — Sectors are the story's loop past the handover, and the gate takes the pile's pressure

decision · accepted · 2026-09-16-sectors-are-the-story-loop

After the handover the story still ran the campaign board's single-breach wave clock: storyApi.breach overwrote one story.source, programmeSpent sealed every breach when a sector's last wave was held, checkVictory played the campaign pull-out, loseGame showed the campaign verdict, and the campaign HUD lines, tower-unlock toasts, WHY THEY COME brief, NEW THREAT cards and SitRep leaked into the story. QA (2026-09-16) also found no pressure: waves piled outside the closed gate forever, so a wave never cleared and the colony could not be lost. src/td-tab.js was at its line budget.

src/fx/sector-run.js composes src/domain/sectors.js, sector-stats.js and the new src/domain/gate-integrity.js through controller hooks. Once the story reaches the expedition phase (or a defend stage) with no brief, shot or screen in the way, sector 1 starts with a brief card and Isao's two lines, then opens its breaches as a list (pickBreachCells over the gunshipFar ring; back-side candidates from storyApi.backBreachCandidates when present, else all on the gate side), staggered 1.5 s. The wave clock arms only while every breach stands ready and releases one programme wave per live breach (computeWavePlan at the sector's threat, a hard core per content flag). killPortal is the single seal hook: the gunship's 105 (executeStrike use gunship.heavy), the strike, three tank shells on a sector breach, and a 'laser' reason book closeBreach with forfeitOf's estimate; a breach whose last wave has emerged is spent, pays HELD as biomass and score (score.addBonus) and collapses. SECTOR SECURE, a 3 s pause, then the real src/fx/sector-debrief.js card; CONTINUE starts the next sector (refillArrays, setLaserOnline, openBackDoor with an 8 s lead), THE COLONY HOLDS after sector 3, KEEP HOLDING continues, NEW RUN and a loss (LAST TRANSMISSION) reload. Stats are recorded at the real sites (damageEnemy with a via argument for tower key and gunship gun, the ram, shell resolution, playerHit, heartHit, finishOrder, scoreKill and the wave bonus) or polled (gunship passes, shield seconds, arrayStation.drawn, delivered parts, biomass ledger, storyApi.laserStats). Bests persist under td.story.sector-bests. THE GATE: enemies within 2.5 cells wear it (0.5 hp/s fodder, 3 hp/s solid core, 60 hp); at zero it breaks, story.sealed lets the swarm through and the gate clip is forced open; Isao mends it at 3 hp/s while no enemy is within 8 cells and it closes again at 60%. The campaign debrief builders and the SitRep moved to src/fx/campaign-debrief.js unchanged, freeing 292 td-tab lines.

Alternatives: Walls take damage too: rejected for tonight; the gate is the one opening the swarm paths toward, and wall damage would need a pathfinder rebuild per break.; Open the gate under pressure and close it when the lane clears, without hit points: rejected because it gives no readable integrity and no Isao repair beat.; Keep the global wave counter and split one plan across both breaches (the campaign spawner): rejected; each breach owns its programme so closing one forfeits only its waves.

Evidence:

- npm test (112 programs) and npm run check green after merging v1-loop
- test/gate-integrity.mjs
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --sectors: 8 steps pass (artifacts/browser/sectors-*.png); the first sector's report: 18 s, 119 kills, 181 kg left in the field, breaches closed by gunship and held
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --defense: 6 steps pass once the step calls sectorQuiet(true); before that the loop's pressure broke the gate and a leak at the story's Stalheart threw every frame (heartSprite.userData.hit missing, fixed with optional chaining), then the one-Rotor base lost the colony mid-step
- Not run tonight: --story-world and the default suite (budget)

## 2026-09-16 — Sector rules and the sector stats accumulator: pure, Node-tested, not yet wired

decision · accepted · 2026-09-16-sector-rules-and-stats

The V1 session design (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-15-v1-session-design.md, sections 2 and 4) needs a sector programme of two breaches with a forfeit rule, a secure test, a breach placement policy, and a debrief report contract with stamps and records. The game controller is at its line budget, so the rules land as pure modules first and the integrator wires them into src/td-tab.js later.

src/content/sectors.js holds the SECTORS table (THE LANE, THE BACK DOOR, BOTH WALLS), the sector 4+ generator, placement in cells (12 apart, 6 from a sealed breach, a 3-hop band), the forfeit assumptions, the belt of every enemy type, closer and source labels, nine stamps and six records. src/domain/sectors.js owns sectorDef, makeSector, releaseWave, nextWaveIndex, breachLive, closeBreach, spendBreach, isSecure, summary, forfeitOf, waveYield and pickBreachCells. src/domain/sector-stats.js owns makeSectorStats, record, report, reportMetrics, stampEarned, mergeBests, checkReport and campaignTotals. src/content/debrief-fixtures.js carries four generated reports as DEBRIEF_FIXTURES { secure, flawless, lost, campaign }. Calls made without the owner: (1) the wave ladder does not add up across sectors. Added up, sector 3 fights ladder waves 8 to 12, about 670 bodies a breach once the invasion surge starts at wave 9. Instead each sector starts at ladderStart (0, 1, 3; generated 3) and every wave is capped at ladder wave 8, so the threat does the growing. (2) The spec's 'waves 5 + n' counts n past the authored table: 6, 7, 8 waves. (3) 'Alternating sides' means the generated pair alternates sectors, both on the gate side and then both at the back. (4) A record's best is the standing best before the sector (null when none); mergeBests returns the store to persist. (5) The report adds kills.byGun { rotary, bofors, heavy } to the contract for the debrief's gunship 25/40/105 bars. (6) The extra stamps are NOT A SCRATCH (secure, 10+ tank or ram kills, no damage, no hull lost), SPECIAL DELIVERY (a part home) and CLOSE AIR SUPPORT (30+ gunship kills). (7) Stamp rules are data, [metric, op, value] triples, so the content stays a frozen table.

Alternatives: Continue the ladder across sectors (waveBase = the sum of earlier waves): rejected, it reaches the invasion surge in sector 3 and thousands of bodies by sector 4; Restart every sector at ladder wave 1: rejected, sector 3 would bring only the first three enemy types; Stamp rules as functions in content: rejected for a frozen, serialisable table

Evidence:

- node test/sectors.mjs
- node test/sector-stats.mjs
- npm test
- npm run check
- computeWavePlan totals at threat 1.7: wave 8 = 32, wave 9 = 106, wave 12 = 227

## 2026-09-16 — The back-door cost step waits out the sector breach shot; rock breaks do not scale with open breaches

change · resolved · 2026-09-16-rock-break-with-live-breaches

--backdoor failed at 'no long task over 100 ms from the collapse (107)' once the sector loop kept two breaches open. Hypothesis: every rock break recomputes per-portal route fields over the 71k-cell story planet, so cost scales with live breaches.

Hypothesis rejected by a CDP CPU profile of the collapse window with both sector breaches live. The route work is two single multi-source BFS passes (rebuildAfterBreach 2.6 ms incl. the surface patch, recomputePortalDist 1.5 ms, bfsDist self ~3-4 ms), independent of the number of portals; openBackDoor totalled 6.9 ms. The 65 ms busy frame was 36 ms of a synchronous shader link (WebGLProgram.getUniforms -> getProgramInfoLog in onFirstUse) and state().shot read 'breach': the step measured while the sector breach's establishing shot was still running, so openBackDoor refused the dive (a shot was live) and the window caught the breach cut's first-use programs. Fix, test only: after the breaches stand, wait for shot===null (+500 ms) before the baseline and collapse windows. With it the collapse window showed no long task, max frame 25.6 ms (baseline 19.2), call 9.9 ms, no new programs compiled, and the dive framed the collapse. No game code changed; src/td-tab.js delta 0. SOL-82 rock burns (breakCells) and tank shells breaking rock run rebuildAfterBreach only: one BFS plus the patch, ~2.6 ms from the same profile, not separately measured in a browser run.

Alternatives: Defer recomputePortalDist/distToHeart to a coalesced next-frame job: not needed, the BFS is ~4 ms and does not scale with breaches.; Pre-warm shader programs at load: the first-use link belonged to the breach establishing shot, not the collapse; left as a possible follow-up if a real-play hitch is reported.

Evidence:

- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --backdoor: PASS backdoor-stage6, backdoor-dive, backdoor-mouth, backdoor-swarm-inside
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --sectors: PASS all 8 steps
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --defense: PASS all 11 steps
- npm test: 116 test programs passed; npm run check: exit 0
- temporary Profiler.start/stop around the collapse window (removed before commit)

## 2026-09-16 — The Quiver's reticle becomes a live targeting instrument in SOL-82's language

decision · accepted · 2026-09-16-quiver-scope-like-the-laser

Owner, playtesting the live build on 2026-09-16: 'we make the reticle/targeting system of the Quiver closer to that of the Orbital Laser, more exciting, moving elements, colors change, more tracking within the tracking, blinking confirmation that lock was acquired, more telemetry.' The Quiver's optic today is the sniper scope with one lock square and a timer along its top edge (src/fx/story-scope.js); SOL-82's scope and inset HUD (src/fx/laser-scope.js, src/fx/laser-inset-hud.js) carry the moving rings, phase colours and telemetry the owner wants.

Accepted as presentation work within the authorised playtest round: the lock RULES do not change (whatever sits inside the square is the target, the timer runs while it stays, 0.9 s to lock; agency is legibility, 2026-09-13), only what the player sees. The scope gains phase-coloured elements that move, an inner tracking box that converges on the held target, a blinking TARGET LOCKED confirmation and telemetry (range, bearing, time to lock, what is held). Monochrome vocabulary with amber for warnings only.

Alternatives: Change the lock timing or cone to feel better; rejected, the owner asked for the instrument, not the rule, and the current lock was tuned into fairness on 2026-09-13.; Share one scope module between the Quiver and SOL-82; rejected for now because the laser aims a continuous beam from orbit and the Quiver holds a seeker cone, so their readouts differ.

Evidence:

- Owner note, 2026-09-16, on build 5e204f48.
- Quiver lock rules and the box: docs/STORY.md 'The lock is the box'; src/fx/story-scope.js; STORY_QUIVER in src/content/story-defaults.js.
- The look to borrow: src/fx/laser-scope.js and src/fx/laser-inset-hud.js.

## 2026-09-16 — The Quiver's optic is built in SOL-82's register: four phases, an inner box that closes on the held body, a TARGET LOCKED blink and telemetry in the margins

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-quiver-scope-built

The owner asked while playtesting for the Quiver's reticle to come closer to the Orbital Laser's: more exciting, moving elements, colour that changes, tracking within the tracking, a blinking confirmation that the lock was acquired, more telemetry. The lock RULES were fought for on 2026-09-13 ('agency is legibility') and could not move: whatever sits inside the lock square is the target, the timer runs while it stays there, and the square is drawn at the seeker's true angular cone (STORY_QUIVER.coneDeg projected through the camera's own field of view). So this is presentation only - no timing, cone, range or targeting rule was touched, and the reticle must not claim anything the seeker does not take.

src/fx/story-scope.js is rewritten from an SVG reticle plus a DOM field panel into one 2D canvas drawn each frame from a plain frame object, in the idiom of src/fx/laser-inset-hud.js. Four phases carry their own colour and motion: SEARCHING drifts a wide segmented ring in dim cyan with nothing held; TRACKING tightens the ring and speeds it up as the timer fills, in bright cyan; LOCKED snaps it still and blinks the tag in white; AWAY counter-turns it slowly while a round flies and stops the blink. Amber is left for warnings only (out of range, the launcher holding while its TALON is in the air). Tracking within the tracking: the true-size square is unchanged and a second box of corner brackets converges from its edge onto the held body's own projected point on the glass, fed from the same enemy the lock holds, with a dashed tether while it closes. The TARGET LOCKED blink floors at a third of full brightness so a still frame still reads as locked, and a short laser_click (an existing cue, nothing newly pinned) ticks once when the lock takes. Telemetry sits in plated blocks in the margins beside the sight, never over the square: SEEKER (cone in degrees, magnification, reach), LAUNCHER (state, rounds in flight, cooldown, trigger armed or safe) and TRACK (contact, type and belt from BELT_OF, range in metres, bearing, time to lock with its meter). prefers-reduced-motion drops every spin and blink and names the phase in words instead. src/td-tab.js stayed at its 13,505-line budget: the call site and the pilot test API grew only within existing lines, and the dead DOM panel's CSS came out of styles.css. The acceptance probe gained __stalheartPilotTest.lock() and aimSky() so the run can stand in each phase deliberately, and --quiver-frame now saves SEARCHING, TRACKING, LOCKED and the round away as screenshots.

Alternatives: Keep the SVG reticle and animate it. Rejected: the phases need per-frame motion and the existing code only rebuilt innerHTML when the meter bucket changed, so every spin or blink would have meant rebuilding the whole markup each frame.; Keep the DOM TRACK panel beside a canvas reticle. Rejected: two rendering paths for one instrument, and the owner asked for the laser HUD's register, which is plated monospace blocks tucked around the scope.; Size the inner box from the body's true angular size. Rejected for now: the honest subtense is not plumbed to the seat, and a guessed size would make the reticle claim something about what the seeker takes. The inner box converges to a small fixed bracket on the body's point instead, and the outer square remains the only size statement.; Draw the LOCKED blink over the whole sight. Rejected after looking at the first screenshots: it caught TARGET LOCKED at a fifth of its brightness, so the state that matters most was the hardest to see. Only the tag and the closing box blink now.

Evidence:

- npm test: 120 test programs passed. npm run check green (architecture: 87 pure-layer modules, line budgets hold; src/td-tab.js is still 13505 lines).
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --quiver-frame: green. PASS quiver-scope-searching, quiver-scope-tracking, quiver-scope-locked, quiver-scope-away, and all twelve quiver-frame view captures. The round still stays in frame: pov 148 of 148 from launch to ignite (100.0%), third 152 of 152 (100.0%).
- artifacts/browser/quiver-scope-{searching,tracking,locked,away}.png are the four phases; they were read, and the blink floor, the ring's gaps at full lock and the phase word's position above the seat's views strip were changed in response.
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --story-world: reached story-world-rotor-kill and then failed the Rotor heat-peak assertion (peak 0.041 against a 0.05 floor) BEFORE the Quiver section, which is the intermittent sampling failure already recorded in docs/STATE.md on 2026-09-16. Nothing in this change touches rotor heat, and the same quiver-piloting beat (lock, fire, flight) is exercised green by --quiver-frame. The Quiver steps of --story-world were not reached and remain unverified on this branch.

Supersedes: 2026-09-16-quiver-scope-like-the-laser

## 2026-09-16 — Held inputs are released when the page stops listening; the gunship is thermal only, its contact squares are gone, and 7 8 9 0 take the seats

change · resolved · 2026-09-16-playtest-input-and-views

Owner playtest of the V1 session (docs/STATE.md, priority 1) returned four notes. (1) The plasma thrower stuck on auto-fire with a repeated clicking cue, triggered by taking a screenshot: focus leaves the document, the keyup for a held trigger never lands, and the game keeps holding it; the not-ready voice behind it then retriggers every frame. Only 'blur' was handled, and only for the five drive keys (src/td-tab.js) and the seat's own trigger (src/sentry-pilot.js) - a window can keep focus while the input stops arriving. (2) The gunship seat opened in thermal but M cycled it to night and normal, so FLIR was a mode rather than the seat's identity. (3) A small square sat over each enemy. (4) Switching between TANK, GUNSHIP, SOL-82 and MAP had no intuitive keys.

src/core/held-input.js rules on the four events that mean the page lost the input (blur; visibilitychange when HIDDEN; pointerlockchange when a lock we HAD is gone; mouseleave of the canvas) and drops every held flag; src/td-tab.js and src/sentry-pilot.js both release on all four, the tank clearing all seven keys instead of five and the seat clearing its trigger and turn. src/core/cue-gate.js floors each cue name so a flickering hold cannot turn the `ready`/`stop` edges into a stream of clicks (src/weapon-voice.js); a round actually leaving the barrel clears the floor, so a real burst still announces its end while a stuck trigger, which fires nothing, stays quiet. The gunship optic is thermal and nothing else: the MODES cycle, the M handler, the seat footer's offer and the dead .gunship-night CSS are gone, and the GROUND TRUTH / IMPACT monitor and the radar keep their true colour. The squares were the optic's contact markers - one screen-sized white point per living enemy, depthTest off, drawn over everything (src/fx/gunship-optic.js) - and are removed outright along with contactsStyle(); the lock reticle the owner keeps is a different thing, the sentry optic's centred SVG box in src/fx/story-scope.js, untouched. 7 8 9 0 are TANK, GUNSHIP, SOL-82 and MAP: a capture-phase handler registered before any seat installs its own clicks the views strip's matching button, so a seat that is not available yet refuses exactly as the button does, the existing keys still work, and src/fx/controls-card.js teaches the four. src/td-tab.js stayed at exactly its 13506-line budget (net 0).

Alternatives: Handle only 'blur', as before: rejected - the screenshot case can leave the window focused while the keyup is delivered elsewhere, which is the reported bug.; Gate the repeated cue inside src/audio.js for every sample: rejected - the floor is a presentation decision about weapon edges, not a property of the mixer, and a global gate would swallow legitimately dense fire.; Key the cue gate by role (ready vs stop) rather than by sample name: rejected - the Rotor's stop and ready are the same sample, so a flicker would still alternate two gated roles and buzz at twice the floor.; Keep the gunship's view switch but default it to thermal: rejected by the owner's note - FLIR is the seat's visual identity, 'no switch'.; Scope the contact markers to the seat's map view instead of removing them: rejected - they are one square per enemy wherever they are drawn, which is exactly what the owner asked to remove; the Quiver's lock box is a separate, centred reticle and was already scoped to the optic.

Evidence:

- npm test: 118 test programs passed, including the new test/held-input.mjs (four release events, hidden/lock edges, every held flag dropped once) and test/cue-gate.mjs (first cue speaks, a flickering trigger cannot buzz, names independent, reset re-arms).
- test/trigger-sequence.mjs caught the first cut of the gate swallowing the Rotor's stop cue (expected ['minigun_ready','sentry_rotor','minigun_ready'], got two); reset-on-shot fixed it and the test passes unchanged.
- npm run check: architecture 85 pure-layer modules checked, game/lab controller boundaries, line budgets and top-level placement hold; src/td-tab.js 13506 lines against its 13506 budget (net 0).
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --gunship: PASS gunship-off-station, gunship-briefing, gunship-pov-rotary, gunship-pov-heavy, gunship-thermal, gunship-thermal-held, gunship-top-view, gunship-third, gunship-rotary-fired, gunship-bofors-burst, gunship-heavy-falling, gunship-heavy-reloading, gunship-skip-enemies, gunship-track-before, gunship-track-after.
- The --gunship step now asserts the seat stays thermal under M and that neither gunship-normal nor gunship-night is reachable (scripts/browser-test.mjs, step gunship-thermal-held).
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --defense: PASS defense-handover, defense-controls-card, defense-towers-fire, defense-gunship-called, defense-towers-under-gunship, defense-flag-raised, defense-part-carried, defense-crate-on-hull, defense-crate-dropped, defense-trophy, defense-part-dropped.

## 2026-09-16 — The grey sheet over the landing was the foundry's skinned mesh cloned off a cached glTF

change · resolved · 2026-09-16-playtest-grey-sheet

Playtesting the bare index.html the owner reported 'a strange very large grey sheet in the background of the base' at the landing; a QA run had independently called the same thing 'a large pale structure blocking the Rotor optic at stage 1'. A raycast through the offending screen points could not hit anything there, so the object was found instead by rendering the story scene to a render target, sampling the wedge pixels, and hiding each drawn object in turn until those pixels changed.

The sheet is ARRIVAL_FOUNDRY_ROOT, the SkinnedMesh root of assets/models/story/afr_01_seed_foundry_d0_lod1.glb (160 triangles, one skin of 5 joints, the only skinned GLB in the project). src/fx/story-base.js mount() cloned every landmark with gltf.scene.clone(true), which copies a SkinnedMesh's skeleton BY REFERENCE: the clone stayed bound to the cached glTF's own bones, which are never added to a rendered scene and so never get a world-matrix update, and the bind pose resolved into a sheet spanning 2.13 x 2.23 x 1.24 world units on a radius-1 planet (three's skinned boundingSphere read 627). The geometry bounding sphere stayed 0.0042, which is exactly why Mesh.raycast early-outs and no ray could find the thing that was plainly on screen. cloneSkinned - the rebinding clone that already existed, uncalled, in src/units.js - moved to src/fx/skinned-clone.js, and mount() now uses it; it is a plain deep clone for unskinned graphs, so nothing else changes. The wedge pixels go from [38,42,43] to [0,0,0] and the foundry renders as the model it is.

Alternatives: Vendoring SkeletonUtils: rejected, the sibling repos all ship three r180 against this project's r160, already a recorded dead end from the Line2 trio.; A second rebinding clone written inside src/fx: rejected, the helper existed in src/units.js; it moved to src/fx/skinned-clone.js and units.js re-exports it rather than growing a duplicate.; Hiding the foundry until stage 2, or a pending piece drawn at zero scale: ruled out, the foundry is meant to stand at the landing and the sweep showed the pose, not the reveal, was wrong.; A mis-scaled far tier: ruled out, the derived LOD2 carries no skin at all and renders correctly.

Evidence:

- Before and after from the same camera: artifacts/qa/sheet-owner/owner-view2.jpg and artifacts/qa/sheet-after/after-view2.jpg.
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node artifacts/qa/sheet-owner.mjs <label> sweeps every drawn object: Scene/Story base/Group/foundry/ARRIVAL_FOUNDRY_ROOT owns 12 of 12 wedge pixels before the fix and no object owns them after, the pixels reading pure black sky.
- npm test: 116 test programs passed. npm run check: passed, architecture and the src/td-tab.js line budget unchanged (td-tab untouched, 0 lines).
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --grow: PASS through grow-finished.
- REGRESSION LEFT OPEN FOR THE OWNER: --story-world fails on this branch at the Rotor heat peak assertion (scripts/browser-test.mjs:774) with 0.0367 and 0.0402 against its >0.05 threshold, on two runs, while the same suite passes end to end on 38d1a313. That step banks a sampled peak only while state().key is 'rotor', and removing the sheet's overdraw (gpu 11.5 ms to 9.6 ms at the landing) shortens the Rotor's share of the fight before the hand-over. The assertion was NOT weakened to make the change pass.

## 2026-09-16 — The V1 playtest notes: waves that climb into the hundreds, Isao mending the base between waves, and an eased hull steering

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-playtest-difficulty-and-rebuild

The owner playtested the published V1 session and sent three notes: the waves are much too easy ("slow start, then it should start getting hectic... hundreds of low levels, a few more hardcores"), Isao never rebuilds the gate or the walls the swarm breaks, and the tank turns too sharply. A fourth note asked whether the plasma thrower should be gate-locked behind a technology discovery. The session the owner played is a bare index.html.

Three fixes, one finding. (1) DIFFICULTY had two independent causes. readStoryQuery treated a bare index.html as a deep link and handed it the sparse 0.35 threat multiplier meant for inspecting a jumped-to stage, so the owner fought a third of the swarm the sector table asks for: sector 1 opened with two bodies alive and the whole six-sector run peaked at 54 concurrent. Only an explicit ?stage= or ?story= keeps the sparse waves now. Separately the table capped every programme at ladder wave 8, below computeWavePlan's invasion surge - the only thing in the ladder that puts low-belt bodies on the ground in the hundreds - which made late waves SHRINK rather than swell (a sector-3 wave went 90 bodies, then 66, because the headline turns non-rammable and its density rule halves instead of multiplying). The ladder windows now climb through the surge and the hard cores are a small explicit count (SECTORS[n].hardcores) instead of the twenty-odd solid bodies the surge throws in on its own. (2) ISAO REPAIRS: a new pure rule (src/domain/repair-orders.js) queues the gate first, then the blown-out wall cells in rim order, only while no wave is running and never ahead of an order the player placed; it rides his existing travel-and-print loop and he says one line the first time. (3) STEERING: the steer keys wrote the full yaw rate on the frame they went down and zero on the frame they came up - a step function, not a fast tank. src/domain/steer-ease.js ramps the same top rate over an attack constant and releases it over a shorter one, and the hull banks into the turn through the hover rig's existing roll channel. (4) The PLASMA THROWER is ALREADY gated and was left alone: it is the `plasma` tower (src/lore.js:161 names it PLASMA THROWER), unlocked only by bringing the 'coil stack' part home from expedition site rocket-c, itself hidden until three parts are in (src/content/story-defaults.js:100), with the refusal reading PART NOT HOME (src/td-tab.js:6819). The MORK's hold-to-sweep secondary is called 'plasma' in tutorial text but is the twin beam rig, not the thrower.

Alternatives: Raise the sector threat numbers alone and leave the 0.35 bare-page multiplier: rejected, it fights the wrong lever - the content table was already asking for three times what the owner was fighting.; Keep ladderCap at 8 and grow difficulty only through threat: rejected, it cannot reach hundreds of low-belt bodies, because the amoeba/ghost re-flood lives only above ladder wave 8, and it leaves the late-wave shrink in place.; Generator ladderCap 12 with threatStep 0.25: measured, rejected at 718 concurrent in sector 6 - past what the frame budget holds. Cap 11 / step 0.2 tops out at 490.; Make the tank turn more slowly: rejected, the owner asked for smoothing, not a slower hull; the top yaw rate is unchanged at 2.6 rad/s.; Repair the gate passively through mendGate alone: rejected, the owner asked for Isao to go and build, so the trip is the fix and the passive trickle stays as it was.

Evidence:

- Node harness over sectorDef + computeWavePlan, per sector and wave (scratchpad difficulty.mjs). BEFORE at the bare page's real 0.35 multiplier: S1 opens 2 alive, peaks 10; run peak 54 concurrent; 1012 bodies S1-S6. BEFORE at threat 1: run peak 144, and sector 3 fell 90 -> 66 in its last two waves.
- AFTER (bare page now runs at 1): S1 8/16/28/38, S2 38/54/64/72, S3 74/80/254/308/374/374, generated sectors plateau at 490; 9838 bodies S1-S6; every sector's biggest wave beats the last one's.
- Harness cross-validated against the real spawner: it predicted 1464 bodies for sector 3's whole programme, the browser reported 1496 alive.
- Browser, source build, scripts/browser-test.mjs --waves --last through browser-lock.sh: sector 3's biggest single wave measured 406 concurrent at p50 20.9 ms, p95 46 ms, max 72.7 ms over 474 frames.
- Browser, deliberate overload (--waves, whole programme released at once): 1496 concurrent at p50 24.4 ms, p95 53 ms, max 146 ms over 392 frames.
- npm test: 118 test programs passed, including new test/steer-ease.mjs and test/repair-orders.mjs and the reshaped test/sectors.mjs (which now pins the climb: sector 1 opens gently, every sector peaks harder than the last, sector 3 fights in the hundreds, nothing past 520).
- npm run check: architecture guard, line budgets and top-level placement hold; src/td-tab.js unchanged at its 13506 budget (10 insertions, 10 deletions).

## 2026-09-16 — The procedural survey tank is deleted; a hull is MÖRK or it is nothing

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-legacy-tank-removed

Playing the live V1 build the owner saw the wrong hull on load: "Main now loaded the ancient model for the tank. On refresh it swapped to the correct one, but there is still a frame where the old tank loads and is then replaced. REMOVE that model from the UNITS, remove it from everywhere." The relic was not an asset at all, which is why no lock file named it: it was makeTank() in src/units.js, a ~350-triangle Lambert casting with treads, a 3x3 sphere shell rack and two mini-guns, described in the source as the mesh-unit proof of concept. It was still live in four places: UNITS.tank (a roster entry the Units lab listed as 'the procedural tank', with its own codex page LORE.tank, SURVEY TANK, PATTERN A), the synchronous fallback inside UNITS.mork / mork-low / mork-proxy (built with userData.loading while the GLB downloaded and swapped out by applyCreature, which is the frame the owner saw), the swarm lab's opening hull, and buildUnit's default for an unknown name.

makeTank and its 79-line block are gone from src/units.js, together with the UNITS.tank roster entry, the unitcatalog 'tank' choice and the LORE.tank codex page. In its place makePendingHull() returns an EMPTY Group carrying MORK_UNIT (asset 'mork', baseScale, lift) plus loading: true: the unit contract with no body, so every consumer sizes, places and reports it exactly as the hull it stands in for and it draws nothing. The three MÖRK tiers return it until their bytes land; buildUnit's default for an unknown name is now UNITS[DEFAULT_TANK]. The swarm lab stands the placeholder in its lane and swaps MÖRK in when ready instead of building the casting. td-tab's briefing-card icon builds DEFAULT_TANK rather than the literal 'tank'. Nothing non-MÖRK can be drawn any more, because the code that drew it no longer exists. Kept deliberately: the metal lab's SUBJECTS key 'tank' (a ?unit=tank deep-link key whose cast is already buildCreature(DEFAULT_TANK), labelled 'MÖRK (full)'), td-tab's spriteShot cache key 'tank', the 'tank' kill-source, bloom-group and audio-bus names, and glbmodels' makeShellRack / makeHeatSleeve / addGunTubes, which are shape helpers for authored models rather than the relic.

Alternatives: Keep the casting and hide it for a frame: rejected, because a hidden relic is still a relic in the roster, the codex and the Units lab, which is what the owner asked to remove.; Return null until MÖRK is ready: rejected, because playerMesh is dereferenced unconditionally in dozens of td-tab paths (scale, bloom groups, placeActors, feel), so a null hull is a far larger and riskier change than a meshless one.; Delete a model file under assets/: not applicable. The relic was procedural code. No GLB, no docs/*.lock.json entry, no scripts/assets.mjs lock and no ATTRIBUTIONS.md credit ever existed for it, so there was nothing to remove there.

Evidence:

- artifacts/qa/legacy-hull.mjs through scripts/browser-lock.sh: a census of every Object3D subtree entering the scene, taken by patching Object3D.prototype.add on the vendored three module at document start (hook installed 124 ms, first hull 1291 ms). Over the story boot (world=story&stage=7) four hull subtrees entered: three empty placeholders (asset 'mork', loading, 0 meshes) and then MÖRK at 1574 ms (50 meshes, HOVER_RIG, TURRET_YAW, Long_cannon_barrel). No subtree carried userData.ammoDots, the deleted casting's shell rack, and no hull carried a Lambert material. Raw census artifacts/qa/legacy-hull/census-story.json, verdict census-verdict.json, screenshot story-tank-rollout.png.
- The same probe re-ran the Units evidence: units-tier-mork 24196/50, units-tier-mork-low 6742/49, units-tier-mork-proxy 1706/1 with its proxy flag; units-dart-quiver and units-dart-heptapod firing MISSILE_MOTION; ?unit=tank selecting nothing; entriesIn('friendly') carrying no 'tank'.
- node scripts/browser-test.mjs --defense green (11 steps). --story-world reached story-world-rotor-kill green and then failed on the Rotor heat-peak step (window.__rotorHeatMax 0.037 against > 0.05): a timing-sensitive step the test itself documents as racing the Quiver hand-over, and recorded as flaky on main. Nothing in this change touches tower heat or the sentry pilot; it was NOT re-run, because the session's browser-run budget was spent.
- npm test (120 test programs), npm run check (architecture guard included) and npm run assets:check green.
- src/td-tab.js 13506 -> 13505 lines; docs/architecture-budget.json lowered to match. topLevelModules unchanged: no top-level module was deleted.

issue · observed · 2026-09-16-laser-lab-step-fails-before-sol82

While bringing SOL-82 into the game, `node scripts/browser-test.mjs --laser` stopped at "the beam cut the wall (0)" after the burn step. The move of the lab's scope and inset HUD into src/fx was a suspect.

Not caused by the SOL-82 work: the same step fails identically on a tree without it (worktree at 0001882, which carries the lab exactly as on main plus pure sector files): PASS laser-lab-load, laser-lab-overhead, laser-lab-burn, then AssertionError the beam cut the wall (0). The likely cause is the lab feel commits of 2026-09-15 (beam inertia with LASER_BEAM slew 10 m/s and accel 5, the fleeing swarm), which were never browser-checked: the step's drag and dwell across the gate mouth were timed for the old constant 40 m/s chase. To do: re-time the step's nudge-and-hold for the inertial beam (or steer with the lab's pad until the contact settles on a wall cell) and confirm a wall cell still needs LASER_BURN.wall seconds.

Alternatives: Blame the scope move and revert it; rejected because the failure reproduces without it.

Evidence:

- scripts/browser-test.mjs --laser run in .claude/worktrees/agent-a18f0d804159bc367 (0001882): exit 1 at the wall-cut assertion.
- The SOL-82 branch reported the same failure; its own --laser-game step passes (19 bodies, a breach sealed, a rock cell broken in 0.7 ms, two wall segments burned).

## 2026-09-16 — Isao works the AFR-01 with his print beam before he prints the gate

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-isao-works-the-foundry-built

Owner, playtesting the live V1 on 2026-09-16: 'before building the gates (nice, works), Isao should use his beam to work on the recycle factory we first see.' The proposal 2026-09-16-isao-tends-the-foundry left the design open: presentational or barrel-driven, how long he stays, and the hard constraint that the gate step is what the tremor waits for, so the first wave pays for every second of the beat.

Built as a build-programme beat that prints nothing. A step may now carry `over: <structure id>` and a `plot` (half extents in metres): src/fx/base-print.js gives such a step the machine's own footprint as its bed instead of the island it shares, and holds the bed at `metres` from the first frame instead of climbing with the print, so the beam rasters printpath's three patterns across the AFR-01's 16 x 12 m deck at its working height rather than climbing out of the dirt. The step is src/content/base-programme.js `{ id: 'foundry', over: 'foundry', plot: [8, 6], when: { phase: 'rotor-ready' }, seconds: 8, metres: 6 }`, first in the programme; the domain rule (src/domain/build-programme.js) is untouched and every number stays in content. One line, build_foundry: 'AFR-01 is eating our rocket on its own. / Working the cut with the beam. Barrels first, then your gate.' src/td-tab.js needed one line changed (net 0 against the 13,505 budget): the status row reads 'working' for an `over` order and 'printing' for everything else. The foundry's cut is NOT driven by the beat: its own 24 s clock (arc at 2 s, scrap at 10 s, barrel at 15 s) keeps running under him, and the beat is timed in content instead. Tying the beat's end to a barrel was rejected on the timing budget: a window that is guaranteed to contain a barrel is a whole 24 s cycle, and the beat already costs the first wave 14.5 s.

Alternatives: Gate the beat on the foundry being mid-cycle (a `cycling` flag into due()) so his work ends as a barrel lands. Rejected: guaranteeing a barrel inside the window needs the full 24 s cycle, three times the timing budget the tremor allows, and a never-satisfied gate would stall the whole programme once the foundry is spent.; Put the beat before `rotor-ready` so it costs the gate nothing. Rejected: there is one drone and one FIFO, so a claimed beat queues the beats' own Rotor order behind it and would delay the Rotor print and the optic hand-over — exactly what the task said not to fight.; Give the beat the landing island as its plot, like every other step. Rejected: the island is 16 x 16 m of ground around the machine, so the raster would have fallen on the dirt beside the AFR-01 rather than on it.

Evidence:

- Two traps found in composition, both silent: src/platform/story-world.js filtered BASE_PROGRAMME to steps whose PIECES the plan holds, so a beat that prints nothing was dropped from the programme entirely; and `standing` (no pending piece) would have marked it done from the first frame. Split into `needs` (pieces plus the machine an `over` beat works) for the filter and `standing` (pieces only, else !grow) for the done set, so the beat is owed on a growing base and already past on a static stage.
- test/build-programme.mjs extended: the beat is first, builds nothing, carries no perk, has a cell to fly to (a -1 would stall the programme), its bed sits exactly on the machine's x/z, its height is constant at `metres` at k=0 and k=1, the raster stays inside the plot, and progress()/finish() grow nothing. npm test: 120 test programs passed. npm run check: architecture, line budgets and the frozen module list hold; src/td-tab.js 13,505 lines, unchanged.
- Browser --grow through scripts/browser-lock.sh, baseline on d9da03e7 then after 747e85a0, both bare-page stage=1&grow=1, both exit 0 with every step PASS. Seconds from page load. Rotor printed 26.0 -> 25.9; foundry beat begins - -> 25.9; foundry beat ends - -> 38.1; gate print begins 26.0 -> 38.1 (+12.1); gate stands 38.7 -> 53.1 (+14.4); tremor 40.1 -> 54.6 (+14.5); landing pad 48.3 -> 62.3; override 72.9 -> 87.4; first wave cleared 79.4 -> 93.9; Quiver optic 84.8 -> 99.8 (+15.0); settled/handover 99.8 -> 114.7; study 112.4 -> 127.2; expedition 112.6 -> 127.5; solar 134.8 -> 149.3; bays 156.4 -> 171.2; HUGIN 176.3 -> 191.0; radar and assembly 217.9 -> 232.9 (+15.0). The offset is a constant ~14.5 s and does not compound: the whole opening is shifted, not slowed. Of it, 8 s is the beat and ~6.5 s is the flight out to the landing island and back to the gate.
- Not browser-verified: the status-row wording (52331bb8) was changed after that run's page had already loaded src/td-tab.js, so the screenshot shows the old 'ISAO printing seed foundry 12%'. The change is a string ternary on a line node --check, npm test and npm run check all pass on, and the line budget is unchanged, but the new 'working seed foundry' text has not been seen in a browser.
- The Rotor beat is untouched: the Rotor still prints at 25.9 s (26.0 s baseline) because the beat's `when` is `rotor-ready`, and the Quiver optic hand-over moves only by the same constant shift.
- Screenshot artifacts/browser/grow-foundry-beam.png, 5 s into the beat: Isao hovers over the AFR-01 with the beam laid on the machine's deck, the SH02 already swapped for its salvage sections, a barrel on the rack beside it, and the status row reading his line. --grow asserts at that moment that print.step is 'foundry', 0 < k < 1, printed is empty and the gate is not built.
- --grow re-run after the change: every step PASS, including grow-foundry-beam, and the final order is ['foundry','gate','landing','stalheart','solar','bays','hugin','radar','assembly'] with all seven perks and the gate's wall count assertion untouched.

Supersedes: 2026-09-16-isao-tends-the-foundry

## 2026-09-16 — Isao works the recycling foundry with his beam before he prints the gate

decision · proposed · 2026-09-16-isao-tends-the-foundry

Owner, playtesting the live V1 on 2026-09-16: 'before building the gates (nice, works), Isao should use his beam to work on the recycle factory we first see.' Today the AFR-01 seed foundry cuts the SH02 into feedstock on its own clock (src/content/foundry.js, src/domain/foundry.js, src/fx/foundry-fx.js) and Isao never touches it; his print beam only appears over a tower socket or a base-programme step (src/printpath.js, src/fx/base-print.js, the build programme in src/content/base-programme.js).

Proposed, not yet designed: a build-programme step before the gate that sends Isao to the foundry and holds him there working it with the print beam, so the first thing the player watches him do is tend the machine that is eating their rocket. Open questions: whether the beat is purely presentational or actually drives a barrel (tying his work to the foundry's 24 s cut would make the economy legible), how long he stays before the gate step takes over (the gate step now starts at 39.2 s and the tremor waits for the gate, so the beat must not push the first wave late), whether he returns between barrels, and which of his emotion faces plays. The step needs no structure reveal: the foundry already stands from the arrival beat.

Alternatives: Leave the foundry autonomous and spend the beat elsewhere; rejected by the owner's note, which wants his first visible work to be on the recycler.; Animate the foundry harder instead of sending Isao; keeps the beat but loses the character the owner asked for.

Evidence:

- Owner note, 2026-09-16 playtest of build 5e204f48.
- Build programme order today: gate + 12 walls at rotor-ready, then the landing pad (src/content/base-programme.js); the gate step begins at 39.2 s on a bare page (--grow run, 2026-09-16).

## 2026-09-16 — The gunship's third weapon fires a real missile that drops, ignites after two seconds and rides down

decision · proposed · 2026-09-16-gunship-mini-nuke-drops-then-ignites

Owner, playtesting the live build on 2026-09-16: 'the Gunship should fire an actual missile, one of our large ones, we see it drop, then it ignites after 2 seconds and heads down (for Weapon #3 mini nuke)'. Today the gunship's third gun is the 105 (src/domain/gunship.js, src/content/gunship.js), which lands as a strike without a modelled round in flight; the game already owns a drop-then-ignite flight profile for the Quiver's TALON (src/domain/missile-flight.js: eject, coast, fall, ignite, climb) and modelled missiles in src/missiles.js / src/missile-presentation.js.

Proposed, not yet designed: weapon 3 becomes a modelled mini-nuke released from the gunship, falling free for about two seconds before its motor lights and drives it down onto the aim point, with the seat watching it go. Open: which of the pinned large missiles is the body; whether the existing missile-flight phases can be reused inverted (a drop from altitude rather than a pop-up) or need their own profile; what the 2 s free-fall does to the 105's current timing, damage and danger-close warning; whether the seeker/impact view follows the round down; and whether the yield deserves its own blast and scare numbers rather than the 105's.

Alternatives: Keep the 105 as an instant strike and only add a falling shell effect; cheaper but the owner asked to see a real missile leave the aircraft.; Reuse the orbital strike's falling camera wholesale; it frames a warhead from above rather than from the gunship's belly.

Evidence:

- Owner note, 2026-09-16, on build 5e204f48.
- Existing drop-then-ignite phases: src/domain/missile-flight.js (the Quiver's TALON pop-out was capped at 8 m on 2026-09-16 for framing).
- Gunship guns and their cadence: src/content/gunship.js, src/domain/gunship.js.

## 2026-09-16 — The gunship's third weapon is the MK-9: a modelled TALON released from the belly that falls two seconds, ignites and dives onto the painted cell

change · accepted · 2026-09-16-gunship-mini-nuke-built

Owner, playtesting the live build on 2026-09-16: 'the Gunship should fire an actual missile, one of our large ones. We see it drop, then it ignites after 2 seconds and heads down (for Weapon #3, mini nuke).' Weapon 3 was the 105: GUNSHIP_GUNS.heavy with strike:true, a tracer line drawn from the muzzle to the ground over travel 3.5 s, then executeStrike at the orbital strike's own blastCells and the howitzer-blast module. Nothing modelled left the aircraft. The drop-then-ignite shape already existed for the Quiver's TALON in src/domain/missile-flight.js (eject/coast/fall/ignite/climb, its pop-out capped at OPENING_METRES = 8 on the same day).

Weapon 3 is the MK-9 mini nuke, a pooled TALON body in the world. THE BODY: the missile kit's large mesh (1,340 triangles against the DART's 188, authored for 'single special rounds / close views'), already pinned and already pooled for the Quiver's heavy shot, so no asset was added; four metres nose to tail (GUNSHIP_NUKE.length). THE FLIGHT extends src/domain/missile-flight.js rather than adding a second model: dropFrame/sampleDrop/DROP_PHASES reuse missileFrame's frame (forward/right/metre) and the sampler's { position, direction, ignition, phase } contract, which is what src/missile-presentation.js poses a pooled round from and what drives the kit's exhaust; only the curve is replaced, inverted. The authored opening is a stylised local-unit parabola, but a round let go 340 m up must fall real metres for a real two seconds, so the unpowered leg is ballistics (p = from + v0.t - up.g.t^2/2) and the powered leg a cubic Hermite entered at the fall's own velocity, so nothing kinks at ignition, and arriving straight down at arrivalLead x the distance left, so speed climbs into impact. The clock is SECONDS, not a normalised u: the free fall does not stretch with range. Every number is a parameter and src/content/gunship.js owns them: freeFall 2, drive 1.8 (travel 3.8 s), gravity 9.81, ejectSpeed 6 m/s down, arrivalLead 1.5, up passed by the caller so down is toward the planet's centre. THE NUMBERS CHANGED FOR THE 105: label 105MM -> MK-9; blastCells and dangerCells 3.2 -> 5.5 (55 m, against the 105's 32 m); travel 3.5 -> 3.8, now composed of the free fall and the drive rather than authored; reload 14 -> 20 and a new perPass 1, so the heaviest tool the seat owns is one release a station pass (src/domain/gunship.js heavySpent/heavyPass, phase 'spent'); heavyState's 'falling' split into 'released' (motor cold) and 'ignited'; the blast is EXPLOSION_USES['gunship.nuke'] (orbital-strike at 0.8, ~72 m and 10 s, against the 105's 45 m) with EXPLOSION_SCARE 10 cells / 4 s against 6 / 2.5, and executeStrike takes an explicit blastCells so the nuke's radius is its own rather than the strike tune's. EXPLOSION_USES['gunship.heavy'] is kept but no longer reachable from the gun. FRIENDLY DAMAGE IS NOT MADE SAFE: the 55 m radius breaks our own walls and sentries exactly as the strike does (strikeTune.breakWalls/breakTowers unchanged), and the warning is moved forward - from the moment a cell is painted and all the way down while the round falls, the optic rings stand on the round's cell rather than the live aim, the HUD 'In blast' reports that cell, and a red DANGER CLOSE banner over the reticle names the walls, sentries, tank or Isao standing in it. THE SEAT: src/fx/gunship-drop.js owns the presentation only (release, tick, pool) while src/domain/gunship.js stays the single clock for the impact, so the body and the damage cannot drift; the GROUND TRUTH feed rides the round (a new feedLabel/feedBack/feedLift on the mesh lets it ask for a camera 13 m above itself looking down, because the seeker's behind-the-round framing puts the sky behind a falling body) and then frames the blast; the HUD reads ARMED / PAINTED / RELEASED / IGNITED / IMPACT / SPENT.

Alternatives: A second flight model for the drop, separate from missile-flight.js: rejected - the phase vocabulary, the frame and the pose contract are the same, and a second model would need its own pooling and exhaust rules.; Keeping the authored local-unit parabola for the unpowered leg: rejected - it scales with range, so a two-second fall from 340 m would not be two seconds of real fall, which is exactly the number the owner gave.; Re-timing the powered Hermite for acceleration the way the pop-up's course() does: rejected - re-timing breaks the velocity match at ignition; the arrival tangent (arrivalLead) gives the same acceleration with no kink.; Letting the round keep only the aircraft's velocity: tried and seen to fail in the first browser strip - it drifted out of the sight within a second. It is ejected six metres a second straight down now, so from the seat it drops away down the sight.; Reusing the 105's howitzer-blast for the yield: rejected - the owner asked for a mini nuke, and the module list already pins a nuclear-size explosion.

Evidence:

- Owner note, 2026-09-16, playtesting the live build.
- npm test (120 test programs passed) and npm run check green on each commit.
- test/missile-flight.mjs: the drop profile's free fall is unpowered ballistics for exactly freeFall seconds, the motor is cold until then and lit after, no jump at the catch, unit nose the whole way, nose-down arrival, exact arrival on the point, metre and up both scale it.
- test/gunship.mjs: ready -> painted -> released -> ignited -> impact -> reloading -> spent, one release a pass, and a fresh round on the next pass.
- test/explosions-content.mjs: gunship.nuke out-yields gunship.heavy by more than 1.4x, stays under strike.orbital, and scatters the swarm wider and longer.
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --gunship: PASS, with the five-frame strip artifacts/browser/gunship-nuke-{release,freefall,ignite,dive,blast}.png read by eye. The first run showed a black GROUND TRUTH box at release (the feed rode behind a round whose nose was horizontal, so the sky was the backdrop) and the round sliding out of the sight; both fixed and re-run green.
- scripts/browser-lock.sh node scripts/browser-test.mjs --defense: PASS.
- src/td-tab.js unchanged in line count (13,505, its budget): the wiring went into existing lines and the presentation into the new src/fx/gunship-drop.js.

## 2026-09-15 — Tank-shell breaches patch the board surface in place; SOL-82 is the orbital laser's name and asset brief

decision · accepted · 2026-09-15-board-surface-patch-and-sol-82-brief

Owner, 2026-09-15: breaking rock with a tank shell hitches for about half a second, and it gets far worse when the orbital laser takes several rocks in a row; the laser lab's patchable planet surface (357d98b) should be ported to the game's tank shells. The owner also named the orbital laser SOL-82, a homage to SOLD 740 and 1982, and asked for a short brief for the 3D asset designer.

8911781: src/fx/board-surface.js holds td-tab's four board buffers in fixed per-cell, per-edge and per-corner slots. buildGeometry builds through it, breachWallCell queues breached cells, and rebuildAfterBreach re-lays the route field and patches only those cells. Per-cell colour jitter is a hash of (seed, cell), so a patched board equals a fresh build; the jitter pattern shifts once relative to the old running sequence. td-tab.js drops 99 lines and its architecture budget follows. d31a739: docs/SOL-82-BRIEF.md is the asset brief for the SOL-82 platform (capabilities from src/content/orbital-laser.js, the export contract, nodes, materials, clips, what the game draws itself).

Alternatives: Keep the sequential jitter and accept that a patched cell's colour differs slightly from a rebuild; rejected because byte-for-byte equality with a fresh build is the test that proves the patch.; A per-cell height texture sampled in the vertex shader; not chosen because the fixed slots keep td-tab's Lambert materials, gameBreaches' material patch, raycasts and paintCell unchanged.; Make the route field incremental too; unnecessary: the BFS measured about 2 ms on the 71k-cell story planet, and the hitch was the geometry.

Evidence:

- test/board-surface.mjs on the baked story planet: three breached cells (a rim rock, an interior rock and its neighbour) patched in place equal a fresh build in every attribute of all four buffers, in black and bright modes; build 136 ms / 78 ms, patch 0.199 ms / 0.147 ms.
- bfsDist over the story planet's 71,314 cells: 2.07 ms mean over 10 runs.
- npm test (107 test programs) and npm run check (architecture budgets hold; td-tab.js 13,858 lines) green on 8911781.
- NOT browser-verified: the memory guardrail stayed at WARN (3.07 GB swap), so no --story-world or --defense run covered td-tab's live breach path after the change.

## 2026-09-15 — Names: floor, rock, wall, gate; a planet surface that a breach patches instead of rebuilding

decision · accepted · 2026-09-15-floor-rock-wall-gate-and-patchable-surface

Owner, 2026-09-15: agree the names for the planet's open ground, its raised lattice, the man-made wall segments and the gate. Rock that tank shells can break should also break under the orbital laser. Breaking rock causes a jarring ~0.5 s hitch, which shows on a single tank shell and badly when the laser takes several rocks in a row, as if something is redrawn. Try hiding the lines on the rock tops for a more uniform look. The beam also shows a straight dividing line, especially on rock.

Names, agreed by the owner: FLOOR is open ground (PATH cells); ROCK is the planet's own raised lattice (BLOCKED cells, the planet mesh's rock layer); WALL is the man-made segments either side of the gate (plan.walls, the kit wall); GATE is the armoured opening (plan.gate). The laser lab's labels and LASER_BURN's comments use them; td-tab.js still says 'wall cell' for rock and is not renamed in this change. b563a74 made rock cells burn under the laser as a tank shell breaks them (structure and socket cells anchored), with LASER_BURN.rock 0.5. 357d98b adds createStoryPlanetSurface: fixed per-cell and per-edge slots, so refreshCells() patches a breached cell and its four edges by addUpdateRange and nothing is rebuilt. rockLines is 'all', 'rim' (only where rock meets floor) or 'none', and the lab defaults to rim. The lab's beam, glow and smoke now land on the real ground height (terrace floor, or rock top) instead of 4 m inside a rock.

Alternatives: Keep rebuilding the whole planet mesh and throttle it to one rebuild per 0.3 s (the first cut in b563a74); rejected because every rebuild is still a ~150-200 ms hitch, only rarer.; A cell-height data texture sampled in the vertex shader, so a breach writes one texel; not chosen now because the fixed-slot buffers keep the plain MeshBasicMaterial and vertex colours and patch in microseconds, at the cost of drawing collapsed slots.; Port the patch into td-tab.js's buildGeometry for the game's tank shells in the same change; deferred because td-tab.js is outside this change's files and carries the architecture line budget. It is the follow-up that removes the game's hitch.

Evidence:

- Node benchmark on the baked story planet (71,314 cells): buildStoryPlanetMesh 201 ms; createStoryPlanetSurface 46 ms to build; refreshCells on one cell 0.002 ms. Opening a rock cell ringed by rock added exactly 4 rock sides and 4 rim lines, and closing it again restored the side buffer byte for byte.
- npm test green (106 test programs passed) and npm run check green on 357d98b.
- Not browser-verified: the memory guardrail stayed at WARN. The straight line on the beam is diagnosed from reading (the flat beam ribbon placed at floor height inside a 4 m rock) because the owner's two photos in ~/Downloads could not be opened here.
