decision · accepted · 2026-09-15-laser-destroy-ignite-kept

The branch review of the laser lab (517cbcb..3289bb2) found that destroy() fires laser.ignite (about 16 m, with an 8-cell scare) for every wall cell, structure and the seal, which the lab spec did not ask for, and that spec §3's pointer reticle in the satellite inset was never drawn. Alternatives: Drop the ignite on destruction to keep to the spec's letter; rejected because it removes an explosion the owner has since asked to have more of.; Record the reticle as deferred; rejected because the aim point and the lagging contact are indistinguishable without it now that the contact has inertia (8503f3c).

Evidence:

## 2026-09-15 — Laser lab feel from the owner's notes: live look, full-width landing, embers and smoke, a slow inexorable drag, the owner's view

change · accepted · 2026-09-15-laser-lab-feel-owner-notes

Owner notes on labs.html#laser, 2026-09-15: the lab loads slowly; an infinite laser is needed to judge the look without resetting; the look sliders (core/glow width, intensity) change nothing and the beam looks tiny; walls do not break even at 0 s; the beam meets the ground as a point instead of at its own width; more explosion and smoke where it hits; the movement should feel slow and inexorable, not hectic; the black burn is too jagged and should feel like burning embers with continuous smoke along the path; the view defaults should be altitude 4, satellite fov 20, inset 0.44, ground camera back 120 and up 60.

064d911: src/main.js awaits the planet bake for #laser as the story routes do, so the lab stops relaxing and carving the planet live (buildStoryPlanet was 6.2 s of a 9.3 s load in a CPU profile). createOrbitalLaser gains tune(look) and look(): the look sliders and the footprint radius write the column's uniforms and the ring scale live, where before createOrbitalLaser read the frozen LASER_PRESET and the sliders only fed COPY PRESET; a core intensity slider joins them. The panel gains an infinite-laser toggle (the pass held open with a full budget, carried as ?infinite=1 so RESET keeps it) and REVIVE TARGETS (burned walls from the matrices saved at build, structures, bodies and counters, without a reload; a sealed sinkhole still needs RESET). 8503f3c: LASER_PRESET.capEnd 0.02 -> 0.001, the column ends 2 m under the contact and a contact glow disc of the column's own width marks the landing; laser.contact 0.6 -> 1.3 and a new laser.smoke use (bofors-burst 0.55, with a scare) at LASER_SMOKE_RATE 2 per second; aimLaser gains inertia under beam.accel (speed ramps toward slew, brakes on sqrt(2 a d), a turn bleeds speed, a reversal starts from rest; accel 0 is the old constant-rate chase) with LASER_BEAM slew 40 -> 10 and accel 5 and panel sliders for both; the HUD names what stands under the beam while it burns. d279faa: the scorch becomes soft ember stamps every 0.8 m (char, a glow cooling over LASER_TRAIL.hot 4 s, smouldering coals, restamped every 0.4 s while still); LASER_TRAIL_SMOKE lays smoke along the path on the pinned explosion kit's puff layer as a 256-instance ring buffer; LASER_VIEW becomes altitude 4, fov 20, inset 0.44, groundBack 120, groundUp 60.

Alternatives: Fix the pinpoint landing in src/beamfx.js's cap shader; rejected because the board and shot beams share it, and the laser only needs its own capEnd and a buried end.; More ground smoke by raising EXPLOSION_CAPS.medium so laser.smoke bursts could fire faster; rejected because the gunship's Bofors shares that cap. The continuous smoke is the laser's own ring buffer instead.; Slow the drag by lowering slew alone; rejected because a constant-rate chase still starts and stops instantly. Inertia with braking is what reads as inexorable.; Make the planet's lattice rock walls burnable; not done: the lab's destruction covers the kit wall cells, the structures, the bodies and the sinkhole, and whether the owner meant the rock walls is an open question.

Evidence:

- CPU profile of labs.html?acceptance=1#laser before the fix: 9.3 s to ready, buildStoryPlanet 6.2 s (grid relax 3.5 s, generateDungeon 2.0 s).
- test/orbital-laser.mjs: the constant-rate chase still moves exactly slew * dt with accel 0; with slew 10 and accel 5 the contact moves 0.05 m in its first 0.1 s from rest, never moves away from its target, reaches and holds 10 m/s, brakes onto the target and arrives, and a reversal drops it to accel * dt; the close resets the speed. Pins follow LASER_BEAM, LASER_VIEW, LASER_TRAIL, LASER_TRAIL_SMOKE and LASER_SMOKE_RATE; test/explosions-content.mjs pins the nine uses.
- npm test green (106 test programs passed) and npm run check green after each commit.
- NOT browser-verified: the memory guardrail stayed at WARN (3.3 GB swap) through these commits, so no headless run was made. The owner is judging them on the running 8155 server.
- Walls at 0 s: the domain reports a 0 s wall on its first frame and the lab zeroes the instance in every walls InstancedMesh; the Task 7 browser round cut five wall cells at 0.5 s. The HUD readout 'under the beam' now shows whether a wall cell is in the footprint at all.

## 2026-09-15 — The orbital laser's rules and its lab: a timed satellite beam burning the real base from a satellite inset

change · accepted · 2026-09-15-orbital-laser-lab-landed

Owner, 2026-09-15 (2026-09-15-orbital-laser-brainstorm, 2026-09-15-orbital-laser-lab-design): the orbital laser is an add-on to the gunship on a timer; the satellite view must feel high; the ground reality of the destruction is full screen with the satellite far view as a large inset where the pointer steers; the Stalheart can be destroyed if the beam is misused; build the lab first, with real map elements and a line of enemies in a trench. Sub-project 1 is the rules, the look and the lab only.

src/content/orbital-laser.js carries the first values (period 180 / window 20 s, 10 s of energy, a 6 m footprint, 40 m/s of slew, the burn seconds per kind, the view and the beam preset). src/domain/orbital-laser.js is pure and Node-tested (test/orbital-laser.mjs): makeLaser, stepLaser, aimLaser (a rotation along the sphere capped at slew * dt metres, snapping on the first aim of a pass), burnLaser, burnContacts (per-id seconds, reported once, forgotten when a thing leaves the footprint) and laserProgress. src/fx/orbital-laser.js builds the column through src/beamfx.js, the footprint ring and a 400-quad scorch ribbon fading over 60 s. labs.html#laser (src/labs/laser-tab.js) stands up the shipped story base at stage 6, two sentries on their story sockets, an open sinkhole down the sightline and a trench of twenty bodies on the lab's own integrator, renders the ground view full screen with a scissored satellite inset (a band across the top in portrait), steers only inside the inset through a screen-to-sphere pick, and destroys by dropping wall instances, hiding structure holders, sealing the sinkhole with rubble and ending the colony on the Stalheart. laser.ignite and laser.contact joined EXPLOSION_USES and EXPLOSION_SCARE so the existing scare herds the queue. A --laser step in scripts/browser-test.mjs opens the lab, jumps the clock, drags the beam up the trench and across the gate mouth, then walks it back to the hole and seals it, with four screenshots for the owner. The first browser round settled five things reading alone could not: the sinkhole is hosted at cell scale the way src/game-breaches.js does it (group scaled by cellSide, planetRadius R / cellSide) so craterRadius 1 is a ten-metre hole under a matching cap; the ground camera's back-along-the-trench direction is projected onto the tangent plane at the point it frames, because one direction taken at the trench's mouth dips nineteen degrees under the surface 250 m away and walked the camera into the ground; the inset moved to the bottom left, because the lil-gui panel owns the right edge and the middle is where the contact is held; LASER_PRESET.capEnd fell from 0.985 to 0.02, since both caps are fractions of the beam's LENGTH and 0.985 tapered a 400 m column into an invisible needle; and laser.ignite's explosion fell from half an orbital strike (45 m) to 0.18 (about 16 m), which is twice the footprint instead of wide enough to swallow the ground camera.

Alternatives: Wire the weapon straight into the story (the strip button, the call-in window and the acceptance step) without a lab first; rejected because the owner asked to judge the look and the destruction on real map elements before it reaches the game.; Draw the satellite view into a second renderer or a render target rather than scissoring one scene; rejected because src/fx/story-monitor.js already proves the one-renderer scissor on this scene and a second context is a second GPU budget on a 16 GB machine.; Give the lab its own flat ground and stand-in props; rejected because the spec's whole point is that the walls, the Stalheart, the foundry and the structures are the shipped ones.; Keep the satellite inset in the bottom-right corner the way the other labs put their furniture; rejected after the browser round, where it sat first under the lil-gui panel and then, moved clear of it, straight over the contact the ground camera holds at screen centre.; Aim the browser step at fixed inset coordinates the way the plan's draft did; rejected because the inset re-centres on the contact every frame, so a fixed steer is a constant drag rather than a destination. The step reads state().contact and aims at world points instead.

Evidence:

- test/orbital-laser.mjs: the clock arrives and closes, unused energy is lost, the contact moves exactly slew * dt metres of arc, soft dies at once and a wall cuts through after 0.5 s, an id that leaves the footprint is forgotten.
- test/explosions-content.mjs pins the eight explosion uses; test/impact-scare.mjs already required the scare table to match, so laser.ignite and laser.contact carry their scare.
- npm test green (106 test programs passed) and npm run check green (69 pure-layer modules; game/lab controller boundaries, line budgets and top-level placement hold); npm run build wrote 410 files.
- node scripts/browser-test.mjs --laser: PASS laser-lab-load, laser-lab-overhead, laser-lab-burn and laser-lab-sealed, with no load errors, twenty bodies burned, five wall cells cut, forty-six scorch quads laid, the sinkhole sealed and the Stalheart INTACT; artifacts/browser/laser-lab-burn.png and laser-lab-sealed.png are for the owner.
- node scripts/browser-test.mjs (default suite) and --dist both green on this tree. --nav fails on its DEV/Tuning/bloom assertion, but it fails identically on the clean commit before this one: it is a standing branch failure, not this change.
- A wall cell needs 0.5 s of contact and a 40 m/s drag gives a 6 m footprint only 0.24 s over one, so the browser step nudges and then stands still across the gate mouth; that is what a hand has to do too, and it is the first measured statement about how the weapon plays.
- The story world puts the pole at the origin (src/domain/base-plan.js:12) while createExplosions (planetRadius: Math.hypot(point)) and createBreachRubble (.normalize()) assume a centre-at-origin scene; the lab parents both to one sphereRoot group at (0, -radius, 0) and hands them planet-centred points.

## 2026-09-15 — The --nav failure at the bloom tuning button was caused by this branch's tenth Workshop lab, not a standing break

issue · resolved · 2026-09-15-nav-suite-tenth-lab-bloom-button

2026-09-15-orbital-laser-lab-landed recorded --nav failing at 'DEV · Tuning · bloom opens the variables' as a standing branch failure because it also failed on the commit before. The investigation (.superpowers/sdd/nav-investigation.md, read-only, no browser run under the memory guardrail) dated the break from artifacts/browser/: --nav passed at f2d7d78 with nine labs and first failed after 1ef4366 added the orbital laser lab as the tenth Workshop entry.

The DEV drawer (#shell-nav) is clipped at calc(100vh - 70px); with ten Workshop buttons the sixth tuning button, bloom, sits about one pixel past the clip at the suite's 1440x900, so the raw-coordinate click landed on the canvas and the drawer's outside-click closer shut it. 3289bb2 changes that step from click to the suite's own tap() helper, which scrolls the target into view first, as every drawer step below bloom already did. Corrects the earlier entry's causation; the run itself waits for the memory guardrail.

Alternatives: Fold scrollIntoView into click() itself and retire tap(): the durable fix, not taken as the minimal one.; Drop the bloom assertion: rejected, it covers the tuning panel.

Evidence:

- artifacts/browser timestamps: nav-tuning and later screenshots at 08:19 JST before 1ef4366; nav-switch-failure.json at 12:40 JST after it, error 'DEV · Tuning · bloom opens the variables', no console errors.
- styles.css:3919 (max-height calc(100vh - 70px)), :3921/:3925 (50 px button pitch); scripts/browser-test.mjs:97 (raw-centre click), :276 (tap), src/fx/shell-nav.js:134-137 (outside-click closer).

## 2026-09-15 — Idea: the Mortar can lay suppressing fire on a chosen area to scare and herd the swarm

decision · proposed · 2026-09-15-mortar-suppressing-fire-idea

Owner, 2026-09-15, while brainstorming the orbital laser: the impact scare (src/domain/impact-scare.js, confirmed in play: small rounds herd the swarm) suggests a deliberate use. The Mortar today picks its own targets on the normal wave ladder.

Proposed, logic to do: the player selects an area or bottleneck for the Mortar to aim at; the Mortar walks rounds onto it as suppressing fire, and the impact scare of each round (EXPLOSION_SCARE) does the herding, turning bodies away from the barrage and toward the exits the player wants them on. Open: how the area is chosen (a ring on the map, a marked cell, a lane), whether suppression costs the Mortar its kill fire, how long a barrage lasts, and how it reads on the HUD.

Alternatives: Leave the Mortar automatic and rely on incidental scare from its splash.; Give the suppressing role to the gunship's Bofors instead, which already herds in the seat.

Evidence:

- Owner, 2026-09-15: for the mortar, add a possibility to create suppressing fire, selecting an area or bottleneck to aim at, to scare and herd the enemies; the logic is to do.
- 2026-09-14-gunship-seat-tuning-scare-thermal-sealed-sinkholes: impacts scare nearby bodies and the owner confirmed small rounds herd the swarm.

## 2026-09-14 — Direction: leave the spherical Stålberg grid and go back to a flat world; the sphere may seed a spin-off

decision · proposed · 2026-09-14-flat-world-direction

Owner, after the PoC cleanup and the concept audit (2026-09-14-concept-audit-first-pass, which found the sphere interesting but paying emotionally only in a few scale moments while costing everywhere): leaning towards a major reform and simplification; abandon the spherical Stålberg grid as a PoC that is interesting but not fun enough to justify its complexity; go back to flat for this project; the sphere might be used for a spin-off. The full FunMap audit is to be reviewed together first.

Recorded as the proposed direction, not yet executed. Sequence agreed: commit the cleanup, rework navigation (player and development), review the whole FunMap audit with the owner, then plan the flat reform. The navigation rework is kept shell-level (menus, workshop, roadmap and FunMap views) so it survives the change of world. Open questions for the plan: what the flat world is (grid shape, scale, whether the Stålberg organic quad grid survives on a plane), what carries over unchanged (sentries, tank, gunship, Isao, foundry, breaches, FX packages, labs), what the sphere-arc contracts become (ground distances, story planet bake, far tiers, horizon), and where the spherical kernel goes for a spin-off.

Alternatives: Keep the sphere and simplify around it: the audit's finding was that its cost is everywhere and its payoff local.

Evidence:

- docs/FUNMAP.md reflection: 'The sphere is interesting. It is not yet fun.'

## 2026-09-14 — PoC remnants removed: missions, mines, hacks, the relay, the classic entry and tutorial, cine and portals, MK-CX and old props, Astro, Sniper and Sentry Control practice

decision · accepted · 2026-09-14-poc-remnants-removed

Owner: since the game was forked, anything lost can be recovered from history; clean out what we have outlived, part clean-up and part refactor, a stronger base for early days. Owner's choices: cut rescue, raid and mines (mines may return), the hacking minigames, legacy cinematics and portals, the MK-CX tanks and old props, the classic campaign entry, the Astro diorama (crew to return as lower-poly exports), the Sniper bench and Sentry Control practice; keep the wave simulator; delete stale docs. Three read-only maps were made first, classifying each target per the remove-dead-code skill.

On branch poc-cleanup. td-tab.js 17,557 -> 13,979 lines (budget lowered). Deleted modules: rescue, mines, stargate, portalfx, cine-tab and cine scenes (rail moved to core/rail.js, the weathered material to fx/weathered-material.js), astro-tab and its labs, sniper-tab with ballistics/sniper-scale/mortar-ground/mortar-map/shot-trace/sniper-environment and the ballistics facade, domain/pilot-posts, domain/yard-route, wormhole and corona shaders, impact-tab, two unused sinkhole helpers; minigames/hdt and minigames/pzk; 15 models (mkcx x2, server, portalring, astronaut x2, dish70 and eight Astro-only landmarks) with their lock and inventory entries; 13 test programs and 71 stale docs. The campaign board stays for ?acceptance and ?sim: LEGACY_SWITCHES is now acceptance and sim; retired classic= and mission= links open the story. Gameplay consequences: the Mortar's hack gate is gone, so the wave ladder is the whole roster (Mortar at wave 5, Heptapod at 8); the black market and hack/relay achievements are gone (stored ids drop through sanitiseRecord); the server vault carve is gone, so seeded campaign maps and older sim comparisons shift; the tutorial, field manual and cold open are gone, so the campaign board opens on the briefing. The portal tab is now the breach lab (sinkhole only, same route). The lab stress mode lost its wormhole knobs, the perf tiers their wormhole budgets. index/labs pages, styles (151 rules), main.js menu handling, the pilot panel's practice chrome and the browser scenarios followed. Kept deliberately: the portal_warn and server_dialup cues (keys in the shipped FX package, need a package migration), fabricator.glb (Isao's load fallback), container.glb (campaign life containers), the weathered metal (dresses the live tank). npm test (90 programs) and npm run check pass; browser acceptance recorded in the commit.

Alternatives: Keeping the classic campaign board as a mode: the simulator and most acceptance runs need its rules, so the board stays; only its player-facing entry and onboarding were removed.; Deleting fabricator.glb with the other props: it is Isao's hard load fallback; unpicking that is its own refactor.

Evidence:

- Three read-only footprint maps (missions/mines/hacks; cine/portals/MK-CX/props; classic entry/Astro/Sniper/pilot) before any edit; every scripted cut anchored on exact line content and refused on mismatch.
- Baselines before the change: default browser suite, --story, --story-world and --gunship all passed.

## 2026-09-14 — The game's identity: resourceful joy under pressure

decision · accepted · 2026-09-14-identity-resourceful-joy-under-pressure

The concept audit (2026-09-14-concept-audit-first-pass) asked which single emotion the game is for, offering resourceful joy under pressure as a candidate that reconciles Isao's protopian optimism with the military register of the gunship and sentries. Owner: 'lots to do, like Isao, let's keep positive under pressure! so much to do! I like that as the identity; resourceful joy under pressure.'

Accepted as the identity every component answers to (Rosewater 6): the pressure is the swarm, the clock and the military hardware; the joy is Isao, the builder who rebuilds, and the colony growing out of the wreck it arrived in. A component that serves neither the pressure nor the joy is a candidate to cut. Recorded at the top of docs/FUNMAP.md.

Alternatives: Leaving the emotion unnamed: the audit found the two registers pulling against each other without a chosen contrast.

Evidence:

- docs/FUNMAP.md, reflection and question 1.

## 2026-09-14 — The concept audit's first pass: docs/FUNMAP.md reads the game through Rosewater and Meier, a reflection, a concept walk, dated felt observations and open questions

decision · accepted · 2026-09-14-concept-audit-first-pass

Owner launched the proposed audit, expecting it to be subjective, vague and opinionated but food for thought, and offered a self-reading: fitting the game to expected behaviour, bringing emotional resonance, piggybacking on proven fun mechanics and honing them.

docs/FUNMAP.md written as a companion to ROADMAP.md, not a task list. The reflection argues: the project was born as a proof of concept and the log still reads that way (about a dozen of 117 entries are about feel), so R12 is its main exposure; the sphere is interesting but pays emotionally only at scale moments (breach dive, gunship pass, planet pull-back); the story has moved the game from tower defense (placement) toward an operator game (aim, lead, overheat, paint), which risks a box of polished piggybacked minigames without a spine; three spine candidates already exist: attention (which seat, where the piloted x60 matters, and the view switch the 09-01 playtest found too hard), growth (barrels and biomass, hollow per the wave-42 playtest), and the vibration language (the one long-horizon curiosity hook, currently a pausing modal); Isao's protopian voice and the AC-130 military register pull against each other unless the contrast is chosen, with resourceful joy under pressure offered as a candidate emotion; the owner's self-reading is accurate and mostly a strength (R1, R3, R4) but piggybacking earns liking, not love (R11), and the originals (recycled rocket, bays as lives, BJJ belt ladder, ram-combo farming, vibration language, Isao) get a fraction of the attention. The earned-automation proposal is flagged as the most R13-sensitive idea on the roadmap. Each concept (arrival and foundry, Isao printing, Rotor and Quiver, wall and gate, swarm and hard cores, strike and gunship, tank, economy, modes and labs) gets its emotion, fun versus interesting, and whether its decision is real. Nineteen dated felt observations are tagged by lesson, and ten questions are phrased so a playtest could move them. docs/PLAYFEEL.md is kept as the owner's raw notes.

Alternatives: Per-lesson walk (twenty sections): rejected as checklist-shaped; the concepts are what the owner playtests, the lessons are lenses on them.; Prescriptive recommendations: kept to questions, per Rosewater 19 and the proposal's own rule that prescriptions stay the design's.

Evidence:

- All 117 entries in docs/log/entries read in full; docs/PLAYFEEL.md, docs/PLAYTEST-TODO.md, docs/STORY.md, docs/STATE.md, docs/archive/research-README.md.
- ~/Dev/game-design-lessons/MarkRosewater20LessonsMtG.txt for the twenty lessons.

Supersedes: 2026-09-14-concept-audit-and-funmap-proposed

## 2026-09-14 — The post-tutorial handover: automatic towers, an earned gunship call-in, expeditions that unlock towers

change · accepted · 2026-09-14-handover-gunship-call-expeditions-landed

Sub-project 1 of the puzzle tower defence (docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-14-handover-gunship-call-expeditions-design.md). The story had no automated phase: towers stayed silent unless piloted, the story had no wave clock, a piloted Rotor hit 60 times harder, and towers unlocked on the wave ladder.

Past the Quiver's hard cores (phase settled, or the Defend stage) every tower fires on its own at normal strength and the wave clock runs from a far breach; pilot multipliers apply only before the handover; tower mounts leave the views strip. The gunship waits in reserve: a meter fills from kill biomass (streak included) and cleared waves, a full meter calls a 120 s pass, and it restarts empty. Guarded nests stand at the landing sites; the tank clears one, carries its part to the foundry and Isao can print that tower (rocket-a Relay, rocket-b Mortar, wreck Lancer; later sites Plasma, Needle, Heptapod); a lost hull drops the part back at its site. A DEFENSE jump lands past the handover. New pure modules: automation, gunship-call, expeditions.

Alternatives: Per-tower automation after each tower's own tutorial beat.; A separate defence phase with its own entry, splitting story and defence into two worlds again.; The gunship on a fixed timetable, or bought with biomass.

Evidence:

- npm test, npm run check, npm run build; browser --defense, --story-world, --gunship, --nav, default and --dist passed.

## 2026-09-14 — Next, not yet started: an audit of the game's concepts, not its code, through Rosewater's lessons, and a FunMap kept beside the roadmap

decision · proposed · 2026-09-14-concept-audit-and-funmap-proposed

Owner, at the end of the 2026-09-14 session: after the gunship, the foundry and the Rotor fixes, try something new. An audit, but of the concepts of the game rather than the code, inspired by https://kai-denrei.github.io/game-design-lessons/#rosewater: a reflection, through the early development, on where the game is going. Possibly a separate roadmap kept as a FunMap, logging what feels satisfying and what can be improved.

Recorded as the next thing to try; nothing done. Shape to confirm with the owner before starting: take Rosewater's twenty (design for the human you have; know the emotion each component evokes or cut it; don't confuse interesting with fun; make the fun path the winning path; fear boredom more than challenge; players spot problems, not solutions; the details are where they fall in love) and Meier's interesting-decisions test, and walk the game's concepts as they stand: the arrival and the foundry, Isao printing, the piloted sentries, the wall and the gate, the swarm and the hard cores, the strike and the gunship's pass, the tank, the economy of barrels and biomass. For each, say the emotion it is for, whether it is fun or merely interesting, what the player decides and whether the decision is real, and what today's playtests said felt satisfying (the Rotor annihilating the pile, the gunship's paint-then-launch) or did not (the grey seat, the static top view, rounds that seemed to miss). Output: docs/FUNMAP.md as a living companion to ROADMAP.md, each line a felt observation with its date and the lesson it touches, never a task list; and a short reflection at the top on where the game is going. The audit reads the owner's playtest rulings from the log as its primary evidence: those are the diagnoses, and per Rosewater the prescriptions stay the design's.

Alternatives: Folding fun notes into ROADMAP.md: the owner suggests a separate FunMap, since the roadmap is generated from open items and a feeling is not an item.; A code audit: explicitly not the ask; the architecture guard already audits the code.

Evidence:

- https://kai-denrei.github.io/game-design-lessons/#rosewater retrieved 2026-09-14: Rosewater's twenty lessons and Meier's interesting-decisions test, as summarised there.
- Today's rulings in the log: 2026-09-14-gunship-view-is-the-planet-not-thermal, 2026-09-14-orbital-gunship-top-view-shelved, 2026-09-14-gunship-pov-with-travel-time, 2026-09-14-piloted-rotor-annihilates.

## 2026-09-14 — The post-tutorial tower defence is a placement puzzle; sub-project 1 is the handover, the gunship call-in and expeditions

decision · accepted · 2026-09-14-puzzle-tower-defence-and-the-handover

The owner wants the tower defence after the tutorial to have better and worse solutions: a laser at the end of a long straight lane piercing many, a slow tower building a bottleneck for area damage. A read-only survey found no player-facing tower defence (the classic entry retired), towers silent in the story unless piloted, no story wave clock, a piloted Rotor about 60x stronger, every tower targeting the nearest, chord range, line of sight for only two towers, a looping map generator, and the expedition beat only a line and a camera shot.

Owner choices in the design dialogue: authored challenges first, then generated waves on the same rules; a better setup is rewarded by economy margin, not grades; the handover comes after the Quiver's hard cores, where every tower turns fully automatic and weaker and can no longer be piloted; the player pilots the MÖRK tank and sometimes the gunship, called in from a meter earned by kills and cleared waves; the tank rams, shells and reshapes lanes; guarded expeditions to the landing sites bring home parts that unlock Relay, Mortar and Lancer, later Plasma, Needle and Heptapod, replacing the wave ladder. Split into sub-projects: 1 handover, gunship call-in and expeditions; 2 tower geometry (targeting, line of sight, range along the lane); 3 authored challenges; 4 generated waves and simulator scoring of margin. Sub-project 1 spec and a 7-task plan are written; implementation has not started.

Alternatives: Generated waves only, or authored challenges only.; Grades per challenge, or survival only, as the payoff.; Keep piloted towers with a boost, one seat on many fronts, or an overdrive charge.; Per-tower automation, or a separate defence phase with its own entry.; A scheduled or biomass-bought gunship.; Expeditions as their own later sub-project; one generic material for any tower.

Evidence:

- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-14-handover-gunship-call-expeditions-design.md (5de461b)
- docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-14-handover-gunship-call-expeditions.md (2e1debb)
- Planning found three gaps the spec missed and the plan covers: the story has no wave clock (armWave returns in story mode), the beats offered the views before entering settled, and the beats cannot start past the handover.

## 2026-09-14 — An explosion research brief for outside artists: procedural Three.js r160 modules built in their own lab, imported by contract

change · observed · 2026-09-14-explosion-research-brief

Owner wants satisfying explosions, first from the gunship's seat, then in the ground game, with mini nuclear clouds for the orbital strike; asked whether artists could research procedural r160 explosions and we import the best. The researcher's first pass came back with six open questions (field of view, bloom, the device, the deciding load, scorches, the palette) and three risks (replays, rings on a curved planet, a cloud at a kilometre).

docs/EXPLOSIONS-RESEARCH.md (copied to the owner's Desktop): the stack (r160 native ESM, no textures, bloom applied by the game, the monochrome palette as an option), the three seat views with their exact canvas filters and the consequence that shape must live in luminance, the four sizes (4.5, 11, 32, 60 to 120 m) with lifetimes, the module contract (createExplosion with object, tick, alive, dispose; meta with budget; seed), the lab to build, the five judgements, and the answers: vertical FOV 60 over the gun's zoom (23.1, 31.6, 46.2 degrees; a small blast is 70 px across from 340 m, not 12); UnrealBloom 0.3 / 0.5 / 0.2 with group weights map 0.35, enemies 1.3, others 1.0, applied before the filters; the phone stand-in is Chrome at 4x CPU throttle with the phone tier's settings until the owner names a device; the deciding load is twelve smalls, one medium, one large under 16.7 ms; scorches belong to the game; a concrete palette object ordered by brightness; motion from elapsed time or fixed substeps; a planetRadiusM option with the sag table (0.7 m at 32 m, 9.6 m at 120 m); and the finding that on a 753 m planet the ground horizon from a 2 m eye is 55 m away, so a cloud a kilometre off is invisible and the background event must read from 150 to 300 m as a column and cap over the horizon.

Alternatives: Adopting an external particle tool or flipbooks: rejected, no loader, no texture pipeline, and a look the game does not have.

Evidence:

- src/td-tab.js zoom hook (fov 60 / zoom), src/content/gunship.js zooms, src/postfx.js and src/bloomweights.js defaults, src/perftier.js tiers, src/looks.js, the story planet's 753 m radius; the horizon and sag numbers computed from them.

## 2026-09-14 — The piloted Rotor annihilates fish in a barrel: a round is worth one phage and passes through three bodies, tracers are streaks, the barrel loop holds between shots

change · resolved · 2026-09-14-piloted-rotor-annihilates

Owner (2026-09-14 11:27, two screenshots): a fully cooled Rotor fired point blank into the packed first wave until overheat dropped two or three; the tracers felt too round; no secondary bullet sound over the rotors. Measured on the story swarm with a four-second burst through the acceptance hooks: 85 rounds, 84 hits, 4 kills. So aim and hit detection were fine after the straight-line fix; the damage was not: a piloted round did 0.083 against a phage of 1 hp (base 0.0167 from the Rotor's def.dmg 0.1 split over its burst, times the story pilot multiplier of 5), and each round stopped at the first body, so hits spread across the pile and almost nothing died.

STORY_PILOT.dmgMul 5 -> 60 (src/content/story-defaults.js): a piloted round is worth one phage before the kill combo (the combo multiplies further; a reading of 8.7 a round mid-chain was the combo, not the base). A piloted round now passes through up to three bodies (td-tab's tower shot hit loop, manual only) and counts each. Measured after: a four-second burst of 38 rounds landed 16 hits and killed 50, the whole wave; at a multiplier of 10 the same burst killed 16, judged too little for the owner's brief. The piloted round's tracer is a THREE.Line streak through its trail points (additive) instead of round point sprites. The Rotor's barrel loop is held 0.6 s past the last report instead of 0.35 s, which was shorter than the shot interval and made the loop stop and restart between shots while a single allowed voice was still fading, so it refused to restart and went silent; rotor_pov_fire allows two voices with a 0.05 s minimum interval. Auto sentries are untouched: the multiplier and the pass-through apply to the piloted mount only. The story-world scenario's strip assertion is conditional on the wave still standing, since the Rotor can now clear it during the kill steps. td-tab.js stays at 17557 lines.

Alternatives: Raising the Rotor's def.dmg: rejected, that changes every auto Rotor and the balance pass is not open; the story pilot multiplier is the piloted mount's own number.; Widening the hit radius further: it was already near-certain (84 of 85); the damage was the defect.

Evidence:

- Acceptance probes 2026-09-14 on story-world at the fodder step: {rounds:85,hits:84,kills:4} at dmgMul 5; {rounds:85,hits:84,kills:9} with pass-through; {rounds:38,hits:16,kills:50,roundDmg:8.667} at dmgMul 60; {rounds:61,hits:60,kills:16,roundDmg:0.167} at dmgMul 10 over two seconds.
- browser-test --story-world passes end to end after the change; --sentry-pilot passes; npm test 103; npm run check; npm run build.
- The barrel loop's audibility is not verifiable headless; the timing defect is fixed by construction.

## 2026-09-14 — A // comment appended mid one-liner silently commented out a declaration; check and tests passed

issue · resolved · 2026-09-14-one-liner-comment-swallowed-a-declaration

While making thermal the gunship seat's default, a hook was appended to sentry-pilot.js's setMode one-liner with a trailing // comment. The rest of that line held `const GUNSHIP_EYE=-.35;`, which became part of the comment.

Not a syntax error: npm run check and npm test passed, and the game threw `ReferenceError: GUNSHIP_EYE is not defined` every frame. It surfaced only as a far-downstream browser assertion (the seat monitor showed the TALON feed instead of the impact) and was found by logging the harness's collected browser errors. Fixed with a block comment before committing (ec9e4ce). Rule for long one-liners in td-tab.js, sentry-pilot.js and similar files: use /* */ inside a line and confirm the line's original tail is still live code.

Alternatives: Bisecting the batch by reverting the thermal default: did not isolate it (the failure persisted), which pointed at an error rather than a behaviour change.

Evidence:

- node scripts/browser-test.mjs --gunship: 'the monitor shows the impact point' failed twice with actual 'TALON · seeker feed'; passed at HEAD without the batch in a scratch worktree; the diagnostic run's errors array held the ReferenceError at sentry-pilot.js:149.

## 2026-09-14 — The gunship seat tuned in play: explosion sizes, sounds, heat, rounds from under the gunner, the impact scare, thermal first, sealed sinkholes that pile up

change · accepted · 2026-09-14-gunship-seat-tuning-scare-thermal-sealed-sinkholes

Owner playtests of the Heavy Gunship after the lab explosions landed (2026-09-14-explosions-flir-amoeba-landed): the smallest bursts filled half the screen; rounds came in from the top right of the frame; the Rotor overheated too fast; amoeba deaths drowned the gun; a red target lingered after leaving the seat; a 105 on the sinkhole did not close it; the radar lost its green in night vision; Isao stood still after 'so much to build'. The owner also asked for a build reference on the page to be sure of the version under test.

Build tag names the commit: the dev server stamps branch@sha(+dirty) per request, a release stamps its token and commit (bdbf97e). Explosions were orders of magnitude too big because a puff quad is widened after modelViewMatrix, so object.scale never reached it; the adapter now passes scene units through the module's uScale and the planet radius at the impact (e0dba7f), then the owner's sizes: doubled, then half again (rotary ~7.5 m, Bofors ~15 m, 105 ~45 m). rotor_pov_fire 0.55 -> 0.8, enemy_die_a/b/c 0.6 -> 0.3 through presets promote (985c932). Overheat twice as long: gunship rotary heatSeconds 6 -> 12; the Rotor sentry's perShot and cool halved together, resume 0.6 (lock 9.9 -> 20.0 s at 15 rounds/s, recovery ~4.8 s) (eddd236). From the seat rounds start just below the view's centre (e678579). Impacts scare nearby bodies: freeze 0.35 s, turn from the blast, take exits away, then resume (src/domain/impact-scare.js, EXPLOSION_SCARE per use, every explosion use including tank shells by owner choice); the owner confirmed small rounds herd the swarm. The night and thermal filters skip the radar canvas. The seat opens in FLIR thermal and the base runs warm in it, the Stalheart, foundry, assembly and Isao hot (src/fx/thermal-heat.js). A 105 did seal the sinkhole but the gunship route's continuous waves reopened a breach on the same cell; sealed cells are now avoided, and the rubble cap drops in over 1.2 s. Leaving the seat clears painted targets and rounds in flight. The strike console is hidden (2026-09-14-orbital-laser-idea). Isao tends the foundry from its deploy (ec9e4ce, later made robust when he spawns after it in cbe33f6).

Alternatives: Tune explosion sizes before finding the scale bug: rejected, the object scale could never reach the view-space quads.; Halve only the Rotor's per-shot heat: rejected, at 14 rounds/s it would never lock.; Scare only from the gunship's weapons: offered; the owner kept it for every explosion.

Evidence:

- Commits bdbf97e, e0dba7f, 985c932, eddd236, e678579, ec9e4ce, cbe33f6; test/impact-scare.mjs, test/breach-rubble.mjs, test/thermal-heat.mjs; browser --gunship and --breach-game passed.
- Rotor heat simulation (15 rounds/s): lock 9.9 -> 20.0 s, recovery 4.6 -> 4.8 s, next burst 7.9 -> 11.9 s; 12 rounds/s and below never lock.
- Owner in play: 'the herding works, enemies scatter from the Bofors'.
- Review of e678579 approved: e.prev is never read for enemies, no oscillation after a turn-back.

## 2026-09-14 — A piloted round flies a straight line in space from the muzzle to the body under the reticle; the story-world acceptance's Rotor-kill step passes for the first time

change · resolved · 2026-09-14-piloted-rounds-fly-straight-from-the-barrel

Owner's eighth brief (2026-09-14): the Rotor's misses might be an angle problem with the source of the bullets; they should go in a straight line from the barrel toward the reticle, with satisfying tracers, and hits should feel registered. Until now every tower round, piloted or not, was spawned at the muzzle's surface projection and flown along the surface at half wall height in the tangent direction toward the target, with a 2D proximity hit test; from a wall-top optic aiming down at a body the picture and the round disagreed.

spawnTowerShot takes a straightTo point: for the piloted mount the target's body centre (its position lifted 0.3 cells). A straight round keeps its own point and direction in space, advances along that line, draws its tracer at that point, is range-limited by rayToTerrain from the muzzle along the line (rock and ground stop it), and is hit-tested in space against each body's centre. On a piloted hit: a white ten-dot burst on the body, a quiet high kinetic click, and the pilot's reticle cross flashes red for 120 ms (pilot.hit). td-tab.js is 17557 lines; the budget follows. Result: scripts/browser-test.mjs --story-world passes end to end, including story-world-rotor-kill, which timed out on main and on every branch run since 2026-09-14 morning; the strip assertion now excludes the GUNSHIP button.

Alternatives: Widening the hit radius alone (done earlier the same day): it did not make the step pass; the geometry was the defect.

Evidence:

- browser-test --story-world: every step passes through story-cine-redirect, with story-world-rotor-kill and story-world-fodder green; --sentry-pilot passes; npm test 103; npm run check; npm run architecture with the budget at 17557.
- docs/log/entries/2026-09-14-heavy-gunship-open-items.json item 6 and 2026-09-14-gunship-own-105-downtime-bars-and-rotor-tracers named the miss as open; this resolves it.

## 2026-09-14 — Stay on the sphere: the flat-world reform is not needed

decision · accepted · 2026-09-14-stay-on-the-sphere

The owner had proposed leaving the spherical planet for a flat playing field (2026-09-14-flat-world-direction), then refined it to keep the Stålberg grid on a plane, and asked for a cost map. docs/SPHERE-TO-FLAT-COST-MAP.md sized it: the grid kernel, story planet seam and effects flatten cheaply; the real costs are a bounded planar kernel, triaging 190 radial-up sites in td-tab, and redesigning the orbit build camera and whole-planet shots. Asked what flat gains long term, the assessment was: little frame rate (the frame budget is enemy dot clouds, bloom and draw calls, not sphere maths; a plane loses the free horizon), modest code simplicity mostly for future features, and the real long-term gain is design freedom (authored maps, conventional cameras, borrowed tools). The owner said huge fps or code-simplicity gains could have swayed them; they are not there.

Owner: switching is not necessary; decision made. The game stays on the spherical planet with the Stålberg grid. The cost map stays in docs as the reference if the question returns. Recommended regardless of the world shape: route radial up through one helper so the per-feature sphere tax (aim rays, reprojection, bend shaders, local up for cameras) is paid in one place.

Alternatives: Flat playing field keeping the Stålberg grid: modest gains, costs concentrated in a bounded kernel, td-tab's radial up and two cameras that are spherical by design.; Flat world without the Stålberg grid (the original proposal): larger change, loses the grid identity.

Evidence:

- docs/SPHERE-TO-FLAT-COST-MAP.md (commit 9438ab1): four read-only sweeps and direct counts on 1f582fb.
- Frame measurements this session: 60 fps with 72 enemies (headless gunship skip, 185 calls); 44 fps with 89 enemies and 113.5k points in the owner's browser, budget dominated by points and bloom.

Supersedes: 2026-09-14-flat-world-direction

## 2026-09-14 — The navigation shell landed differently from its spec in four places

decision · accepted · 2026-09-14-navigation-shell-spec-changes

The final whole-branch review of the navigation shell (2026-09-14-navigation-shell-landed) compared the shipped src/fx/shell-nav.js against the navigation spec and found behaviours the spec described that the build does not have. They are recorded here so the spec is not read as the current contract.

DEV's groups (labs, tuning, docs, tools) are always expanded, not collapsible: collapsing was not carried into the plan. The drawer is a right-hand dropdown under the PLAYTEST | DEV toggle on desktop, not a left panel. On phones it is a height-capped dropdown rather than a full-height sheet: DEV is hidden on touch and PLAYTEST is short enough not to need one. The Sound toggle in the drawer footer was dropped, because the game has no single mute for it to drive.

Alternatives: Collapsible DEV groups, as the spec drew them.; A left-hand panel on desktop and a full-height sheet on phones, as the spec drew them.; A footer Sound toggle, which would first need one mute across the game's audio paths.

Evidence:

- src/fx/shell-nav.js and app.css as landed on poc-cleanup; browser --nav (nav-phone, nav-narrow-lab) covers the phone dropdown and the hidden DEV.

## 2026-09-14 — Still open after the story-opening playtest: the breach freeze, the Rotor's sound by ear, Rotor shots into the wall, and ISAO-Birudorōn's review

issue · observed · 2026-09-14-story-playtest-open-items

The story-opening playtest batches (commit 89b81b5) fixed what could be measured and verified headlessly. Several items were changed or partly addressed without an acceptance the owner can rely on, and should be picked up rather than assumed done.

1) WALL BREACH FREEZE: a tank shell breaching a wall on the 71k-cell story planet was a 430-530 ms main-thread long task; numeric edge keys built once per board cut it to about 250 ms. The remaining cost is the full floor/wall/edge array rebuild and GPU re-upload; patching only the breached cells is the proposed next step if it still reads as lag. 2) ROTOR SOUND: the spin loop and the per-shot report are confirmed separate cues in code, and the report now plays at the gun's own cadence, but no one has listened to it — the owner's browser audio was suspect. 3) ROTOR MISSES: the owner saw Rotor fire fail to kill enemies that looked hit and suspected rounds hitting the wall; the gun's hit box was widened and the mounts moved to the rock's real edge, but whether rounds were striking the wall was not measured. 4) ISAO-BIRUDORŌN: upstream marks LOD1 production alpha, pending art-direction and gameplay review; LOD2 (static, one draw) is not pinned and no distance swap exists; the emotion clips hold their final pose, which leaves any A/B glyph pair on whichever frame the clip ends. 5) The asset-tier switches (MÖRK LOW, Stålheart LODs) are approved but shelved while the A6 library settles, and HUGIN's booster awaits the upstream fix (2026-09-14-asset-tier-rulings-shelved).

Evidence:

- 2026-09-14-quiver-grace-hull-contact-drive-ramp (breach long tasks before and after).
- 2026-09-14-quiver-route-sight-isao-birudoron-comms-rotor-report and 2026-09-14-held-faces-plumes-edge-mounts-hitbox-quick-quiver (the Rotor, hit box and mount changes).
- docs/isao-birudoron-assets.lock.json (the pinned revision and its review note).

## 2026-09-14 — The gunship's own 105, separate from the orbital strike; heat, magazine and reload downtime for all three guns read on the HUD; the piloted Rotor's rounds seen as bigger, longer tracers with a wider hit and hit counters

change · observed · 2026-09-14-gunship-own-105-downtime-bars-and-rotor-tracers

Owner's seventh brief (2026-09-14): the gunship should get its own strike, separate from the orbital strike; overheating and reloading of weapons 1, 2 and 3 must be communicated better, with infinite ammo while testing but downtime; the Rotor's PoV still feels off, add more tracer so where the bullets go is seen; enemies that look hit are not dying, cause unknown (landing wrong, hitbox, the wall under, the angle).

src/domain/gunship.js owns the 105 now: paintHeavy, launchHeavy (the shell falls `travel` 3.5 s, then `reload` 14 s, ammo not counted), nudgeHeavy (one steer while it falls), stepHeavy (the landing cell), heavyState (ready / painted / falling / reloading with time left); strike.js is no longer touched by the seat (its arm, paint, launch and retarget hooks are gone from the pilot host). The host lends the blast: on landing executeStrike(ci) runs the orbital strike's own rings, firework, damage and wall rules on the cell, so the blast language is shared and the ration is not. Downtime: the rotary heats for every moment the trigger is down (6 s to overheat, 3.5 s to cool, tested); the Bofors carries a six-round magazine and reloads 2.8 s (tested); the HUD's readout gains a bar (HEAT, MAG, SHELL / FALL / RELOAD) that turns red in downtime, and both the reticle caption and the FIRE field say READY, FIRING · HEAT n%, OVERHEATED · COOLING, READY · n / 6, RELOADING n S, FIRE TO PAINT, PAINTED · FIRE TO LAUNCH, SHELL FALLING · NUDGE ONCE. The Rotor from its optic: a piloted round's tracer is 1.9x the size with six more trail ghosts, its hit radius is 0.6 cells or 1.1x the body instead of 0.42 / 0.8x, and rounds and hits are counted (pilotRounds / pilotHits in the acceptance state) so the owner's miss report can be measured rather than guessed. The cause of the felt misses is not established: the round flies along the surface at half wall height from a wall-top mount and hits by a 2D proximity test, so the two suspects left are the tight box that decides whether the round is aimed at the body at all, and rounds dying at the parapet when the muzzle points along the rampart.

Alternatives: Keep the 105 on strike.js with its ration: rejected by the owner; the gunship's 105 has its own reload and unlimited ammo while testing.; A separate blast for the 105: not now; executeStrike's rings, firework, damage and wall rules are the right blast, only the ration and the camera differ.

Evidence:

- test/gunship.mjs: overheat at heatSeconds, cooling, the magazine emptying and reloading, paint / launch / nudge / land / reload for the 105; npm test 103 programs; npm run check; npm run build.
- browser-test --gunship twelve scenarios pass: gunship-heavy-falling (the first press paints, the second launches, the seat keeps the camera) and gunship-heavy-reloading (after the fall the gun reloads); --sentry-pilot passes with the bigger tracers.
- artifacts/browser/gunship-pov-heavy.png (FIRE TO PAINT with the SHELL bar full) and gunship-rotary-fired.png (the HEAT bar after a burst).

## 2026-09-14 — Navigation is two modes, PLAYTEST and DEV, one click apart on every screen

decision · accepted · 2026-09-14-navigation-shell-landed

The owner, after playing the gunship: the flat story/defend/arrival/record/settings/workshop row was confusing, the variables panel had no meaningful home, and the skip panel collided with the build tag. They asked for a higher level first: DEV for workshops, roadmap, FunMap and labs; PLAYTEST for the sections under playtest; either reachable from anywhere.

src/fx/shell-nav.js renders an always-visible PLAYTEST | DEV toggle joined to the build tag and one drawer per mode from src/content/nav.js. Switching modes never navigates. DEV holds the workshop labs, Tuning (the variables modal's pages), the docs overlay (FunMap, roadmap, devlog, practices) and tools (Felt it, copy deep link, frame readout, diagnostics, the jump enemy count). DEV is automatic on the source tree and hidden on a release unless ?dev=1. Backslash toggles the drawer; Esc closes it without reaching the game. The tab bars, the stage strip, the skip panel, the notes tab and the corner menu, gear and link buttons retired. On touch screens PLAYTEST targets are 44 px and DEV is hidden (a phone does not require DEV).

Alternatives: Two tabs inside one drawer behind a single menu button (cleaner during play, one more step to switch).; A player face and a dev face in one long drawer (the first spec, before the owner's higher-level ask).

Evidence:

- npm test (100 programs), npm run check, npm run build; browser --nav (including nav-phone and nav-narrow-lab), --nav --dist, --gunship, --story-world, default and --dist passed.

## 2026-09-14 — Isao holds one face per state; the landing plumes stay under the skirt; mounts stand at the rock's real edge; a gun's box covers the whole body; the Quiver takes over the moment the first wave is down

change · accepted · 2026-09-14-held-faces-plumes-edge-mounts-hitbox-quick-quiver

Owner playtest: some landing thrusters appear outside the rocket and should emanate from under it; the Isao animation is too aggressive, with constant facial changes, when it should be minimal — one held state per emotion, only scanning and skeptical briefly animated; confirm the Rotor has separate sounds for its spinning barrels and its firing; Rotor fire sometimes misses enemies that look right there, maybe hitting the wall, and the turret should stand closer to the edge, touching it without being cantilevered; basic enemies should die more easily to the Rotor whether hit high or low; and between the first wave being dead and the Quiver it should be almost immediate, straight off the Rotor into the Quiver with a zoomed view of the next enemy.

PLUMES: each is a camera-facing sheet `width` wide centred `radius` off the axis, and the cluster was 6-7 m wide at 1.25 m out against a 2.1 m hull radius, so the sheets stood well past the skirt; the cluster is now 2.1-2.4 m wide at 0.8 m out, inside the skirt. FACES: ISAO-Birudorōn's emotion clips play once into their pose and hold (they looped, alternating glyphs); the game's per-frame face picker is glee when a print lands, determined while he builds, neutral otherwise (the crowd count and the travel and idle faces changed his face every few seconds); the brief panel draws held faces, animating only scan and skeptical for their first 1.5 s. ROTOR SOUND: confirmed in code, two separate cues — the barrel spin is a loop (minigun_ready with rotor_spin.wav, gain and pitch following the barrels' spin) and the report is the one-shot minigun_fire, capped per key (maxVoices, minInterval), with no bus-level cap that lets one choke the other. MOUNTS: a mount perched halfway between centres minus a footprint taken from the model's longest extent — the barrels — so it sat back from the edge by the barrel length; it now slides to the real shared quad edge minus the pedestal's half width (the model's shorter extent). HIT BOX: a gun's box around a body was max(0.22, size x 0.55) cells about a point 0.3 cells up, smaller than the dot cloud; it is max(0.34, size x 0.95). QUIVER HAND-OVER: the Quiver goes on Isao's book the moment the Rotor is handed over, so it prints while the wave is fought; 0.6 s after the wave is cleared (and once it stands) a hard core rises and the Quiver's optic is handed over at once at 3x zoom — the near-the-gate wait and the override delay are gone.

Alternatives: Keep the emotion loops and slow them: rejected, the owner asked for one state, not a slower cycle.; Raise the Rotor's damage instead of its box: rejected, the report was misses on bodies that looked hit, not bodies that took too many hits.; Instant-build the Quiver at the clear: rejected, printing it during the wave keeps Isao's printer the one way things are built.

Evidence:

- test/story-beats.mjs: the Quiver ordered at the Rotor hand-over, quiver-piloting within a second of the clear with the hard core spawned and the optic handed over.
- Measured: SH02 hull radius 2.1 m (sh_rocket.glb bounds); ISAO emotion clips named in the pinned GLB; the story-world suite's Quiver and study steps.

## 2026-09-14 — HUGIN's runtime LODs merge the booster into static geometry, so the story can no longer hide it

issue · observed · 2026-09-14-hugin-booster-not-hideable

The story places the HUGIN launchpad with hide: ['REUSABLE_BOOSTER'], and docs/ASSET-COLLABORATION.md names REUSABLE_BOOSTER as a part the game may hide. The author's README for the c827eda candidates says static geometry is consolidated separately from the six moving groups, and that LOD2's lookup nodes remain but do not articulate its merged geometry.

Observed at c827eda. In both LOD1 and LOD2 the REUSABLE_BOOSTER node is present but carries 0 triangles, against 6,572 triangles in 26 meshes (16.5% of the model) in the shipped game tier. The booster's geometry was not removed: 21.9% of LOD1's triangles and 24.5% of LOD2's sit inside the booster's box, where the shipped model holds 17.9% with the booster and 1.6% without it. A height test could not decide it, because the booster tops out at 18.4 m below a 33.1 m tower; the density test did. Loading these tiers would leave the launchpad showing a booster the story removes. HUGIN is otherwise sound — all six catcher joints still move geometry in LOD1, and dimensions agree within 0.1% — so it is held back on this alone. A fix belongs in the A6 source: keep the booster as its own geometry under REUSABLE_BOOSTER in both tiers. When that lands, pin it, give HUGIN a candidate, and replace the hold-back test in test/landmark-tiers.mjs with one that measures triangles under REUSABLE_BOOSTER in each tier.

Alternatives: Cull triangles inside the booster's box in the game: rejected, guessing at merged geometry on the game side breaks the collaboration contract rather than honouring it.; Stop hiding the booster: rejected, the story removes it on purpose.

Evidence:

- Measured with three.js GLTFLoader against the pinned shipped tier and the c827eda LOD1 and LOD2 plain files: triangles under REUSABLE_BOOSTER, and triangle centroids inside the booster's world-space box with and without the booster present.
- test/landmark-tiers.mjs: HUGIN carries no candidate until its booster can be hidden again.

## 2026-09-14 — Stålheart's runtime LODs are in the game camera for review, behind one switch the lab and the game share; HUGIN is held back because its booster can no longer be hidden

change · accepted · 2026-09-14-stalheart-lods-in-game-camera-review

The owner reported progress in jelaludo/SentryTowers_A6 at c827eda: LOD1 and LOD2 for HUGIN and Stålheart across D0–D3, inside numeric budgets, with required names, clips and dimensions preserved, Stålheart's animated hose omitted from LOD1, and the files marked pending game-camera and reference-phone review with no FPS claim. The upstream roadmap lists that review as the work remaining before the files can be called game-ready, which makes it this repository's work.

Every claim was measured rather than repeated. Stålheart LOD1 D0 is 7,342 triangles in 10 draws against the shipped 40,506 in 105; LOD2 is 2,173 in one. HUGIN LOD1 D0 is 7,190 in 7 against 39,731 in 90; LOD2 is 2,188 in one. Dimensions agree within 0.1%. Stålheart's clip keeps exactly nine controls — gantry, carriage, tool lift, J1–J6 — and drops exactly forty Flexible_carriage_material_feed segments; each kept control still carries geometry, which was measured because a clip can keep its node names while moving nothing. Stålheart is pinned for review in docs/landmark-tiers-assets.lock.json at c827eda: the plain files the author names as source of truth, D0 only because nothing uses D1–D3, the manifest listed first so a fetch verifies it before any tier is measured against it. All four hashes match the author's manifest. scripts/assets.mjs reads the lod1 and lod2 budgets from that pinned manifest instead of retyping them, and its count matches the author's README exactly: 7,342 / 10 / 329,364 bytes and 2,173 / 1 / 75,836. One mapping, withLandmarkTiers, and one parser, landmarkTierMode, live in src/content/base-layout.js beside the structures they change; the game world and the story lab both call them before planBase, which spreads every field through. td-tab passes the switch and reports the story base's lod and errors with no new lines (17,608 of 17,609). The story base now reports the file behind each tier, and a far tier only reports once its file has loaded, so the switch is tested on files rather than appearances. Packed for the release, the candidate game tier is 57.7 KB against the shipped 555.5 KB and the distance tier 17.9 KB. HUGIN is held back: its LOD1 and LOD2 keep the REUSABLE_BOOSTER node, but it carries no triangles against 6,572 in the shipped model, while 21.9% and 24.5% of their triangles sit in the booster's box against 1.6% of the shipped model with the booster removed — the geometry was merged, so the story's hide would do nothing, breaking the hideable-part rule in docs/ASSET-COLLABORATION.md. The game swaps landmark tiers at 150 m and back past 195 m, where the author's provisional contract says 150 m with 20 m of hysteresis; recorded as a question for the review. Two timing bugs in the browser suite were found and fixed: the new game-camera check read the frame rate while its moving average, seeded from zero, still said 0.58; and the existing story-world check behind a fixed three-second sleep read 22.7 at exactly 3.0 s, passing by a tenth of a second. Both now wait on the condition.

Alternatives: Pin HUGIN's candidates anyway: rejected, the story would show a booster it deliberately hides.; Work around the merged booster in the game by culling triangles inside its box: rejected, guessing at merged geometry is the wrong side of the collaboration contract; the fix belongs in the A6 source.; Pin D1–D3 as well: rejected, nothing in the game uses them yet, and they would only add release weight.; Make the candidates the shipped tiers now: rejected, the author and the owner both mark them pending game-camera and reference-phone review.; Put the switch in a new module: rejected, base-layout.js already owns each landmark's tiers, and a candidate belongs beside what it would replace.; Lengthen the sleep before the frame-rate checks: rejected, a longer sleep is a slower guess; the checks wait on the condition.

Evidence:

- test/landmark-tiers.mjs: shipped mode returns the same array; candidate mode swaps only structures with candidates and keeps clips and hides; one parser; every candidate file pinned and each shipped far tier still derived; Stålheart's nine controls carry geometry at the shipped size; its distance tier is static and one draw; HUGIN carries no candidate while its booster cannot be hidden.
- node scripts/browser-test.mjs --story: story-lab-candidate passes, then the arrival cases unchanged.
- node scripts/browser-test.mjs --story-world: story-world-candidate passes in the real game camera at stage 6; story-world asserts a plain link still loads the shipped tiers; all twelve existing story-world cases pass.
- npm test, npm run check (both locks, architecture), npm run build, npm run test:browser and node scripts/browser-test.mjs --dist pass. The release run covers the default suite; the landmark switch was verified against source, and the release build is confirmed to carry it.

## 2026-09-14 — Three reticles and a fire-control HUD for the gunship, per-gun zoom, the owner's 40 mm report and the Rotor's own muffled firing sound from its optic, impacts that land seconds after the report, and the 105's paint-then-guide flow; FX package base 8

change · observed · 2026-09-14-gunship-reticles-hud-sounds-and-the-rotor-optic-firing-sound

Owner's brief 2026-09-14: from the Rotor's PoV add the supplied HeavyMachineGun_RotorGunPOVSound.mp3 only while it fires, muffled, over the spin-up and spin-down, separating the rotors turning from the barrels firing; in the gunship PoV three reticles to tell the selected weapon apart, more HUD (distance, contacts, planet coordinates, calibres), each gun its own zoom, the rotary closer with lots of impact feedback and rotor-like sounds slightly deeper, the 40 mm with the supplied GunshipWeapon2Blast.mp3 and small area explosions that decimate groups, the 105 keeping the strike's sounds and effects but starting from the seat with its own reticle as a one-two: paint a red laser area, then launch and guide as the current strike does; fire 1 and 2 with a lag between the report at the gunship and the impact on the ground.

Audio: assets/audio/rotor_pov_fire.mp3 and gunship_bofors_fire.mp3 pinned in docs/gunship-audio.lock.json and checked by scripts/assets.mjs; src/audio.js voices accept a lowpass option (a BiquadFilter between source and gain); rotor_pov_fire loops at 0.9 gain through a 1400 Hz low-pass only while the piloted Rotor has fired within 0.35 s (td-tab, beside the spool loop) and stops with the seat; the gunship's rotary plays minigun_fire at pitch 0.8 from the ship and kinetic_fire at 0.9 where every second round lands; the Bofors plays the owner's report from the ship and blast_fire at 0.7 where the shell lands. Adding sound keys required the FX package base to move to stalheart-fx-8 (src/content/preset.js: an additive migration gives a version 7 package the two sounds at the baseline and keeps any it already carries; the shipped package promoted as gunship-and-rotor-optic-sounds through npm run presets -- promote). HUD: src/fx/gunship-hud.js draws the rotary's ring of ticks with a spinner that turns while the barrels fire, the Bofors' brackets with a red marker while a shell is in the air, the 105's diamond over a long cross with FIRE TO PAINT then TARGET PAINTED · FIRE TO LAUNCH, and a readout (gun, range, impact coordinates as a survey grid N/S E/W from the aim's unit vector, contacts, nearest from base, in blast, station, fire state, magnification). Zoom per gun: rotary 2.6x, Bofors 1.9x, heavy 1.3x. Impacts: every rotary round lands a small ring and a seven-dot burst, the Bofors an amber ring at 1.6 blast radii, a white core and a 44-dot burst, through host hooks burst/laser/falling folded into the pilot host; the 105's first press paints the aimed cell and lays the strike's red range ring, the second launches, the pointer lock is released and the seat yields the camera and the pointer to the strike's own fall cam and taps (pose returns false while the strike is falling), then the seat comes back. td-tab holds 17558 lines.

Alternatives: Adding the two sound keys without a base bump: rejected, validatePreset requires every key in every package, so stored packages would have failed to load.; A deeper rotary sample for the gunship: not now, minigun_fire at 0.8 rate reads as the same family a register down.

Evidence:

- npm test 103 programs (test/presets.mjs asserts the version 8 migration); npm run check (the gunship audio lock verified); npm run build; browser-test --gunship eight scenarios pass with the rotary and strike reticles asserted; browser-test --sentry-pilot passes with the Rotor's fire loop wired.
- artifacts/browser/gunship-pov-rotary.png and gunship-pov-heavy.png: the two reticles and the HUD readout; the Rotor's PoV sound is not audible in a headless run and awaits the owner's ear.

## 2026-09-14 — The Quiver sees the whole route from the sinkhole; Isao is the A6 ISAO-Birudorōn; his comms are a small terminal on the left; the Rotor's report plays at the gun's own cadence

change · accepted · 2026-09-14-quiver-route-sight-isao-birudoron-comms-rotor-report

Owner playtest of the story: fifty enemies confirmed; Isao still used the older model instead of ISAO-Birudorōn from SentryTowers_A6; the Isao comms box is much too long and should be a small terminal on the left (a recurring request); the Rotor's firing audio needs to be better (the barrel spin is fine; spherical-stalberg sounded better); one wall still stood in the Quiver's line of sight; and despite a good view the Quiver would not lock until the enemy was really close, when it is a long-range weapon.

SIGHT: measured in node against the real story planet — the Quiver stands beside the lane, so its line to the sinkhole left the centre cut: a rock cell at 44 m (the wall in the owner's screenshot) and one at 157 m hid everything past 59 m. A second cut runs from the Quiver socket to the sinkhole, and then base-plan walks the actual shortest route from the sinkhole to the forward cell, opens every rock cell on a line from the Quiver to a cell of it, and walks again until a pass opens nothing (opening rock once shortened the route onto newly hidden cells, which is why it iterates). The route trace now reports no cell off the cut and none out of sight. LOCK RANGE: the long-range probe showed the lock itself was never range-limited — a held hard core locked at 75 m the moment it was boxed, and the 400 m config stands; the far one was simply hidden from 236 m to 153 m, which the route sight fixes. ISAO: docs/isao-birudoron-assets.lock.json pins LOD1 at SentryTowers_A6 da2f64f (sha256 matches the upstream manifest); src/content/isao-faces.js maps the game's 21 emotion ids onto the model's 14 authored clips (each clip scales its FACE_* LED glyphs and poses body and limbs), and units.js makeIsaoDrone builds it with the same hooks (setFace crossfades the clip, tickFace advances the mixer, spinRotors scales Rotor_Cycle, Hover_Idle always on), fitted to the fabricator's board size, the fabricator staying the fallback. COMMS: #story-comms is a fit-content card at most 280 px on the left, like the game's #td-brief. ROTOR: the sample and its manifest entry are identical to spherical-stalberg; the difference was cadence — six presentation rounds per shot and sentry control's rate multiplier made one short sample fire about fifteen times as often, which the voice cap turned into a buzz; it now plays once per shot of the gun's own rate. RACE, found by the story-world suite once the Quiver could see far: the beats counted enemies as bodies alive, not bodies queued, so the tick the first hard core died the second was still in the spawn queue and the Quiver beat settled with it to come (probe: tower kills 51, hard cores 2, alive 0, the second in sight at 235 m). The story API's enemy count now includes queued spawns, which also guards the first wave's cleared check.

Alternatives: Raise the Quiver's lock range or loosen its gate further: rejected, the probe showed range was not the limit; a body behind rock should not lock.; Widen the centre cut until the diagonal clears: rejected, a blanket width removes far more rock than the actual sightlines need and still misses route cells beside the cut.; Merge the Isao model for draw calls like the fabricator: rejected, its faces are scaled nodes driven by clips; a merge would weld the LED glyphs away (the test pins the node names).

Evidence:

- Node trace on the story planet: blocked cells on the Quiver-to-sinkhole line [] after the cut; route sinkhole->forward 27 hops, none off the cut or out of sight after the route pass.
- Long-range probe (quiver-piloting): first lock at 75.3 m; second hard core in sight at 235 m, hidden 236-153 m before the route pass.
- test/isao-faces.mjs against the pinned GLB; node scripts/assets.mjs check; story lab screenshots show the Birudorōn model with its LED face and the comms card at 215x30 on the left.
- Kill-count probe with killsBySrc on the test hook: phase settled at 7.6 s with hardcores 2, alive 0 and the second hard core in reach at 234.8 m; the story-world suite's 'two hard cores, two rounds' failed 1 !== 2 before the fix.

## 2026-09-14 — The seat is the PoV from the gunship: the rounds take seconds to reach the ground and land where the gunner aimed, the Bofors paints a red target while its shell flies, the 105 launches from the same eye without a cut

decision · accepted · 2026-09-14-gunship-pov-with-travel-time

Owner's ruling 2026-09-14 01:30: PoV from the gunship; weapons 1 2 3; the rotary a Rotor on steroids with a slight travel delay of about two seconds; the heavier gun also delayed, shoot then a painted red target, two or three seconds, then a large impact; the gunner must guess the enemies' movement; the missile starts from the same PoV, no jump cut.

The seat opens in the belly PoV (eye 0.35 cells under the platform, pitch -1.5..-0.56 so the shallowest aim lands just inside the 30.5° horizon dip at 12 cells), click to lock the mouse and move to aim, Space or the button fires, the wheel zooms, V looks at the ship, M the shelved top view. Rounds are in flight: src/domain/gunship.js fireRound/stepRounds keep { gun, point, at } on the station clock (stepGunship advances it) and a round lands `travel` seconds later at the point aimed at when it left, rotary 2.0 s and Bofors 2.6 s (src/content/gunship.js); what stands there THEN takes the splash, so a moving swarm has to be led. The optic draws each round as a short bright segment sliding from the muzzle socket to the aim over its travel; the Bofors also paints a pulsing red ring at its aim until the shell lands, then the impact rings (amber 1.6 blast radii, white core) and the blast sound at the point. Leaving the seat drops the rounds in the air. The 105 is unchanged: arm on selection, paint the aimed cell, launch on the trigger through strike.js; the pilot's camera takes precedence over the missile cam, so the fall is watched from the seat and the impact in the monitor. The contact markers and the contacts/nearest readout from the top view stay in the PoV.

Alternatives: Instant hits with a visual delay only: rejected, leading the swarm is the skill the owner asked for; the hit is resolved at arrival.; A ballistic arc for the rounds: not now, a straight flight reads at these ranges; the shell geometry family can replace the segment later.

Evidence:

- test/gunship.mjs: rounds land at their times where aimed, and leaving the seat drops them.
- browser-test --gunship eight scenarios pass (gunship-pov-rotary, gunship-pov-heavy, gunship-top-view, gunship-third, gunship-rotary-fired, gunship-skip-enemies); artifacts/browser/gunship-skip-enemies.png shows the PoV with the 72-strong swarm glowing on the horizon, 109 s left.

Supersedes: 2026-09-14-gunship-seat-is-the-strike-map-with-a-briefing-and-a-far-breach
