## 2026-09-14 — The first wave is one harmless swarm of fifty; a straight sightline to the sinkhole; Isao says the study in a close-up; he sends the tank to the rocket landing sites; the roll-out shows one hull

change · accepted · 2026-09-14-swarm-sightline-isao-study-expedition

Owner review of the story opening: during the get-out-of-the-container animation two turrets show, one static and one sweeping; the first wave is weak and should not hurt the tank or the gate, fifty of them in a single wave, all out of the sinkhole almost at once so it feels like a swarm; the map needs a clear long line of sight to the sinkhole so the Quiver's targeting means something; before the study screen, a close-up of Isao whose face goes neutral, skeptical, then to work as he says 'They talk in vibration', 'I started reverse-engineering it', 'Need more compute', with the screen retitled from SYNTHETIC LEARNING x58 to Preliminary Alien Vibration Language Analysis; then Isao asks the player to fetch material at the other rocket landing sites, the view pulls back to the whole planet and the sites are marked on the radar with triangles.

DOUBLE TURRET: the deploy hid our hull every frame during the bay's authored roll-out, but the frame loop set playerMesh.visible again before rendering, so our hull (turret on its idle sweep) stood inside the authored one (turret still). Both render paths now keep it hidden while a roll-out clip runs. SWARM: STORY_FODDER in content/story-defaults.js (total 50, alive cap 50, one spawn a frame, harmless, spread 0.8 cells, stagger 1.2 s) feeds makeStoryBeats; each spawn carries a golden-ratio-spaced emerge delay and a random scatter across the crater, so the whole crater boils instead of fifty bodies rising from one point; a harmless enemy never calls playerHit (the gate had no damage path). SIGHTLINE: KIT.sightline (240 m, half width 7 m) makes planBase cut a straight lane from the forward cell outward along the mouth's bearing and put the sinkhole at its far end; the cut cells join the opened ground the story world applies before the gate walls. STUDY: the beats gain study-talk (a close-up shot on Isao, the vibration_study brief with per-line faces neutral, skeptical, determined — his in-game working face — worn by the brief panel and the drone alike) before the screen, and expedition after it closes (rocket_sites brief, the landing sites painted as amber triangles on the radar by fx/story-hud.js, a shot pulling back to the whole planet and ending in orbit view).

Alternatives: Spawn the fifty in one frame at the sinkhole's centre: rejected, that is exactly the one-silhouette blob the spawn queue was built to avoid; delay and scatter are what make it read as a swarm.; Re-seed the planet for a straighter natural lane: rejected, it moves every landmark and invalidates the bake; cutting the lane in the plan keeps the world and says what it is for.; Make the study a new modal with its own portrait: rejected, the brief panel already draws Isao's faces and the drone can wear them in the close-up; one presenter.

Evidence:

- test/story-beats.mjs: close-up before the screen, the screen waits for his lines, expedition with rocket_sites, sites and planet view once it closes.
- test/base-plan.mjs: the sinkhole ends the cut line more than 80% of its length out, on opened ground.
- scripts/browser-test.mjs --story-world: fodder alive up to fifty, the isaoTalk shot during study-talk, the retitled screen, expedition with at least three radar sites and the sites shot.

## 2026-09-14 — MÖRK's game and distance tiers are pinned for review, and verifying them found three bugs no unit test could see

change · accepted · 2026-09-14-mork-tiers-pinned-for-review

Owner direction: every asset family ships three classes — Full, Game-ready lower poly, Static distance — and the static distance MÖRK should sit in the containers and show in the orbital views. Upstream SentryTowers_A6 had moved from our pin 8de41ec to 771e166 with a new tier matrix. The owner also asked for the project's learnings to be logged and visible in the workshop, and for the roadmap to track the work.

The author's own manifest-distance.json states the owner's proposal as policy — 'Static D0 proxy only; swap to the articulated game tier before combat or visible damage' — so the answer is yes, with that rule. We were shipping the tier the page calls Original detail. LOW is a verified drop-in: all 17 sockets scripts/assets.mjs enforces, both plasma pivots, all six clips, the same 109 nodes, a byte-identical 142-entry callout file, and the heat-sleeve barrel, whose raw GLB name 'Long cannon barrel' only resolves after three.js sanitizes it (a raw-name check reported it missing and would have blocked a safe adoption). Through prepareMork, the scope test/mork.mjs pins, the hull is 24,196 triangles in 50 batches and LOW is 6,742 in 49: 3.59x less vertex work at the same draw count. LOD2 is 1,706 triangles in one draw with no clips and no barrel, so makeMork throws on it — proof it cannot be driven by accident. Pinned in a new docs/hover-tank-tiers-assets.lock.json at 771e166, because one lock is one revision and the shipped hull stays at 8de41ec until reviewed. The asset guard now validates LOW (its old suffix match would have let LOW ship unchecked) and trips if the proxy ever gains clips or a second draw. src/mork.js serves every tier from one prepare and one make: MORK_TIERS, preloadMorkTier and makeMorkTier, plus a static proxy builder, all reading one MORK_UNIT contract. The Units viewer shows mork-low and mork-proxy as review entries marked variantOf mork; lore coverage folds a variant under its parent and rejects a variant of nothing or of another variant; review tiers load when chosen rather than when the tab opens. Three bugs surfaced only in a real renderer: the proxy builder set no baseScale, so it rendered 1.33x off the hull while every prepare-level test passed, fixed by the shared MORK_UNIT; the viewer's documented ?yaw= deep link had never frozen a tank, because the per-unit spin default ran on the next line and switched the turntable back on, fixed so an explicit yaw wins; and my own size probe measured an axis-aligned box around a spinning hull. With the pose frozen, the built units agree to 0.01%: hull 1.1405 x 0.5905 x 2.6000, LOW 1.1404 x 0.5900 x 2.6000, proxy 1.1404 x 0.5524 x 2.6000. The proxy is authored 6.6% shorter in its own metres with a matching footprint; that delta is pinned in a test and raised with the owner rather than widened away or rescaled. Packing for the release removes 114 triangles from the hull (0.47%), 104 from LOW (1.54%) and none from the proxy, so the release floors are per tier. Pinning costs the release about 338 KB packed; adopting LOW would shrink the shipped hull from 480 KB to 266 KB. PRACTICES.md now records the project's learnings, renders into DEVLOG.md and opens in labs.html#notes beside the roadmap and devlog.

Alternatives: Bump the existing hover-tank lock to 771e166: rejected, it would switch the shipped hull without the visual and animation acceptance pass ASSETS.md requires.; Widen the proxy size tolerance after it failed at 7%: rejected, it would hide a real authored difference; the measured delta is pinned instead.; Rescale the proxy's height to match the hull: rejected, it would distort what may be a deliberate parked pose.; Give the proxy its own baseScale literal: rejected, a second copy of 0.75 is the drift MORK_UNIT exists to remove.; Write lore for the review tiers: rejected, a render tier is not a unit, and an asset-pipeline artefact does not belong in the codex.; Preload the review tiers when the Units tab opens: rejected, 680 KB nobody has asked to judge yet.; Keep one 1% packing floor for every tier: rejected on measurement, it passed the hull and failed LOW.

Evidence:

- test/mork-tiers.mjs: LOW prepares to 6,742/49, drives every clip the hull drives, keeps the rig and heat sleeve, is the hull's size; the proxy is static and one draw, cannot be driven, shares the footprint, carries a pinned 6.5% height delta, and as a built unit carries the hull's unit contract.
- test/lore.mjs: every catalogue entry has lore, folding variants; every variant renders a real catalogue unit.
- scripts/browser-test.mjs units-tier-mork, units-tier-mork-low and units-tier-mork-proxy pass against source and the packed release with the pose frozen and per-tier triangle floors.
- npm test, npm run check, npm run build, npm run test:browser and node scripts/browser-test.mjs --dist all pass; the release carries the yaw fix and ships PRACTICES.md, ROADMAP.md and DEVLOG.md byte-identical to source.

## 2026-09-14 — The Quiver holds a lock through a sightline blink; the hull checks its own footprint against rock; a long run-up; open ground under landed rockets; a wall breach no longer rebuilds its edge maps from strings

change · accepted · 2026-09-14-quiver-grace-hull-contact-drive-ramp

Owner field report: the Quiver lock climbed to about 62% and reset every time and should be faster or more lenient; from a screenshot, the tank clips into walls; wherever a rocket lands, standing or broken, the ground under it should be open; a tank shell hitting a wall has significant lag before the impact; and the tank should start faster and keep ramping so exploring far is less tedious, which needs better wall interaction.

QUIVER: probed in the real story (stage 4 to quiver-piloting) with drop events recorded — the held hard core never left the box (1-4% of the cone), its line of sight flickered off for about 170 ms every 0.45 s as it grazed a rock edge, and each flicker zeroed a 0.9 s lock. src/sentry-pilot.js now keeps a held body that is still inside the box through a 0.5 s sight blink; STORY_QUIVER lockTime 0.9 -> 0.6 and coneDeg 10 -> 14. The shared stepMissileLock contract is unchanged. ROCKET GROUND: base-layout structures anchored 'open' carry a clear radius (rockets 16 m, wreck 24 m); planBase returns the cells within it and the story world opens them before the gate walls are laid. HULL CONTACT: new src/domain/hull-contact.js — a wall's face is the bisector between the open cell and the wall cell, sampled at the hull centre with half its width and at nose and tail along its facing; freeBlocked refuses a move only when it takes the hull deeper than it already is, so a hull a turn swung into a face can always back or slide out. It replaces the old centre-distance margin that let the nose sit a fifth of a cell inside rock. DRIVE RAMP: new src/domain/drive-ramp.js with TANK_DRIVE in content/tank.js — x1.35 pace at once, easing toward x2.6 (time constant 4 s) while held forward; steering hard gains less, idling or reversing bleeds it, a head-on thud keeps a quarter and a wedge keeps none; the engine level normalises against the full run-up. SHELL LAG: measured as a 430-530 ms main-thread long task on every breach, the 71k-cell planet rebuild; profiled, most of it was a string-keyed directed-edge map rebuilt every time and a rounded-float string per segment for the dedupe. The edge map is now built once per board and segments dedupe on numeric vertex-index keys: the breach long task measured 250 ms after. The remaining cost is full array and GPU re-upload; patching only the breached cells is the next step if it still reads as lag.

Alternatives: Drain the lock instead of zeroing it on loss in stepMissileLock: rejected for now, it changes the shared contract every launcher relies on (test/missile-targeting.mjs pins no inherited progress), and the measured cause was the pilot dropping a target that never left the box.; Stiffen the existing wall cushion: rejected, it is a capped per-frame push by design, and a faster hull outruns any cap that does not also pin it in corridors.; Incremental geometry patch per breached cell: deferred, a larger refactor of buildGeometry; the cheap profile-led change came first.

Evidence:

- Quiver probe (story stage 4, quiver-piloting): before, drop events all visible:false with inCone:true; after, tracking pass locked with 0 resets and held through a NOLOS sample.
- Drive probe (story stage 7 after the bay roll-out): pace rose 35 -> 53 milli-arc/s over 6 s of held W; a head-on stop was refused by the hull check, not by the story or a crater.
- Breach long tasks: 488, 527, 478, 444 ms before; 250 ms after the key rewrite. CPU profile before: buildGeometry self 260 ms, firstTime/segKey/ptKey about 115 ms, GC 29 ms.
- test/hull-contact.mjs, test/drive-ramp.mjs, test/base-plan.mjs (open ground within the clear radius) and test/world-recipe.mjs (no rock under a landed rocket on the real planet).

Owner pointed at the A6 arrival-foundry family (AFR-01 Seed Foundry with the SH02 four-piece salvage layout) with the story: Isao arrives on the rocket, rough landing, and recycles the arrival rocket with this foundry to kick-start the colony. Today story-beats conjures the Rotor's and the Quiver's cost with api.grant(api.cost(key)).

Alternatives: Barrels as extra biomass on top of the conjured grants: rejected, the chain is the point.; Consume the whole rocket: rejected, the legs stay as the arrival's mark.; A one-press deployment by the player: rejected, the opening's lesson is Isao building while the player watches.; Compressing the authored clip: rejected, the first cycle plays as authored; later cycles are spaced by a tunable.

## 2026-09-14 — The gunship seat is the orbital strike's own map view in its surveillance look, aimed with the pointer; a skippable wireframe briefing precedes the first seat; the test breach opens a minute's walk out

decision · accepted · 2026-09-14-gunship-seat-is-the-strike-map-with-a-briefing-and-a-far-breach

Owner's second playtest ruling (2026-09-14 00:07): the view the gunship should use already exists, the orbital strike's white map (screenshot: the story planet as a white wireframe with the swarm as dots and a red range readout); the breach must appear much further from the base, a minute's walk, the other side of the map if need be, to leave time to enjoy the action; and the gunship should be introduced with a skippable wireframe 360 briefing of the airframe, its weapons, ammunition and capabilities.

On the map the pointer is the aim: the cell under it (cellAtScreen) is the impact, the rings and the readout follow it (RANGE in metres from the platform), a click or Space is the trigger, no pointer lock; the heavy paints the pointed cell while armed and launches on the trigger. Enemies for the skip rise from a breach on the open cell about 69 hops from the heart (60 s at the phage's 1.15 cells/s) nearest the lane end, or the farthest reachable cell on a smaller planet (gunshipFar in td-tab, one BFS per breach). src/fx/gunship-briefing.js is a modal with its own small renderer: the pinned KORP as a wireframe turning slowly over a grid, engines tilting, six pages (airframe, orbit and fuel, 25 mm, 40 mm, 105 mm as the strike, the gunner's role), SPACE/click/NEXT to advance, SKIP, Escape; the game is paused under it; once per browser (stalheart:v1:gunship-briefing); the seat is taken when it closes if the platform is still overhead. brief=0 on the skip URL skips it for acceptance. td-tab.js stays at 17558 lines.

Alternatives: A separate white shader for the seat: rejected, the strike's canvas filter already is the look and costs compositing only.; Keeping the PoV as the default with the map as an option: rejected by the owner's ruling; the map is the view.; Placing the story's own gunship breach: not done, the far breach is the skip's; where the encounter's breach opens in the story is the beats' call when the encounter is written.

## 2026-09-14 — Total dots on screen is the wall, not draw calls: merging bought nothing, thinning the authored three took 1,200 bodies from 22.5% late frames to 0.1%

experiment · accepted · 2026-09-14-dots-not-draw-calls

The owner compared four runs by screenshot because there was no way to control across runs, and every card showed PEAK CALLS 0 with GPU timing unavailable. They asked which optimisations to implement — merged dot clouds, temporal spreading, shader pre-compile, all of them — and floated dropping to a cheaper gunship orbital view when frame cost rises.

The cards were from a stale service-worker cache: the current build records calls and falls back to a stalling probe when EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2 is absent, verified by blocking the extension through Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument (49 samples, 306 calls, no timer). Every run record now carries the build tag and whether the timer was present, and a card whose run recorded no draw calls says 'stale build — reload with ?sw=0' rather than showing zeros. A sweep button runs 250/500/1000/1200 back to back keeping every leg, so a comparison is a series under one build in one sitting. Measured with that: 250 bodies 306 calls 123,500 points 0.1% late; 500 556/247,000 2.2%; 1000 1,056/494,000 19.6%; 1200 1,256/592,800 23.7%. Merging was then implemented as src/fx/dot-swarm.js, an InstancedBufferGeometry crowd in ONE draw call, and it REFUTED the hypothesis: 1,256 calls to 146 at 1200 bodies changed nothing and was worse at the top (late 23.7% to 47.7%), because dead instances still run the vertex shader while hidden objects are culled outright. Draw calls are not the constraint at this scale. Point count is: 1,200 ghosts at 180,000 points drop 0.1% of frames while 1,200 phages at 592,800 drop 22.5%, at identical draw calls. The lever is therefore the density factor, which the three authored creatures ignored — the densest bodies on the roster (692, 494, 578 against 150) were the only ones a crowd could not turn down. creatures.thinCloud resamples them uniformly (keeping index i only when it opens a new bucket, so each section keeps its share rather than a slice taking the phage's head and dropping its legs) and DOT_SHAPES routes the three through it; the authored count stays the ceiling because interpolating new dots would be invention. Result at 1,200 phages: density 1.0 is 592,800 points and 22.5% late, 0.5 is 296,400 and 9.6%, 0.3 is 177,600 and 0.1% — a locked 60fps at the same population that was failing.

Alternatives: Adopt merged instancing: rejected on its own measurement. src/fx/dot-swarm.js is kept because it is correct and the technique becomes right if draw calls ever do bind, but it buys nothing today and costs the per-body material the game writes to every frame for the slow field and the death fade.; Temporal spreading of enemy updates: not implemented, simulation is not the bottleneck — 505 concurrent held 60fps in the real game and the cost here tracks points, not logic.; Resolution LOD by crowd size: superseded by density. Thinning the bodies attacks the same vertex work without touching the framebuffer everything else is drawn into.; Switching to a cheaper gunship view when cost rises: not implemented; the trigger would fire hardest at the climax and teach the player to read the frame budget. The inversion — raise density because the cheap scheduled view is arriving — keeps the causality the fiction already has.

Evidence:

- Sweep under one build: 250/500/1000/1200 at 0.1/2.2/19.6/23.7% late frames, medians 16.7 to 16.9 ms throughout.
- Merged vs per-object at 1200 bodies: 146 vs 1,256 draw calls, late frames 47.7% vs 23.7%.
- Density at 1200 phages: 592,800/296,400/177,600 points at 22.5/9.6/0.1% late.
- test/thin-cloud.mjs: the three honour the factor, the authored count is a ceiling, each section keeps its share, a thinned cloud is never empty and never grows, thinning is deterministic, and highlight dots survive.
- npm test, npm run check, npm run build, npm run test:browser and node scripts/browser-test.mjs --dist all pass.

## 2026-09-14 — The swarm lane drives the real tank instead of a second one, and the roadmap and devlog open in the workshop

change · accepted · 2026-09-14-tank-sourced-and-notes-tab

Owner on labs.html#swarm: the tank's movement is a downgraded version — no swerving wanted, but also no animation, no hover on and off, no sound. To truly test we must RE-USE the tank, not reinvent the wheel. This is the architecture principle everywhere: one source of truth for the sentries, one for the tank, so a change in any module reaches every module because they source at the same spot. Separately, the owner wants the roadmap and devlog visible in the labs UI so everything needed to evaluate the game is on one interface, and accepts that the already-public DEVLOG stays public.

The lab no longer owns a tank. Movement is domain/yard-drive.stepYardDrive — the same flat-ground model the astro yard drives — with the canyon walls handed in as its blocker boxes; hover, idle vibration and the touchdown rock are tankfeel.js applied through the persisted FEEL, which MÖRK answers with its own userData.applyFeel; the engine cue is the shared audio bank. src/labs/astro-drive.js was the pattern followed. Crucially the SCRIPTED modes are inputs to that same model rather than a second path: a charge is a throttle, not a teleport, and the ram bump now scales the throttle instead of the position, so an impact reads as the engine losing its bite. The hand-rolled x/z integration and the sine-driven yaw that read as skidding are gone. Two bugs surfaced while wiring it: bodies were seated as far back as z -396 in a lane spanning +/-150, so most of the file stood outside the drawn world, and the hull stopped at the lane end instead of reaching them — 0 kills in 7 s. With the column seated inside the canyon and the corridor freed, 148 kills in 7 s, speed bitten from the model's 11 down to 3.3 by impacts, yaw held flat. New lab src/labs/notes-tab.js at labs.html#notes: it FETCHES ROADMAP.md and DEVLOG.md rather than holding a copy, with a small markdown subset (headings, lists, tables, fences, rules, quotes, inline code/emphasis/links — no dependency, this repo has none by choice), a contents rail built from the headings, and a filter that hides whole SECTIONS rather than lines because an entry without its outcome is only a headline. Both documents were added to the release allowlist in scripts/build.mjs so the tab works in dist, and a build that forgot them renders an explanation rather than an empty page.

Alternatives: Reuse src/labs/astro-drive.js wholesale: rejected, it carries astro's own UI and its 1/3 camera-mode model; the shared truth is the domain and feel modules it calls, and calling them directly is the same single source without dragging a second lab's chrome in.; Keep the scripted modes as direct position writes: rejected, that is exactly the second tank the owner asked to remove — a scripted drive must be an input to the one model.; Render the roadmap into HTML at build time: rejected, a fetched document cannot drift from the file, and a generated page can.

Evidence:

- Headless: drive speed 11 at the model's cap, yaw held at pi across 4 s (no swerving), tankfeel hoverT reaching 1, and 148 kills in 7 s once the seating was fixed.
- scripts/browser-test.mjs case notes-roadmap asserts the Now table renders, the contents rail builds, the generated open block reached the page, both documents fetched, and that the devlog filter narrows without emptying.
- npm test, npm run check, npm run build, npm run test:browser and node scripts/browser-test.mjs --dist all pass; notes-roadmap passes against the release too, which is what proves the build allowlist change.

## 2026-09-14 — The lane is driveable and shouts like the game; a run is a fixed population that scores itself against the 60fps line

change · accepted · 2026-09-13-swarm-runs-and-shout-layer

Owner feedback on the lane: the shader wobble looks better, but did it gain fps; the tank was skidding and should run straight and be drivable by the tester; and the ram chain should appear as it does IN GAME, not only as telemetry, because this is a gameplay-feel test as much as a measurement. Then: after a run — all units destroyed or passed out of frame — show a telemetry score with max, average and median percent of a 60 fps frame, visually, recordable, and comparable against the next run.

FPS answer, measured rather than claimed: no meaningful gain. The three authored creatures cost ZERO before because they had no TICKS entry and never animated in gameplay, so the port bought animation, not frames. The only real saving is the two swimmers leaving the CPU, worth 1.75 us/unit/frame for scoutufo (170 dots) and 3.81 us for shellback (400 dots) — 0.35 ms and 0.76 ms respectively at 200 alive, against a 16.67 ms budget. The tank now holds one heading: a first cut yawed it off a sine, which read as skidding because the hull pointed one way and travelled another. Default drive is manual on WASD/arrows with held-key state rather than key repeat. The shout layer is the game's own: .callout, .co-milestone and the .combobox [data-tier] ladder all come from styles.css and the pack variables from fonts.js applyFontPack, so the lab wears the real phosphors, the real rungs and the tier-5 flicker, and a typeface decision still reaches it without a second edit. A run seats `count` bodies ONCE and ends when the last is rammed, escaped or blocked — free play still recycles, because a number taken off an endless stream describes the sampler rather than the work. The scorecard reports two distributions side by side: wall-clock frame gap (max, p95, median, late-frame count and percent, each also as percent of a 60 fps frame) and the GPU timer, explicitly labelled indicative. Both are shown because they disagree: TIME_ELAPSED_EXT through ANGLE reported a 5.7 ms median and 38 ms p95 for 150 bodies while a gl.finish probe on the same scene read 0.8 ms for 420, so the card carries the one number that cannot be argued with next to the one that can. A per-frame bar chart against the 60 fps line shows the shape of the run, clamped at 4x budget so a single hitch does not flatten every other bar. Runs can be kept (up to four, persisted through src/storage.js) and are rendered as columns beside the current one. Found by measuring: the readout's own cost probe renders the scene six more times and blocks on gl.finish each time, and at 0.4 s intervals was landing a deliberate hitch inside the distribution the run exists to measure — late frames fell from 12% to 8% and the worst gap from 188 ms to 99 ms once it stands down during a scored run.

Alternatives: Report only the GPU timer: rejected, it disagreed with a direct probe by roughly 7x and nothing in the card would have shown that.; Report only wall-clock frame gaps: rejected, they cannot separate GPU work from vsync or compositor jank; the pair is what makes either readable.; Keep recycling bodies during a scored run: rejected, an endless stream has no end to measure to.; Sample with gl.finish every frame: rejected, it distorts the frame it measures — which is exactly the bug that was found and fixed.

Evidence:

- Node microbenchmark of swimWave for the scoutufo and shellback point counts, 30,000 iterations after warmup.
- scripts/browser-test.mjs swarm-study now asserts the study opens under the tester's hand, the ladder shows and climbs and wears the shout pack, and that a finished run accounts for every body (rammed + escaped + blocked === count), sampled wall-clock frames, and can be kept, compared and cleared.
- Headless run, 260 phages: 42.2 s, all 260 rammed, chain of 260, 2725 kg, frame gap median 16.7 ms and p95 23.5 ms with 8% late, GPU timer median 6.32 ms. Headless Chrome has no real vsync, so its late-frame share is environmental; the card exists so that is visible rather than assumed.
- npm test, npm run check, npm run build, npm run test:browser and node scripts/browser-test.mjs --dist all pass.

## 2026-09-13 — The enemy study becomes a canyon lane: a file closing on MÖRK, the ram premium, the splat and the hull bogging down

change · accepted · 2026-09-13-swarm-lane-ram-feel

The first cut of labs.html#swarm orbited a grid of bodies on a turntable. The owner's note after looking at it was that a grid teaches nothing: put them in a long file in a game-like canyon with walls, heading at the tank, crashing into a moving tank, with the animation, the sound, the RAM bonus and the slight slowdown on impact — make it feel more real.

The lab is a lane now: a 300-long canyon with walls and a lip, a file of bodies closing from the far end, and the hull ploughing them. The rules are the game's, imported rather than re-typed — RAM_PREMIUM, STREAK_CAP and STREAK_STEP from domain/economy, rammable and bounty from ENEMY_SPEC, the splat from units.makeDotBurst, the death cues from the shared audio bank. A rammable belt goes under the treads for bounty x premium x a capped streak; a solid core stops the hull instead and breaks the chain, because letting the tank plough a drifter would teach the wrong thing about the belt ladder the lab exists to study. Every impact costs the hull speed it earns back at 1.9/s, so a dense file genuinely bogs it down. Controls: body, dot cloud or jelly, in-the-lane count to 1200, density, file width, tank charging/weaving/parked, chase/side/top/free views, walls and sound. The cost readout stays, because look and cost are the two questions asked at once. Three faults were found by screenshotting rather than by any test: the bodies were scaled near the hull's own size and read as trees beside it, fixed by driving scale from ENEMY_SPEC.size against the hull radius; the HUD sat under the chrome menu button main.js injects top-left, moved right; and the file was a field rather than a column, fixed with a narrower lateral spread, the full lane depth and a charge that sweeps rather than eases down the middle. MÖRK now stands in the lane: the procedural hull builds on the first frame and the real GLB swaps in when preloadMork resolves, so the lane runs immediately rather than waiting on a download. Measured sustained on an M4: 303 kills in 30 s, a chain of 303, 3,198 kg, hull oscillating 0.28 to 0.91, 420 bodies at 1.9 us each.

Alternatives: Keep the turntable and add a count slider: rejected by the owner on sight; a crowd reads by how it funnels, not by how it spins.; Wait for MÖRK before showing anything: rejected, a lab that waits on a GLB to draw its first frame is one nobody opens twice; the procedural hull covers the gap.; Let the tank ram everything for a better kill rate: rejected, the rammable/solid split is the read the whole roster is built on.

Evidence:

- scripts/browser-test.mjs case swarm-study now asserts the lane runs: kills climb, a chain forms, ramming pays, the hull slows, a white belt never blocks, and a drifter blocks and is never rammed — plus motion per family and the jelly arriving as triangles.
- npm test, npm run check, npm run build, npm run test:browser and node scripts/browser-test.mjs --dist all pass.
- Screenshots at 1500x940 DPR2 through the headless harness; the scale, HUD and column faults were visible there and in no test.

## 2026-09-13 — The authored creatures deform on the GPU and finally deform in the game at all; a #swarm lab studies look and cost together

change · accepted · 2026-09-13-gpu-dot-wobble-and-enemy-study

Measurement had shown rendering and simulation are both affordable, so the owner asked for the shader port as a FEATURE rather than an optimisation, plus a lab studying enemies for look-and-feel and telemetry. Tracing the code first found that the amoeba, phage and jellyfish have no TICKS entry: in gameplay they were static clouds, because waveJelly only ever ran in the unit viewer's one-at-a-time carousel. The claimed 17x speed win therefore did not exist in gameplay, and was corrected before any work started.

src/content/dot-wobble.js owns the motion constants and GENERATES the GLSL; creatures.waveJelly and swimWave read the same constants, so the two implementations cannot drift (the GLSL half cannot run in Node, so a constant changed on one side would silently make the lab and the game show different creatures). src/fx/dot-material.js is a drop-in replacement for PointsMaterial: td-tab writes material.opacity for emerge and death fades and material.color.setHex for the slow field every frame and is at its line budget, so `color` is the live uniform value and `opacity` is a defined setter; nothing in td-tab changed. units.js: the three authored creatures now wobble in the vertex shader, and scoutufo and shellback moved their swim off the CPU, which removes the last per-frame re-pose in gameplay. Behaviour is pinned by goldens captured before the extraction: both functions are unchanged to nine decimals. Two bugs surfaced only in a browser probe, neither reachable from Node: makeCloud seeded its buffer with waveJelly(base, 0), which is not the identity because the ripple term is spatial, so the shader deformed an already-deformed body; and the TICKS fallback overwrote the wobblers' tick slot, which would have frozen the wobble at t=0 with no error anywhere. New lab src/labs/swarm-tab.js at labs.html#swarm: body, form (dot cloud or jelly), count to 3000, density, spread, layout and turntable on the left, and a readout giving cost in ms, microseconds per body, percent of a 60 fps frame, draw calls, points and triangles. It measures with a tight loop and gl.finish rather than rAF deltas, because rAF measures vsync — a first cut of the earlier benchmark reported a flat 16.6 ms for every variant at every count for exactly that reason. Measured in the lab: 1000 amoebas at 474,020 points cost 1.2 ms, 1000 phages 1.4 ms, 300 jelly bodies at 1.52M triangles 0.3 ms.

Alternatives: Add a crowd mode to units-tab.js: rejected, that module documents its own one-unit-at-a-time rationale and is already 1401 lines; a crowd study is a different question and belongs in a sibling.; Give every roster body the wobble: rejected, the rest read as machinery and keep their transform idles; only the three authored organic bodies and the two swimmers deform.; Write the GLSL by hand next to the JS: rejected, two copies of 0.18 with no failing test between them is the drift this design exists to prevent.

Evidence:

- test/dot-wobble.mjs: eight tests including waveJelly and swimWave goldens captured before the extraction, and an assertion that every content constant appears in the generated shader.
- scripts/browser-test.mjs case swarm-study: asserts the shader compiles and draws, that the authored creatures report motion 'wobble', that swimmers report 'swim' and roster bodies 'static', and that a jelly draws triangles and no points.
- npm test, npm run check, npm run build, npm run test:browser and node scripts/browser-test.mjs --dist all pass.
- Lab readout on an M4: 1000 amoebas 1.2 ms at 474,020 points; 300 jelly bodies 0.3 ms at 1.52M triangles.

## 2026-09-13 — Swarm headroom: 505 concurrent enemies hold 60 fps in the real game, and the static board costs more than the crowd

experiment · accepted · 2026-09-13-swarm-simulation-headroom

Rendering cost was measured first and found not to be the limit. The remaining unknown was simulation at swarm counts: pathing, collision, hit detection, the hard-core proximity sensor and slow-field tower linking. The owner asked for a harness with an unkillable tank and a wave slider before deciding enemy type and rendering.

No harness was written: the instrument already existed. src/lab.js provides immortalTank, immortalHeart, freezeEnemies, holdWaves and a waveMult slider (1 to 20), switched on with ?lab=1 and driven by ?labwave=N, and labLine already emits fps, frame ms, gpu ms, draw calls, triangles, points and enemy count on one greppable line; ?stress=T:W queues a crowd and ?perf=N reports draw calls. Measured headless on an M4 at 1440x900 with index.html?lab=1&labwave=20&stress=0:4000, waves 4 to 7: frame time stayed 16.7 ms and 60 fps throughout, never dropping a frame, at a peak of 505 concurrent enemies. GPU time was 6.8 to 8.3 ms and did not track population: 7.3 ms at 12 enemies and 7.4 ms at 504. Draw calls ran 1120 to 1976 and points to 272k. The decisive finding is that the static board dominates: with one enemy alive the scene is already 1120 draw calls and 375,000 triangles, so enemies contribute roughly 250 to 800 calls on top and the base, towers and postfx are the larger cost. Concurrency could not be pushed past about 505 because waveMult caps at 20 and enemies are consumed by towers and the Heart as fast as they spawn, so the population reaches equilibrium rather than accumulating; the true ceiling is above 500 and was not found. Note that the 5,334 bodies recorded at wave 75 in docs/STATE.md are scheduled across a wave, not concurrent, and concurrency is what costs.

Alternatives: Write a new stress module with invulnerability and a live wave control: rejected, src/lab.js already provides all of it and src/td-tab.js has one line of budget headroom (17608 of 17609), so adding to it would fail the architecture guard.; Use freezeEnemies to accumulate a larger crowd: rejected for this question, freezing skips updateEnemies and so removes the simulation cost being measured.

Evidence:

- Headless Chrome via scripts/chrome-proc.mjs against scripts/serve.mjs, ~280 s run, LAB telemetry lines captured from the console.
- Combined with 2026-09-13-enemy-render-cost-measured: rendering is free at 2000 units and simulation is free at 500 concurrent, so enemy representation is now an aesthetic decision rather than a performance-constrained one.
- Not measured: concurrency above about 505, and weaker devices than an M4. The stock harness cannot force accumulation while enemies remain mortal.

## 2026-09-13 — Enemy render cost measured: CPU re-pose is the only thing that fails; merged shader clouds carry 1.38M dots in one call, and the jelly body works at swarm scale

experiment · accepted · 2026-09-13-enemy-render-cost-measured

Owner is deciding enemy type and rendering so the game can scale to swarms, and wants the cost understood before any decision: per unit, at large scale, and for smooth animation. Open questions were whether the amoeba is too expensive to use widely, why the borrowed roster is 150 dots, whether the jelly-effect boss body could be used for smaller enemies, and whether unexplored options (cheap deforming spheres, shaders, resolution LOD by crowd size) are better. The owner wants to keep the BJJ belt colour ladder, the solid-core tell and the hard-core proximity sensor, which playtested well.

Measured on an M4 (ANGLE Metal, headless Chrome via scripts/chrome-proc.mjs, vsync-free with gl.finish, median of 60 frames, 1280x720, bare scene). Frame cost in ms at 20/100/500/2000 units: current CPU re-posed cloud 0.30/1.40/6.80/27.50 (23.78 ms of the last is CPU animation alone, 36 fps, the only failing path); static cloud 0/0.10/0.40/1.60; vertex-shader wobble cloud 0/0.10/0.40/1.60, identical to static, so the deformation is free; merged single-draw-call shader cloud under 0.10 at every size, carrying 1,384,000 animated dots in ONE draw call at 2000 amoebas; jelly bodies 0/0.10/0.40/2.00 at 2000, which is 5.6M triangles. Node microbenchmark of the pure functions: waveJelly 13.617 us per amoeba per frame (692 dots, 8.1 KB/frame upload), 8.932 us phage (494), swimWave 5.875 us jellyfish (578); 100 amoebas is 47.5 MB/s of buffer upload against the 99 MB/s that jelly.js already rejected for a single imported boss. Conclusions: dot count is not a cost axis once the wobble leaves the CPU; per-unit CPU re-pose is the whole problem; the jelly body is viable for ordinary enemies, not only bosses; per-instance colour in the merged path makes the belt ladder free at swarm scale; resolution LOD by crowd size is unnecessary for cost on this class of GPU. The 150-dot roster figure was traced to the default parameter of enemyDotPts and an archive note that round 1 is 150 points of fodder, not to any measurement; neighbouring defaults are 170, 190, 400 and 1150. Also found: the phage is the only one of the classic three without its own movement treatment, receiving waveJelly (a blob squash-stretch) on a rigid lunar-lander silhouette, which is the reported bad animation; the jellyfish already has swimWave. DOT_SHAPES can swap a silhouette without touching the type id, as scoutufo and saucer already do, so replacing the phage's shape needs no change to story beats, cinematics, lore or tests.

Alternatives: Reduce dot density to buy performance: rejected by measurement, density is not the cost axis once the wobble is on the GPU.; Render at high resolution for small crowds and drop resolution at large ones: available but unnecessary for cost at these sizes; keep for weak devices rather than as an architecture.; Replace dot clouds wholesale with meshes: unnecessary, both idioms are affordable, so representation can be chosen aesthetically.

Evidence:

- Headless benchmark comparing cloud-cpu, cloud-static, cloud-shader, cloud-merged and jelly at 20/100/500/2000; renderer.info for draw calls and primitive counts; gl.finish to defeat vsync (first run read a flat 16.6 ms across every variant, which was vsync, not work).
- Node microbenchmark of waveJelly and swimWave over 20,000 iterations after warmup.
- src/units.js header claims per-vertex CPU animation does not scale to crowds; measurement confirms the CPU path but shows the same clouds are nearly free once the wobble moves to the vertex shader.
- Rendering only. Pathing, collision, hit detection and the hard-core proximity sensor at swarm counts are NOT measured and are the likely remaining ceiling.

## 2026-09-13 — The gunship is the orbital strike's platform: a mount on a fixed orbital pass, gunner only, soft-warning collateral

decision · accepted · 2026-09-13-heavy-gunship-mount-design

Owner asked to start planning two things: Heavy Gunship mechanics, and alternatives to half-dotted enemies. The orbital strike in src/strike.js works and is satisfying but has no home — no fiction, no body, no seat — while the module already implies a platform that must re-enter orbit. The KORP/GS01 heavy gunship exists as a pinned-able asset at SentryTowers_A6/korp with rotary cannons, a heavy cannon and engine-tilt clips. Owner's brief: 25mm rotary, 40mm Bofors, 105mm M102; perspective shift, thermal imaging, herding into heavier fire; avoid friendlies and avoid opening your own walls; tension and thematic weight over a decoded-language gap.

Design agreed in docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-13-heavy-gunship-design.md; nothing implemented. GUNSHIP becomes a mount on the existing strip, gunner only — the player never flies it. The diegetic limit is fuel: the gunship is in orbit and cannot manoeuvre or land, so it passes over the base on a fixed schedule the player cannot influence; overhead the guns are live, otherwise the next pass counts down. No fuel meter ships; fuel becomes spendable only if the later VTOL landing progression arrives. One airframe yields two resources: the 105mm IS the orbital strike, rationed by strike.js unchanged and conserved for sinkholes, while the 25mm and 40mm are free while on station. Collateral is soft warning only: per-gun danger rings are readouts, never refusals, and breakWalls/breakTowers stay true, so the player can open their own base. The view is a thermal optic with the real render in the corner monitor, reusing src/fx/story-monitor.js and src/fx/story-scope.js unchanged. New code lands in src/domain/gunship.js, src/content/gunship.js and src/fx/gunship-optic.js with sentry-pilot.js as the only host; strike.js is not modified and td-tab.js does not grow.

Alternatives: Player flies and guns the aircraft: rejected, the fuel fiction makes the gunner-only limit diegetic and it avoids a flight model, chase camera and new input host.; Gunship as a dedicated escort mission only: rejected for the first spec, because the orbital strike would still have no home in normal play. The resupply mission is deferred to its own spec, written after the guns have been held.; Per-gun hard standoff refusing shots near friendlies: rejected by the owner in favour of soft warning; the refusal would remove the tension and turn collateral into a UI state.; Hard lockout on all guns: rejected for the same reason.; Thermal as the only view, or as a toggle over the normal render: rejected in favour of the split, so the player aims in abstraction and sees the consequence in ground truth.; Fuel as a spendable meter now: rejected, it would stack a second rationing economy on top of strike.js before the VTOL unlock gives spending a meaning.

Evidence:

- src/strike.js already rations and re-orbits an unnamed platform and defaults breakWalls/breakTowers true, so friendly fire and wall destruction are existing tested behaviour, not new mechanics.
- src/fx/story-monitor.js (29 lines) already renders a scissored second camera and has an optic mode framing a target through a long lens; src/fx/story-scope.js (47 lines) is host-fed once a frame.
- https://jelaludo.github.io/SentryTowers_A6/korp/ retrieved 2026-09-13: KORP/GS01 heavy gunship, MÖRK family, LOD1/GameLOD0/LOD2, forward/hover/landed poses, rotary and heavy cannon and engine-tilt clips.
- docs/STATE.md records the inherited formula reaching 5,334 scheduled bodies at wave 75; enemies are Points clouds in src/creatures.js and src/units.js, so horde scale is an unverified assumption recorded in the spec.

## 2026-09-13 — The Quiver's lock is the box: sticky target, no range gates, a timer on the square; the study screen keeps four panels

change · accepted · 2026-09-13-lock-is-the-box

Owner, after playtesting: the Quiver's lock-in is the opposite of satisfying, frustrating, seemingly random, no skill, no agency; possibly the minimum or maximum distances. The rule he wants: no minimum or maximum distance, and if a unit is within the lock square a timer shows and it locks, then fire. Separately, the synthetic-learning study screen should show only four panels: two-seq, NBB-XOR, LIN-TFM, EM-SEG.

The randomness was not the ranges alone. sentry-pilot's target pick chose the nearest body in the cone every frame, and stepLock zeroes the meter whenever the candidate id changes, so two hard cores crossing in the cone reset the timer repeatedly; the range test (min 3 m, max 120 m) reset it again at the edges. Now the box is sticky: the body inside it stays the target while it stays inside, whatever else enters. STORY_QUIVER.missile sets minRange 0, maxRange 400 (the horizon from a 4 m mount on a 753 m planet is about 80 m, so it can never bite) and opens the mrad gates (lockGate and lockBreak 1e4, aimTolerance 180), leaving leaving-the-square as the only reset. lockTime 0.9 s. The scope draws the square at the seeker's true angular size, 10 degree half-angle through the camera's own fov, with the timer filling along its top edge, LOCKING percent above it and TARGET LOCKED below; the TRACK panel reads IN BOX or EMPTY instead of an envelope. The study modal keeps the owner's four panels at twice the size in a 2x2 grid.

Alternatives: Widen the milliradian gate only: rejected, the gate was already passing (manual aim reports zero error); the resets came from target switching and range.; Auto-fire on lock: rejected, firing stays the player's Space; only the finding is automatic.

Evidence:

- code read: stepLock resets on id change and out of range; manual path passes off 0 so the gate never bit
- npm test 93, npm run check, browser --story-world PASS (two hard cores, two rounds), --dist legacy PASS

Supersedes: 2026-09-13-seeker-cone-shell-pace-strike-fills-sinkhole

## 2026-09-13 — The packed release drew the hull and the drone as broken geometry: integer attributes transformed raw

issue · accepted · 2026-09-12-packed-hull-quantisation-fix

Owner screenshot of build 249e1147: the tank rendered as a glowing column, the drone broken. Reproduced on the release build served locally: the hull a flat dark polygon while its triangle count still matched.

gltfpack's default quantisation stores positions as 16-bit integers with the dequantising scale on the node; mergeByMaterial cloned such a geometry and applied a matrix straight into the integer array. Fix in two layers: glbmodels.toFloatAttributes converts every integer or normalised attribute to float before the merge transforms it, and the build packs only the landmark folders (astro, kit, far, story) quantised; units, sentries and legacy props are packed with float attributes (-noq: the hull 480 KB instead of 202, still a fifth of the source). The acceptance's triangle count did not catch this, so the state now carries the hull's bounding span over its scale and the suite asserts it is sane.

Alternatives: Disable quantisation everywhere: rejected, the landmarks through the plain loader are correct and three times smaller quantised.

Evidence:

- release probe before: hull a flat polygon, stats 24082/50; after: hull renders, same stats
- npm test 93; browser --dist legacy and --dist --story-world PASS on the fixed build

## 2026-09-12 — Models ship meshopt-packed and the big landmarks swap between a derived far tier and their near tier by camera distance

change · accepted · 2026-09-12-packed-models-and-far-tiers

Owner: swap game-ready and extreme-low tiers by distance; build the swap with the upstream d3 tiers as stand-ins; try meshopt compression; is a game-ready solar still needed? Found: the upstream d1-d3 files are damage states with the same triangle counts (Terraformer d3 39.6k vs 40.5k), useless as far tiers; gltfpack's simplifier at -si 0.1 -sa gives real far tiers (solar 131k -> 7.1k triangles); plain -si without -sa barely reduces (borders locked).

scripts/build.mjs packs every assets/models/*.glb into dist with gltfpack (-cc -kn -km -ke); sources and their upstream locks untouched; gltfpack is a dev dependency and both workflows run npm ci. All nine GLTFLoader sites set the vendored meshopt decoder. dist models 89 MB -> 14.9 MB, release 29 MB. scripts/far-tiers.mjs (npm run tiers) derives far tiers for structures with a `far` field (solar, hugin, stalheart, assembly) and pins source and derived hashes in docs/far-tier-assets.lock.json, verified by the asset check. story-base loads the far tier first, fetches the near tier only within KIT.lod.metres (150 m) and swaps by camera distance with 1.3x hysteresis; the game and the lab pass the camera. Lab acceptance checks all far from the overview and the solar near tier loading up close. The release acceptance allows the packed MÖRK's triangle count within 1 % (degenerate triangles dropped). The owner then exported the solar complex as LOD1 (3,856 triangles, 7 draws, 340 KB) and LOD2 (2,752, 1 draw, 237 KB): pinned in docs/solar-lod-assets.lock.json as the solar near and far tiers; the derived far tier is kept only for hugin, stalheart and assembly. A dist run exposed that a tank model landing mid roll-out rebuilt the actors and emptied the life containers: in the story they now outlive an actor rebuild and the clip deploy holds its own bay handle. docs/ASSET-COLLABORATION.md is the export contract and budget sheet for the assets manager.

Alternatives: Upstream d3 files as far tiers: rejected, they are damage states of the same detail.; Committing packed copies next to the sources: rejected, path churn and duplicate bytes; packing the release copy keeps the pinned provenance intact.; A game-ready solar export: no longer needed for bytes (333 KB packed); still worth asking for if phones stutter near the solar island (131k triangles at the near tier).

Evidence:

- npm run tiers: solar 9.20 MB/131236 tris -> 286 KB/7136; hugin -> 142 KB/4492; stalheart -> 790 KB/4998; assembly -> 1213 KB/6708
- npm test 92, npm run check (far tiers verified), npm run build 29.3 MB
- browser: --story PASS with the LOD checks; --dist, --dist --story, --dist --story-world run before commit
- --dist, --dist --story, --dist --story-world PASS after the actor-rebuild fix

td-tab's placeTowerObj measures each model's pedestal half-width once (a Box3 at unit scale) and perchOf slides the mount from the cell centre toward the open cell it covers by half the centre distance minus the pedestal radius, clamped at zero: the far edge meets the rock's edge. ## 2026-09-12 — The story planet ships baked: relaxed vertices and carve read back in 0.7 s instead of built in 5 s

change · accepted · 2026-09-12-planet-bake

Owner: the story mode always takes several seconds to load; check how heavy it is and what can be optimized. Measured: buildStoryPlanet 9 s in Node (relax 4.1 s, generateDungeon 4.4 s, of which buildCellGraph is 0.1 s) and about 5 s in Chrome on every load; downloads 27 MB at stage 7, 26 MB of it landmark models (solar complex 9.2 MB, Terraformer 4.1 MB, HUGIN pad 3.2 MB, diorama 2.1 MB, rocket 1.6 MB).

src/core/planet-bake.js encodes and decodes a bake (header with the recipe key, float32 vertices, uint8 tags, uint16 distances, heart, spawn, seeds); scripts/bake-planet.mjs (npm run bake) writes assets/story/planet.bin (1.07 MB). buildStoryPlanet takes an optional bake and, when its key and counts match, reads the vertices and the carve back and rebuilds only the lattice and the cell graph with the kernel's own array types. src/platform/planet-bake.js fetches it once; main.js awaits it before the story routes; the lab uses it too. test/planet-bake.mjs proves the bake reproduces a live build (same carve, heart, spawn, seeds, distances, clearing, mouth, lattice; vertices within 1e-6) and refuses other recipes. Chrome: lab build 5.0 s -> 0.7 s; the game reaches ready in 2.2 s at stage 7. The stage strip in the menu now reads STAGES (it had lost the tank bay).

Alternatives: IndexedDB cache of the live build: rejected, the first load on every device still pays the 5 s.; A Web Worker: rejected, it moves the wait off the main thread but does not shorten it.; Quantised int16 vertices (0.4 MB): rejected for now, float32 keeps the bake provably equal to the live build.

Evidence:

- node test/planet-bake.mjs: 806 ms baked vs 8805 ms live, identical carve
- npm test 92, npm run check (49 pure-layer modules)
- node scripts/browser-test.mjs --story-world and --story PASS; lab status reads built in 0.7 s

## 2026-09-11 — Foundations only under key structures, with a simpler game-ready tile

decision · accepted · 2026-09-11-foundation-islands

After the first story lab, the owner set the build stages (landing, foundations, solar with one Rotor, gate and walls, Stalheart, HUGIN arm, later assembly line and radar), asked for a larger rocket and a slow Isao emergence, and rejected pre-existing foundations at landing. The compute review showed the pinned Fortification tile at 2,200 triangles makes tiles the dominant cost: 563,000 triangles for the 64 x 64 m pad and about 5 million for a fully tiled 240 x 160 m base.

Foundations become islands under each key structure, sized to the structure's plot, printed by Isao as the stage begins. The owner will author a simpler game-ready foundation tile; the current Fortification tile remains an Astro-only asset. Consequences: no continuous plate, so the eight-level terrace design collapses to one locally flat pad per island with under one metre of cut and fill, and the natural lattice remains the ground between islands; the landing sequence removes the pre-tiled pad; the tile budget drops to roughly 400 tiles for the planned structures.

Alternatives: Tile the whole base with the detailed tile: rejected, 5 million triangles.; Keep whole-base tiling with a low-detail field tile: not needed once foundations are islands.; Keep terrace bands across the clearing: unnecessary when each island levels only its own plot.

Evidence:

- assets/models/astro/foundation_flat_d0.glb: 18 meshes, 5 materials, 2,200 triangles
- terrace analysis in scratch: 56 m island corner drop 0.9 m at radius 753 m

## 2026-09-11 — Story opening: larger planet on the same kernel, terraced polar clearing, SH02 arrival lab

decision · accepted · 2026-09-11-story-planet-arrival

The owner wants a better initial story: the rocket lands on a larger planet, only Isao is there, the base grows slowly on 4 m foundations, and defenses emerge later. Planning questions settled: substrate, terrain approach, full base size, player role, and the SH02 landing with a borrowed plume effect. The owner asked for a localhost version before further design sections.

Decisions: a new larger sphere on the pinned grid kernel (16,000 points, 71,314 cells, radius 753 m at 10 m per cell, lane density matching the defense map) rather than a flat world or a separate flat stage; a medium planet with 2 m terraces rather than a kilometre-scale planet with wide lanes; a full base the size of the Astro yard (about 240 x 160 m, eight terrace levels) rather than karyoku's 400 x 304 m; the opening encounter as Isao prints a Rotor the player aims by hand, an armored gate closes the natural entry and weak enemies pile up outside. Built: content/core/domain modules for the recipe, terrace profile, clearing with one open lane mouth and the arrival timeline, all Node-tested; the SH02 pinned by checksum at upstream d39f3a78e; a labs.html#story tab rendering the lattice, tiled pad, rocket, ported MIT plume, dust, scorch and Isao at 2.5 m; a --story browser acceptance with screenshots. Planet generation takes about 4 s in the browser. Deferred: sound, chunked rendering, raymarch governor, kit retaining edges/ramps/piers, the encounter, construction and emergence. The fiction assumption (a new natural world; Stålheart is the machine) is recorded in the spec and awaits owner confirmation.

Alternatives: Give up the sphere for flat terrain: rejected, retires the shell fiction, pole heart, antipode relay and sphere-arc distances.; Separate flat story stage: rejected, story and defense would run on two worlds.; Large 2.6-4.8 km planet with 35-64 m cells: rejected for now, lane width and enemy scale no longer match the defense game and lane mouths need walls to narrow them.; Karyoku-size base: rejected on the medium planet, it stacks 22 terrace levels and 43 m of drop.

## 2026-09-10 — Growth ratchet on the TD controller, frozen top-level module list, and three project skills for structural work

decision · accepted · 2026-09-10-architecture-ratchet-and-project-skills

The owner raised maintainability and separation of concerns as projects grow and offered a generic seven-skill coding micro-skills pack for consideration. Assessment: four of the pack's skills (bug-fix, test-and-validate, implement-feature, refactor) duplicate already-installed process skills and would create competing instructions; the real structural risk is src/td-tab.js at 17,630 lines (34% of 51,919 source lines) with only src/core, src/domain and src/content under mechanical dependency guards.

npm run architecture now reads docs/architecture-budget.json: lineBudgets pins src/td-tab.js at 17,630 lines and fails on growth, printing a lower-the-budget hint when the file shrinks; topLevelModules freezes the 100 existing src/*.js files so a new top-level module fails and new code must go into a layer directory. Three project-local skills were adopted under .claude/skills/: add-module, remove-dead-code and architecture-review, each adapted to the Stalheart layers and the ratchet. Each skill was baseline-tested with a read-only planning scenario before writing: the add-module baseline skipped the owner search and planned a hookup that grows the controller; the architecture-review baseline missed the 92-line controller growth in ae88ea2 and the existing src/domain/manual-weapon.js beside the new pilot aiming; the remove-dead-code baseline called two actively imported compatibility facades safe to delete without classifying them. Verification runs with the skills present addressed those gaps. Documented in docs/ARCHITECTURE.md and AGENTS.md.

Alternatives: Install all seven pack skills verbatim: rejected, overlapping and sometimes contradicting installed TDD/debugging/verification skills; the files also lack skill frontmatter and would not register.; Rely on prose rules alone without the ratchet: rejected, two thirds of the source sits outside the enforced layers so advice has nothing to check against.; Auto-lower the budget on every run: deferred; a hint keeps the budget change an explicit reviewed edit.

Evidence:

- node test/architecture.mjs: new assertions for line budgets and top-level placement pass
- node scripts/architecture.mjs with a probe line appended to src/td-tab.js and a stray src/zz-probe.js: both failures reported, exit 1; restored tree passes
- npm run check passes with the budget file in place
- git show --stat ae88ea2 -- src/td-tab.js: 120 insertions, 28 deletions
- grep manual-weapon: imported by src/sniper-tab.js only; src/sentry-pilot.js imports THREE and content/sentries.js

## 2026-09-08 — Bring Sniper into the numbered Sentry roster and shared weapon presentation

change · resolved · 2026-09-08-sniper-shared-sentries

Owner wants Sniper to become a game within the game, using the same numbered weapons and effects before tuning its environment. Its independent five-weapon catalog, fixed Lancer model and old Javelin guidance/presentation had drifted from the game and Sentry lab.

Sniper now offers all eight numbered Sentries via the shared selector and keys 1–8, loads each pinned model/tier, uses shared Sentry sound cues and FX working packages, and derives kind, damage, cadence, reach, splash and pierce from shared game inputs. Quiver and Heptapod use the existing DART pool, launch snapshots, flight profiles, arrival and engagement/lock rules; the scope supplies manual aim in converted degrees. Heptapod retains shared cassette sizes/salvo/reload timing for stationary manual operation; the board walker still owns its travel/home loop. Relay is a non-damaging slow field, Lancer pierces and lobbers resolve nearby bodies through shared splash radii. Tracers, beams, lightning, muzzle and impact effects use shared builders. Sniper imports and reviews the same canonical per-Sentry FX package; explicit draft preview retains weapon selection and normal launches remain isolated. Default target range/zero are 20 m and magnification 2x to match shared engagement metres; longer environment experiments remain possible without extending weapon reach. Scope wind, gravity, drag, sway, breath, target exercise and manual hold remain stage-specific rules, not a claim of full board-combat parity. The old runtime weapon catalog was retired; historical numerical fixtures moved to test-only data and ballistics moved into a pure domain owner behind a facade. Reset clears flights, locks and falling plates; async model loads are guarded; disposal releases frame/listener/audio/model/pool/sky/post-processing ownership. No saved project draft was promoted and no sniper replacement, factory or automation progression was introduced.

Alternatives: Keeping Javelin as a separate weapon was rejected because the accepted DART presentation and numbered roster supersede it; legacy links normalize to Quiver.; Scaling missile reach to the old 700 m scene would undermine the shared metre contract; the initial exercise moved to 20 m instead.; An acceptance probe initially returned a GUI controller that CDP could not serialize; commands now return no controller references.; A blind-zone test exposed a null-candidate comparison in manual launch eligibility; missing candidate and target now explicitly refuse fire before reading angular error.

## 2026-09-08 — Share Quiver and Heptapod engagement rules and promote range/lock settings

change · resolved · 2026-09-08-shared-missile-engagement

Owner requested architecture/parity after repeated lab/game drift. DART presentation was shared, but gameplay ignored authored engagement bands and Quiver accumulated a lock against its tower cell rather than the acquired enemy. Lock knobs were not represented in reviewed defaults.

The pure missile-targeting owner now handles target retention, inclusive ground-distance bands, immediate lock reset after target loss/switch and firing eligibility. Lock/guidance and the Heptapod loop moved behind top-level compatibility facades into domain modules. Gameplay supplies sphere arcs and actual post-slew aim; lab supplies flat ground metres. A fixed 10 authoring metres per cell is independent of fitted asset scale. The shipped 30 m maximum therefore becomes three base cells, replacing old game Quiver 3.5/Heptapod 3.4 ranges; existing 8% upgrade range growth remains and minimum range stays fixed. Version 5 adds per-family lock gate/time/break and aim tolerance to reviewed presets, with strict v2/v3/v4 migration including saved metadata baselines and generated content. Heptapod retains hard-target policy, cassette, standoff, leash and return/reload, and cannot walk into a narrow blind zone. Game damage/economy/progression and cadence are preserved; lab fixtures remain general test targets and drive speed/recoil/cadence controls are explicitly preview-only. This is missile engagement/presentation parity, not full combat-engine extraction. User project drafts remain v4 on disk, readable as v5 with their Quiver 9–40 m and Heptapod 9–36 m values intact; neither was promoted. One local source server was restarted at port 8155 for the updated schema.

Alternatives: Deriving engagement reach from fitted model size was rejected because mesh replacement or tier appearance would change combat range.; Keeping separate lab and game locks was rejected because progress could transfer between enemies and authored changes remained misleading.; A strict floating-point comparison initially excluded sphere endpoints a few ulps beyond the band; a one-nanometre conversion tolerance preserves inclusive boundaries without a gameplay-sized margin.

Owner reported slowdown near wave 42 and suspected numerous ray towers. Existing optional FPS overlay had aggregate render counts, but no grouped workload or retained wave samples; clamped simulation dt could hide frame rates below 10 FPS.

Enable the compact FPS overlay by default unless the player has disabled it; backtick and the existing tuning switch still toggle it. FPS uses unclamped real frame intervals. Expand workload for enemy count, measured enemy/tower/game-frame CPU times, total GPU time when available and a descending batch inventory by Sentry family, tank, Terraformer, enemies, portals, beams/lightning, projectiles, debris, shield and remaining world. Counts are estimates before culling, not per-item GPU timings, and omit shader/overdraw pricing. Inventory is cached for two seconds. Samples are recorded every five seconds in bounded local diagnostics; Copy performance shares a short human-readable snapshot with selected-text fallback. GPU queries are capped at eight, reject disjoint measurements and release pending queries on toggle/disposal. Overlay follows the HUD lower edge.

Alternatives: Do not attribute slowdown to Relay or Lancer without runtime measurements.; Per-draw GPU queries would add cost and can misrepresent tiled rendering; retain total GPU timing and explicitly labeled geometry estimates.

## 2026-09-08 — Continuous Relay shield transfer and one shield clock

change · resolved · 2026-09-08-continuous-relay-shield

Owner saw repeated SHIELD DOWN / 2s messages, erratic shield animation and refusal to deploy near Relay during a crowded wave. The Relay tap was inside its enemy-targeted attack cadence, crediting only one frame of energy per attack. Shield drain used frame dt but cooldown used the vehicle-motion clock, which can stop independently.

A pure frame transaction combines continuous Relay/heart input and shield drain. Tiny sub-frame inputs that never sustain a bubble do not report repeated drops. The game gathers nearby Relays every frame independently of enemy targeting, attack cooldown and build downtime; transfer feedback stays at tower cadence. Shield recharge/drain, cooldown, outage and animation now use the gameplay frame clock. Tapped Relays stop attacks and stay offline five seconds after leaving. Restart clears taps and resets rack/station resources. Rates, cap, two-second rack seam and four-second ram combo remain unchanged.

Alternatives: Debouncing SHIELD DOWN would hide the repeated energy/drop bug without repairing recharge.; Increasing charge duration or lowering cooldown would change balance instead of correcting the timing owners.

## 2026-09-07 — Add per-Sentry missile acquisition and minimum engagement distances

change · resolved · 2026-09-07-missile-engagement-ranges

Owner requested minimum engagement and maximum acquisition controls for Quiver and Heptapod after confirming the existing range knobs only governed test-target spawning and escape.

Missiles / lock now exposes min engage (m) and max acquire (m), defaulting to 3 and 30 meters for each family. A pure targeting module filters targets by inclusive flat-ground distance from each individual launcher before retaining or choosing the nearest eligible target. Crossing a bound immediately drops acquisition and lock; an articulated launcher holds its direction when no eligible target remains. Existing rockets continue. Quiver and Heptapod edits are independent, have hover help and appear in the range readout. UI edits cannot invert the band; strict FX v4 validation rejects invalid, missing or out-of-bounds distances. Version 2/3 imports preserve existing edits and gain the new defaults. This remains a Sentry-lab experiment; game rules are unchanged. The release check also exposed a queued-close race in the shared picker; a stale close event can no longer clear a newly reopened picker.

Alternatives: Filter before selecting so an ignored close target cannot starve farther eligible targets.; Measure horizontal ground distance from each launcher in the flat lab rather than from battery center or using height-dependent slant distance. Sphere gameplay adoption will require arc distances.; Keep separate saved per-family values rather than a global pair that changes when switching Sentries.

Evidence:

- npm test: 67 test programs passed. New targeting coverage includes both exact boundaries, dead targets, too-close targets, retained targets crossing either limit and different launcher positions.
- Preset tests cover version 2/3 migration, retained missile edits, range round trips, missing fields, non-finite/out-of-bounds values and inverted bands.
- npm run check and npm run build passed; build c51c8149.
- npm run test:browser: full source acceptance passed. New live GUI checks verify distant acquisition, immediate lock clearing, no new launches outside either bound, continuing in-flight rockets, bound clamping and independent family settings.
- node scripts/browser-test.mjs --dist --authoring: nested release authoring/browser checks passed, including imported range values, saved draft preview and visible controls.
- docs/MISSILES.md documents the controls, 3–30 m defaults, inclusive bounds and separation from Range / farthest and Range / through at.
- The initial release run passed the new range checks but exposed an intermittent existing picker reopen failure. A deterministic close/reopen-in-one-task regression now passes with the queued-close guard; source authoring was rerun after the fix.

Click ray picking opens a numbered eight-way modal; one controller selection rebuilds every battery member. Orbit drags are ignored and mouse, number keys and Escape are supported. Quiver tracks with 55.8-degree upward pitch and launches 0.32 m DARTs using Swift/1.35 s. Heptapod preserves fixed vertical sockets and launches 0.80 m DARTs using Hook/2.7 s. The pinned core sampler, pure flat-range adapter and shared-resource pool preserve nose-up falling, ignition, target arrival and bounded shot resources. FX v3 exports carry validated missile settings; v2 imports explicitly retain previous edits and add defaults. Draft preview opens the Sentry lab. Campaign and Impact projectile behavior remain unchanged pending sphere/collision integration.

Alternatives: Use one DART mesh at two sizes rather than adding a heavier mesh for Heptapod; 188 triangles and four material batches including exhaust per active rocket.; Use the upstream sampler instead of playing a baked clip simultaneously; correct its fixed endpoint for lab targets.; Preserve Heptapod as a fixed vertical launcher rather than inventing yaw/pitch articulation.; Keep gameplay adoption separate from this requested Workshop evaluation, with an explicit Sentry-lab draft preview.

## 2026-09-07 — Build architecture and lab export contracts before UX and playability

decision · accepted · 2026-09-07-architecture-first-fx-contract

The owner explicitly reordered development: rebuild a strong technical foundation, refine visual and sound effects in labs with clean exports, then work on UX and playability. The inherited code had shared visual builders but mixed source-copy exports, local persistence and independent preset formats. Even the supposedly pure knob helpers imported browser storage unnecessarily.

The active roadmap and agent instructions now follow architecture → labs/export → UX → playability. Foundation v1 gives core helpers, six pure rule modules and content contracts explicit owners with compatibility re-exports. Dependency checks enforce the migrated pure layers, reject their cycles and browser dependencies, and prevent game/lab controller coupling. A strict schema-1 FX package carries all current weapon effects and 29 cue tunings; gameplay fields and sample references remain fixed. The impact lab and a new sound lab share validated import/export, isolated draft storage and explicit game preview. Runtime content is cloned and frozen once before consumers load. The promotion CLI atomically generates a reviewable shipped data module; it never deploys. The sound lab uses the real audio engine with injected definitions, separate audition preferences and disposal. Simulator records carry the selected content ID. The remaining large TD closure, shared stage/lifetime toolkit and beam/material/cinematic schema extensions are explicitly next work, not described as complete.

Alternatives: Starting with HUD changes or difficulty tuning conflicts with the owner’s requested sequence.; Moving files alone does not establish ownership. The new boundaries have executable guards, and the authoring workflow is exercised through the actual game.; Automatic application of local lab edits would make normal gameplay depend on hidden browser state. Draft preview is explicit; normal startup uses shipped content.; A broad framework/renderer rewrite would discard the current parity baseline without fixing the immediate content handoff. This phase retains native ESM and the pinned renderer/kernel.; The first browser file-import check caught an inherited navigation race: it could observe the previous page’s ready flag and query a stale DOM. The runner now clears that flag and verifies the destination URL plus readiness before interacting.

Evidence:

- docs/ARCHITECTURE.md: implemented ownership map, package contract, actual workflow, lifecycle limits and ordered remaining rebuild.
- npm test: 63 test programs passed, including negative architecture guards, preset validation/isolation and real CLI promotion in an isolated checkout.
- npm run check: 13 pure-layer modules, source identity, unchanged kernel hashes, log and 37 pinned Sentry assets passed.
- node scripts/browser-test.mjs --authoring: source file import, cross-lab draft transfer, audio audition, actual game preview and normal-game isolation passed.
- node scripts/browser-test.mjs --dist: full nested-path release matrix passed, including existing game/mission/hack/roster checks, all three A6 hulls, authoring round trip and PWA control.
- artifacts/browser-dist/lab-audio.log: decoded 29/29 cues; analyser peak 0.10332 over 50 frames. This verifies signal in Chrome, not perceived quality or real-device compatibility.
- artifacts/browser-dist/browser-fx.json and preset-preview evidence: visual impact size 0.93 and edited tower cue gain 0.42 reached actual runtime content; normal launch retained the shipped baseline.
- npm run build: b9981afe, 397 files, 63,943,375 bytes. No artistic candidate was promoted in the working repo; src/content/shipped.js still selects the baseline.
- test/promotion.mjs: export/promote/new-runtime round trip passed; repeated promotion is deterministic and invalid promotion leaves the shipped file unchanged.

## 2026-09-07 — Local lab review/apply/undo through one promotion writer

change · accepted · 2026-09-07-local-authoring-promotion

Owner requested architecture for Lab > test > fine-tune > decide > apply, without transferring a 9 KB complete preset for a few Quiver edits.

Added pure per-subject diffs and three-way merging, a localhost authoring API, and a shared CLI/server promotion writer. Source labs save canonical working copies into the project, review only selected Sentry/cue edits, apply exact reviewed snapshots with source-revision checks, run checks/build, and support inverse review/undo with persisted recovery. Failed validation restores prior source if no external edit intervened. Other subjects and independent newer changes survive. Copy now emits a small human-readable changed-values summary; full JSON is under Backup / transfer. API guards host/origin/token/body/subject and is disabled for release/read-only serving. Browser storage is a fallback; project saves work even if its quota fails. The source server on port8155 was restarted with authoring enabled. Actual user defaults were not promoted. Coverage remains existing FX/audio/missile schema; drive/lock and scene controls are explicitly outside promotion pending consumer/schema work.

Alternatives: Per-tab full-package overwrite would lose other subjects and stale-tab changes; selected-field three-way merging avoids that.; A second compact preset import format was not introduced; summaries are for human review, canonical JSON remains the stored/backup artifact.; The existing CLI idempotence test exposed the need to preserve successful no-op promotions after extracting the shared writer; that contract is retained.

## Playfeel

What feels satisfying to play and what needs work, from playtests of the
localhost build. Owner's observations, dated; this page is rendered into
`DEVLOG.md` as its own section. Add a line, keep the date.

## Satisfying (2026-09-13)

- Ramming bonuses.
- Timing a shield and leaving some low-level enemies alive to keep the ramming bonus going.
- Orbital strikes.
- Small animations: the gate opening, the revolving barrels of the first sentry.
- The minigun (Rotor) under manual control, with the spool that follows the barrels.

## Future features (2026-09-13)

## Needs work (2026-09-13)

- The Quiver: more work overall; a smaller missile for one (the Talon is now a third shorter).
- The breach: the camera should dive fast to the sinkhole as the eruption starts, not stay in orbit (done, see the story doc).
- The tank's turret traverse: it swung almost 180 degrees; it should be 90 (done).
- The tank's shell is much too slow (done: faster shell).
- The Quiver's lock was "the opposite of satisfying; frustrating, seemingly random, no skill, no agency". Cause: the target switched to whichever body was momentarily nearest and every switch reset the meter, and the range test reset it again. Now: the lock square is the rule, the body in it stays the target, no range gates (done).
